     2.4's `thr` and `epoch` comments and 2.8's steps 13 and 14, heartbeat and settle now say this. Affects V07 (the `jti` check it already makes), V12 and V15.
5. **Only the reserve and the agent-time read judge runs idle.** INV-C3 said idle runs are settled "by the next ledger call for that account". `reportRun`, `recordCodiumRequest`, `settleRun`, `releaseRun` and `liveRun` touch only their own run and judge nothing idle, so the gateway hook adds one small script to each Codium request and never settles the account's other runs, and INV-C3's own case holds: a run with no heartbeats whose last Codium request was at +20 min is idle-settled at +31 min, by the next reserve or read, at 20 minutes. The idle rule is strict: exactly 600 s after the last activity a run is still live; 1 ms later it is idle (both measured from a heartbeat and from a request). INV-C3's last clause now names the two calls.
6. **A run of another epoch.** A sweep cannot give back into an epoch it was not given a key for, and neither can 2.6 step 2's generation-change settle: the reserve's script holds only its allowance's `used` key, and the epoch a replaced record names is known only inside the script. `reserveRun`, which needs the slot, removes an idle run of another epoch than its allowance's, and settles a replaced run of another epoch (the same check run minting again after a rebind, across a period change), with no give-back: that run's whole reservation stays charged in its own epoch, which no limit reads once the account has moved to a new one (INV-C5), though that epoch's agent-time read stays high by up to the unused part (a Max run whose last heartbeat was at +5 min, replaced at +6 min across a renewal, reads 2700 s there, not the formula's 300). 2.6 step 2 and INV-C3 had said every settle path, a generation change included, charges the formula, and this item had named only the idle case (found in review); 2.6 and INV-C3 now name both, and so does the settle with another epoch's token (deviation 4). Refusing such a re-mint until the record goes idle would not help: the rebind has already killed the record's token, so it would be swept 10 minutes later, still without its epoch's key. `cloudAgentSecondsUsed` frees no slot, and its epoch is the caller's, which may be stale around a renewal (Codium's snapshot) or `none`, so it settles only idle runs of the epoch it is asked about and leaves others alone; removing them there would overcharge the current epoch by their unused reservations. A stale `reserveRun` epoch cannot sweep a newer epoch's idle run: the entitlement cache is at most 60 s old (V04 deviation 2), and a run is idle only after 600 s. A generation change has no such window, so a stale epoch can replace a newer epoch's run, but only when a period change, a rebind and a re-mint of the same check run reaching an instance whose cached allowance predates the change all fall within that cache's 60 s; the replaced run is then under 60 s old, and its unused reservation (at most the plan's longest run) stays charged to the current epoch. That is accepted, on the conservative side. Affects A18.
7. **A record the re-mint cannot renew.** 2.6 covered a re-mint with the same and an older generation. A re-mint from the same check run with a lower generation than the live record's (a mint that read the binding before a rebind, landing after the newer mint) leaves the record and its `jti` alone and answers `at-once`; overwriting it would kill the newer token. So does a same-generation re-mint at or after the record's deadline (found in review: the renewal rotated the `jti` and consumed the ticket whatever the time). The token V12 would mint for that deadline is dead at the gateway at once, so V12 had been told to refuse it, while the rotation had already killed the earlier token, whose `exp` (the deadline plus 15 minutes) still lets it heartbeat and settle; that token's settle, which records the run's outcome and verdict (2.16), then answered `{settled: false}`, and INV-S9 had tied the earlier token's end to a new token that never came. A same-generation re-mint now renews only while `now < deadline`; otherwise it answers `at-once` and writes nothing, consuming no ticket. `at-once`, not a new refusal: the check run's own record holds the slot until it is settled, the CLI's at-once retries (1.4) then find it gone (settled by the earlier token, or swept idle) and reserve afresh exactly as a mint after a settle does, and no new code or `MINT_STATUS` is needed. Every `ok` answer's deadline is now after `now`, so V12 needs no `dl ≤ now` check. INV-S9 and 2.6 step 2 say so.
8. **Types and patterns.** `LiveRun.src` and `ReserveInput.source` are `LedgerSource` (`Extract<RunSource, "github-comment" | "github-dispatch" | "github-schedule">`), and `pur` and `purpose` are `LedgerPurpose` (`"task" | "review" | "scheduled"`), matching `RunTokenClaims`; 2.6 had `RunSource` and `string`. `METER_EPOCH_PATTERN` (`/^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_:.-]{0,127}$/`) is exported for V07's `epoch` claim; it accepts V04's `p<ms>` and Codium's `<subscription>:<periodStart>` and `none` (`origin/codium` `codium-snapshot.ts`).
9. **Expiries are durations from the caller's clock.** 2.7 gave `used` an expiry of `periodEnd` plus 40 days and refreshed `live` to the latest deadline plus a day. Both are set with `PEXPIRE` as a duration from the `now` the caller passes, never with `PEXPIREAT`, so the API host's clock and the store's need not agree, and a test clock in the past cannot delete keys (Redis deletes a key at once when given a past absolute time; with the test's fixed clock, five days back, the absolute form fails 25 of the 33 tests). `used` expires `max(periodEnd − now, 0) + 40 days` from now, set at each fresh reserve, so a stale or past `periodEnd` can never delete an epoch's agent time. `live` is extended at each fresh reserve to expire no sooner than that reserve's `used` key and its deadline plus a day, and never shortened (item 3): renewals, heartbeats, requests, settles, sweeps and releases leave it, and the hash goes with its last run. `sched` is set to 2 days from each scheduled mint.
10. **A request before the run's start.** `recordCodiumRequest` ignores an `at` earlier than the run's `startedAt`: the gateway and the mint run on different instances whose clocks differ, and such a request cannot be the run's. Without that, a few milliseconds of skew would store a request before the start, which the strict decoder (item 2) then refuses on every read.
11. **The gateway hook.** `cloudAgentsGatewayHook.afterSettle` is a method typed `Promise<void>`, as Codium's `GatewayHook.afterSettle` declares; 2.6's arrow returned the union of `recordCodiumRequest(...).catch(...)` and `Promise.resolve()` and needed a non-null assertion. A miss is logged as `console.warn("Vector cloud-agents ledger miss", {runId})`, with the run id only, and without it when the id does not match `RUN_ID_PATTERN`. The test types it against a local copy of Codium's `GatewayHook`, `ModelPlanCaller` and `AfterSettleContext` at `origin/codium` `99fcf1c0`, both as a hook and as a plain function value (so a narrower parameter fails), so drift on Codium's side shows only at A59.
12. **Docs.** 2.18's live-run row now names every `LiveRun` field (start, deadline, last heartbeat, last Codium request, meter epoch, repository id, thread, source, purpose, whether it counted a scheduled run, binding generation, token id), and its agent-time row says what `used` holds, for V11's INV-P3 test. Section 5's A18 row says what the two-argument call settles and answers; its A59 row says the ledger imports the types `CloudAgentAllowance` and `RunSource` from `cloud-agents-entitlement.ts`, so A59 must keep exporting those names, and that the ledger test never imports `createCloudAgentEntitlement`.
13. **The ticket is single-use at the reserve.** 2.5 deleted the ticket inside the reserve EVAL "only when the reservation succeeds", while 2.8 step 8 only peeks it, and the App check, the entitlement, the rate limits and `consumeGithubOidc` run between that peek and step 13. The fresh path deleted the key without checking that it was still there (found in review), so two mints from different check runs carrying one ticket (two minting jobs, or a matrix, in the dispatched workflow) could both pass the peek and both reserve: one ticket, two bot-actor runs (INV-S2), bounded only by `atOnce`. A fresh reservation given `consumeKey` now checks first that the key exists, after the generation-change settle and before the slots (a retry could find a slot free, never the ticket), and otherwise answers `{ok: false, refusal: "ticket"}`, a new `ReserveResult` member beside the `limit` refusals, writing nothing beyond the reserve's own sweep. Renewals stay unconditional, so parallel mints of the check run that consumed the ticket still renew. V12 maps the refusal to 403 `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED`, as step 8 answers a missing ticket, and passes `consumeKey` only for the ticket run step 8 accepted. 2.5's ticket bullets, 2.6's signature and its new step 3 (the old steps 3 to 6 are now 4 to 7), 2.8 step 13 and INV-S2's test list say so. Affects V12 and V13.
14. **One count per schedule event (found in the second review).** 2.6 applied the daily cap (step 5) and the increment (step 7) to every fresh `github-schedule` reserve, including the one a check run makes when it replaces its own live record after a generation change (step 2) or once the reserve's own sweep settled that record idle. The rid is the check run, and one check run is one GitHub schedule event, which its first reserve already counted (INV-C4). So the fifth Max schedule of a day, minting again after a rebind, was refused `scheduled`, and below the cap one event took two of the day's runs, or one on each of two days when the re-mint came after midnight (both confirmed against the module before the fix). The reserve now notes, before its sweep, whether `rid` holds a live `github-schedule` record; if it does, the fresh reservation skips the cap and the increment. A release must then uncount only a reservation that counted, or releasing the replacement (its mint failed) would uncount the event that ran. So a counting reserve marks its record `counted: true` (`LiveRun.counted?: true`, accepted only on a `github-schedule` run by both decoders, item 2), and `releaseRun` decrements its start day's count only for such a record. A replacement does not take the count over: the first record's day may be another day, whose key the release script was not given, and the event did run. Accepted, on the conservative side: a check run that mints again once its live record is gone (settled, swept by an earlier call, or a replacement released) is counted again, because nothing remembers a check run after its record; the workflow mints once per job (2.13), so only an edited workflow or a repeated mint step gets there, and it counts against the account, never for it. INV-C4 (a sentence and a test), 2.6 (`LiveRun`, the stored-data rule, steps 1, 5 and 7, `releaseRun`), 2.7's `sched` row, 2.18's live-run row and the V06 test list say so. Affects V07 (a live record may carry `counted`, so its check reads the record through this module's rules or reads only `jti`) and V11 (the stored-data list); V12 changes nothing, since `releaseRun`'s argument is unchanged.

Noted for reviewers, not deviations: the plan's step that would have moved V05's test harness into a shared module was not taken. The lane runs every `cloud-agents-*.test.ts` in one Bun process, where a module's top-level hooks and server would register only for the first importer, and V06's file list does not include V05's test; the ledger test keeps its own copy of the harness (the `Bun.serve` fake of the REST store, the gate, the saved and restored environment, cleanup of every key touched). It starts no subprocess, never flushes, and never names V04's limits table.

## 2026-10-31 V07 (Run tokens and the verifier seam)

Section 2.4, the V07 test list and section 5's A16 row now say what is below (the section 2 preamble's "add it there first"). This entry says why each departs from what they said before.

1. **The mint's signature.** 2.4 had `mintRunToken(claims)`. It takes `Omit<RunTokenClaims, "v" | "exp">`, sets `v = 1` and computes `exp = dl + SETTLE_GRACE_SECONDS × 1000` itself, so no caller can sign another grace, and it checks every claim with the reader's own rules before it signs, an unknown key included (400 `INVALID_REQUEST`), so nothing is signed that `readRunToken` would refuse. It answers `{token, deadline, expiresAt}` with `deadline = dl` and `expiresAt = exp`. Affects V12, which signs the reserve's answer and releases a fresh reservation when the mint answers 400.
2. **A stricter claim schema, with no change of intent.** 2.4 listed the claims and "`rid` against `RUN_ID_PATTERN`". The reader also requires: `sub` a lowercase UUID in Codium's registry form (version 1 to 8, variant 8 to b), because INV-K5 asks that the caller pass the registry exactly and a token the registry would refuse should fail at the mint, not at the gateway (Supabase ids are version 4, so no real account is lost); `rid` in the `gh-<repositoryId>-<runId>-<runAttempt>-<checkRunId>` grammar of `GithubAgents.runId`, with its repository equal to `repo`, so the binding the check reads and the run the ledger holds always name one repository and the id keeps INV-K5's uniqueness; `repo` a decimal safe integer with no leading zero (the binding's and the ledger's rule); `gen` an integer of at least 1; `pur` `scheduled` exactly when `src` is `github-schedule` (the ledger's rule); `epoch` matching `METER_EPOCH_PATTERN`; `dl`, `exp` and `iat` positive safe integers with `iat < dl` and `exp = dl + 900 000` exactly; `jti` 22 base64url characters; `cs` finite in (0, 1]; and a token with exactly one `.` and base64url characters only. Only Vector signs these tokens, and V12 signs only values that pass these rules, so no valid token is lost. Affects V12 (it must mint with `binding.account` and `binding.generation`, the reserve's `deadline`, `epoch`, `thread`, `source` and `purpose`, and `iat = now`).
3. **The license secret must have 32 characters too** (found in review). 2.4 said "otherwise `HMAC-SHA256(VECTOR_LICENSE_SECRET, …)`" with no length for the license secret, so a module that derived a key from a one-character license secret would have met it. `cli-token.ts` refuses a license secret under 32 characters, and the run key follows the same rule: with no dedicated secret of at least 32 characters and no license secret of at least 32, mint, read and verify answer 503 `RUN_TOKENS_UNAVAILABLE` and reach no store or seam. The test covers a dedicated secret of 31 (ignored) and exactly 32 (used directly), a license secret of 31 (503) and 32 (used), and pins the derivation itself: the derived key, set as the dedicated secret, keeps a license-only token valid. Affects the owner doc (V29): rotating either secret, or setting the dedicated one later, ends every live run token at once, and those runs end as drafts.
4. **The revocation mark's key is built here.** 2.4 said the EVAL's keys come from the key builders the binding and ledger modules export, "so no key layout is written twice". The revocation mark `vector:cli-revoked:<account>` belongs to `revocation.ts`, which keeps its builder private and which Codium's lane edits around it; exporting it there would add a merge conflict for A59, and the file is not ours (0.2). So the module writes the layout once more, says why beside it, and the test pins the two together: it revokes through the real `revokeAccountTokens`, checks that `requireUnrevokedAccount` agrees, and checks that the key the revocation wrote is the one the check declared. A mutation that changes the module's layout fails that test. The binding and ledger keys still come only from `bindingKey`, `generationKey` and `liveKey`.
5. **The check also reads the generation counter, and reads the live record for its `jti` only.** As V05's note asked, the EVAL declares `generationKey(repo)` beside the binding, and it requires the counter to equal the binding's `generation` field, which holds while a binding exists (2.7); a mismatch, a missing counter, or a binding account or generation that the binding module's reader would refuse is corrupt data (503). A suspension needs no check of its own, because a binding's first suspension moves its generation (V05 deviation 6). The live record is read for its `jti` alone, as V06 deviation 14 asked: its other fields, `counted` included, are the ledger's to judge, and a record that is not a JSON object with a string `jti` is corrupt. The revocation mark counts when the key exists, whatever its value, which refuses more than `requireUnrevokedAccount`'s truthiness test only for a mark with an empty value, which `revokeAccountTokens` never writes. Every key of another type than the one its module writes is corrupt. The script writes nothing (the test's fake sends it to the store as `EVAL_RO`, which refuses any write; see the note below).
6. **A seam failure is a 503, and the seam runs beside the EVAL.** 2.4 said "an error fails closed". A rejection, a throw, and an answer that is neither `undefined` nor a safe integer of at least 0 (`NaN`, a fraction, a negative or unsafe number, a string, `null`) answer 503 `PERSISTENT_STORE_UNAVAILABLE`, one code from 2.9.1, so Codium's own error codes are never serialized in our responses; the comparison stays `revokedBefore > iat`, in milliseconds (V04 deviation 5), as Codium's `runTokenRevoked` refuses `issuedAt < before`. The EVAL and the seam call start together, so a verification costs one round trip; when both refuse or fail, the EVAL's answer is the one reported (a store failure is 503 even when the seam refuses; a store refusal is 401 even when the seam fails), so the answer never depends on which finished first. The test proves the parallel start with a store and a seam that each wait for the other. Any rejection of the EVAL, whatever it carries, answers the module's own 503, and so does an answer the module does not know. The store call starts inside a promise chain, as the seam's does, so an injected store that throws instead of rejecting is the same 503 (found in review: such a store's raw error escaped `requireLive`, which V12's `safe()` would have turned into `CLOUD_AGENTS_UNAVAILABLE`). Because every failure is one response, each logs one server line naming its cause (`store`, `corrupt`, `unknown answer`, `seam rejected` or `seam value`) and the run id (found in review: corrupt stored data, which never mends by itself, could not be told from an outage). The line never carries the account (INV-P4) or the failure's own text, which can hold an upstream request's headers; a refusal logs nothing, and the responses are unchanged (INV-P1).
7. **Injectable dependencies, and `requireRunToken`'s shape.** 2.4 had `verifyRunToken(token, deps?)` and `requireRunToken(request, { allowAfterDeadline })`. Both take `deps: RunTokenDeps = { now?, store?, revokedBefore? }` (exported), for V12's `createCloudAgentsApi` deps (2.8) and the tests; the registry still calls `cloudAgentsRunVerifier.verify(token)` with the defaults. `requireRunToken` takes `Pick<ApiRequest, "headers">`, requires the options object, accepts the token past its deadline only when `allowAfterDeadline` is exactly `true`, reads only an `authorization` header that is exactly `Bearer vrt_<base64url>.<base64url>`, and returns the claims. Affects V12 (heartbeat and settle pass `true`) and V27 (artifact-key passes `false`).
8. **Error codes in detail, one message each** (the message rule found in review). 2.4 listed two 401s. `exp` passed at read answers 401 `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_EXPIRED`, not `RUN_TOKEN_INVALID`, since the token is well-formed and was valid; no usable key answers 503 `RUN_TOKENS_UNAVAILABLE` at mint and at read; a store or seam failure, an unknown answer or corrupt data answers 503 `PERSISTENT_STORE_UNAVAILABLE`; the mint answers 400 `INVALID_REQUEST`. Every refusal of a well-signed token (revoked, unbound, a moved generation, another account at the same generation, settled, replaced, before `revokedBefore`, the deadline, `exp`) is one `ApiError` with one code and one message, and every format, length, signature or schema failure and a missing header another, so V12's heartbeat and settle responses, which serialize them, never say why a run stopped or reveal the binder's billing state once A59 backs `revokedBefore` with Codium's holds (INV-P1). The test compares the status, code and message of each cause. A token `readRunToken` refuses, or one past its deadline at the gateway, costs no store call and no seam call. A clock that is not a number refuses: the checks are written `now < exp` and `now < dl`.
9. **Types.** `RunTokenClaims.src` and `pur` are typed `LedgerSource` and `LedgerPurpose` from the ledger, which equal 2.4's unions (V06 deviation 8); the test asserts `RunTokenClaims` and `RunCaller` equal 2.4's shapes exactly, that `requireRunToken` returns `RunTokenClaims`, that `RunCaller` is assignable to a local copy of Codium's `ModelPlanCaller`, that `cloudAgentsRunVerifier.verify` is a `RunVerifier` and its prefix a `RunPrefix`, and, with `@ts-expect-error`, that a mint with an `email` claim does not compile. Codium's types are copied from `origin/codium` at `99fcf1c0`, which match `cb8e7dfb` (the current `origin/codium` and `origin/codium-next`), so drift on Codium's side shows only at A59.

Noted for reviewers, not deviations: the harness is V05's and V06's, copied inline (the one-process lane step; V06's note), with one route more, the path-style `POST /set/<key>/<value>?ex=` that `revokeAccountTokens` sends, and one change: the fake sends the run-token check (the only script that declares a revocation mark) to the store as `EVAL_RO`, so a write on any path of the check would fail every test that reaches it. V07 stores nothing, so 2.7 and 2.18 are unchanged. An idle run that no reserve has swept still verifies: the record stays until a reserve or an agent-time read settles it (V06 deviation 3), and the token's own `dl` (gateway) and `exp` (service endpoints) bound it. The file starts no subprocess, never flushes, and never names V04's limits table.

## 2026-10-31 V08 (OIDC verifier: mint and bind audiences, job-workflow claims, review comments, the App bot)

Section 2.10 and the V08 test list now say what is below: the identity type, the two subject forms, the rule for the added claims, the refusal codes in check order and what `actorIsBot: false` means; V09's entry and INV-S2's tests now carry the author-type check of item 8 (the section 2 preamble's "add it there first"). This entry says why each departs from, or adds to, what they said before.

1. **The added claims are strict only where they are read.** 2.10 named `runner_environment`, `ref_protected` and `repository_visibility` but said nothing about a malformed value. On `mint` and `bind`, which V12 and V10 read them on, each must be absent or well-formed (`^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,39}$`; `"true"` or `"false"`; `public`, `private` or `internal`), otherwise 401 `OIDC_INVALID`, and `mint` requires `runner_environment`. On `token` and `installation` a malformed value is recorded as absent (`refProtected: false`): INV-S10 keeps those audiences' rules, nothing on them reads these claims, and a live shape that differs from GitHub's documentation (a JSON boolean `ref_protected`, say) must not refuse every v2 App credential exchange. `runner_environment` is checked by pattern, not against its two documented values, so V12 can answer an unexpected runner with 403 `RUNNER_NOT_SUPPORTED` rather than a generic 401. A test pins the lenient side ("token and installation accept a malformed added claim as absent, as they did before V08") and mutations of either side fail it or the strict tests. Affects V10 and V12.
2. **`refProtected` is true only for the claim string `"true"`.** `"false"` and an absent claim give `false`, which fails closed for V10's bind proof (INV-S11): only a claim GitHub sent as `"true"` lets bind accept the ref without another proof. Affects V10.
3. **Refusal codes and check order stay today's.** 2.10 did not say which code each new rule answers. The job-workflow rule sits in the first 401 `OIDC_INVALID` block and compares the raw claims, so a mismatch is 401 even when `workflow_ref` is also wrong; every claim shape, the review-comment ref and the actor pattern are 401; a review comment's `ref_type`, `head_ref` and `base_ref` join today's 403 `WORKFLOW_NOT_TRUSTED` checks; the subject is 401 after them; the event list is 403 `EVENT_NOT_ALLOWED`, last. So a review-comment token signed for `token`, `installation` or `bind` fails at its ref (401) before the event list, and an otherwise valid branch token with `event_name: pull_request_review_comment` gets 403 `EVENT_NOT_ALLOWED` on those three audiences. Affects V10 (bind step 1's `EVENT_NOT_ALLOWED` already comes from the verifier) and V12 (step 7's event allow-list is already enforced; the step keeps its default-ref check).
4. **A bot outside a mint dispatch gets 401, not `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED`.** INV-S2 says every bot actor other than the App bot with a ticket "is refused at the mint with `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED`". That holds for every bot dispatch, which the verifier accepts with `actorIsBot: true` and V12's step 8 refuses. A `[bot]` actor on a comment, issue, schedule or review-comment event, or on any other audience, is refused one step earlier by the actor pattern, with the 401 `OIDC_INVALID` today's verifier gives (2.10: "nothing else accepts a `[bot]` login"). A Bot account whose login has no `[bot]` suffix is not caught here at all (item 8). Both codes are `MINT_STATUS` `denied` (2.9.1), and the generated workflow's route job stops bot events before any job (INV-S2, V14), so only an edited workflow reaches the mint with one. Affects V12, whose INV-S2 test is about dispatches.
5. **One identity type.** 2.10 listed the new fields as "optional". `GithubIdentity` stays the verifier's inferred return type, one for every audience, so they are required keys whose value may be `undefined` (2.10's type block); every `GithubIdentity` is still a `BindIdentity` (V05 deviation 2), which `packages/web`'s typecheck checks in both tests. It records `jobWorkflowRef` only: by the rule, `job_workflow_sha` equals `workflowSha`. `actorIsBot` and `refProtected` are always booleans, and `pullRequest`, `headRef` and `baseRef` are set only for a mint review comment. `ref` stays a `string` although a review comment's is `refs/pull/<n>/merge`. Affects V09, V10 and V12, which read these fields on the right audience only.
6. **A review comment's `head_ref` and `base_ref` must be branch names.** 2.10 said only "present". Each must match `^[^\s:~^?*\[\\]{1,240}$`, the branch-name part of the `refs/heads/*` pattern, otherwise 403 `WORKFLOW_NOT_TRUSTED`, because V09 compares `headRef` with the pull request's `head.ref` and a value no branch can have should never reach it.
7. **An empty `ref_type` is refused on a review comment.** 2.10 says "`branch` or absent", read literally: `""` is neither. The owner's live decode (section 4 step 5) decides whether this needs widening.
8. **`actorIsBot: false` means "no `[bot]` suffix", not "a human".** 2.10 said "nothing else accepts a bot", and V12's step 8 refuses only actors with `identity.actorIsBot`. The OIDC token carries `actor` and `actor_id` but no account type, and a GitHub Bot account's login need not end in `[bot]`: GitHub's `Copilot` account (REST `user.type: "Bot"`) matches today's actor pattern, so the verifier accepts it on every audience and event with `actorIsBot: false`, and step 8 lets it by. A one-off test under `/tmp/vector-V08/repro` (not kept in the tree; it only restates the pattern) verified a mint `issue_comment` token with `actor: "Copilot"` and got `actorIsBot: false`. The verifier cannot do better from the claims, and a list of known logins or ids would never be complete, so the module is unchanged and the guarantee moves to where the account type is known: with the App, V09's new author-type check refuses a comment, review comment or issue whose fetched author's `user.type` is not `User` with 403 `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED` (V09's entry, its test list and INV-S2's tests now say so); without the App, as for INV-S1's writer check, the generated route job's `user.type != 'Bot'` pre-filter is the only check, so such a comment or issue reaches the mint only through a workflow the repository's writers edited on its default branch (a review comment without the App is already 403 `EVENT_NEEDS_APP`). A dispatch or schedule has no fetched author, so the same gap remains for a Bot account that can dispatch the workflow or last edited its cron; V09 settles whether `verifyRun` resolves the actor's type for those events (progress, V09's note). Affects V09 and V12.

Noted for reviewers, not deviations: any `[bot]` actor, `github-actions[bot]` and `dependabot[bot]` included, passes the verifier on a mint `workflow_dispatch`; V12's step 8 refuses all but the App bot with a ticket, and no App configuration means no `[bot]` actor passes. The `EVENT_NOT_ALLOWED` message is unchanged on every audience, since no message names a cause and the CLI maps codes. For the owner's decode (section 4 step 5), beyond the `[verify]` item 2.10 marks: GitHub's documentation describes the `github.head_ref` and `github.base_ref` contexts as set only for `pull_request` and `pull_request_target`, and the `pull_request` subject form for "pull request events". If a live `pull_request_review_comment` token carries empty `head_ref` or `base_ref`, a `ref:` subject, or a `ref_type` other than `branch` or absent, the review-comment path fails closed (403 or 401) and the row needs a follow-up before V09 relies on it; the other four events are unaffected. Nothing else changes: `consumeGithubOidc`, `github-api.ts`'s `proof()` (V09 exports it as `githubProof`), `github-app.ts` and the fixture's `makeToken` are untouched.

## 2026-10-31 V09 (App trust policy)

1. **Verification interfaces are explicit.** The contract named `verifyRun` but did not give its result or how bind would obtain an admin decision. The contract-only commit `f26e1334` defines `GithubRunVerification`, the verification results, `verifyBind`, and the injectable persistent-store dependency before implementation. Run verification receives `defaultRef` and `generation` from the stored binding, alongside the binder, so it can check review-comment trust and bind its access cache to the correct binding. Neither verification method returns a credential. V10 and V12 use these results; they still consume the OIDC proof themselves after the policy checks.
2. **Binder cache values bind the decision to its subject.** A repository-keyed `1` could reuse a prior binder's permission after a takeover, or reuse a pre-suspension decision after the generation moved. The key remains `binder-ok:<repositoryId>`, but its value is `{githubId, generation}`. A mismatch triggers a fresh GitHub permission check; malformed values and failed reads or writes fail closed. Only successful checks are cached, with a one-hour TTL. V11's stored-data inventory must include these two fields; no account email or credential is stored. Old values need no migration because no earlier package wrote this cache.
3. **Dispatch and schedule actor types are already known.** The existing authorization path reads `run.actor` and `run.triggering_actor` and requires `type: User`, so no extra actor lookup or type cache is needed for the V08 open point. The same checks remain on every verification. The sole exception is the configured App bot on a mint dispatch, with its exact user id checked; V12 must approve its one-time ticket before setting `bot: true`. A different rerun actor still needs current human write permission.
4. **Issue-comment PR detection comes from GitHub.** OIDC for `issue_comment` does not identify a PR, and the body's optional `pullRequest` cannot establish that the thread is an issue. Verification fetches the thread through the issues API and checks the head repository when its `pull_request` field exists. Omitting `pullRequest` therefore cannot let a fork PR through. Review comments additionally bind the signed PR number, merge SHA, head ref, base ref, run/job heads and current workflow blob.
5. **Validation credentials need Issues read.** The server-only validation profile adds `issues: read` to read issue bodies and comments. It is revoked on every success and failure. Final task and review profiles stay unchanged; the new publish profile has no Actions permission. The public installation response is unchanged.

6. **The shared parser needs the hosted API's stricter indexed-access rule.** Importing `parseReviewCommand` into the API reaches existing parser expressions that Core's own typecheck allowed. Eleven non-null assertions make the existing bounds and mandatory regex captures explicit; they change no emitted JavaScript or parser behavior. An independent read-only review checked every assertion against its loop bound, nonempty array construction, required capture, or previously recorded token index. The focused parser regression and hosted API typecheck are the validation gates for this addition.

The progress file records passing focused tests and typechecks, the independent review, and remaining push/CI gates.

## 2026-11-01 V10 (bind API and management)

1. **O6 needs the App decision before its read-only check.** The numbered bind order placed O6 before App verification, while allowing an App-verified admin to take over. The implementation obtains that decision first; no proof or code is consumed until both the default-branch and takeover checks pass. Contract commit `b659c59b` records this correction and the route's exact input types before implementation.
2. **A disabled service is not an inactive account.** `service-off` and `source-unavailable` do not establish that the previous binder's allowance is inactive and cannot unlock O6's inactive-account exception. Account-specific inactive results still permit takeover. Admin, suspension, owner transfer and 30-day exceptions remain unchanged.
3. **The shared proof limits remain conservative.** `githubProof` keeps its existing 60-request/5-minute IP and repository limits, in addition to the cloud-agent scopes. This preserves the shipped exchange behavior while imposing the new bind limits. The public default-branch cache stores exactly `{repository, ownerId, defaultRef}` for one hour, under the repository id; mismatched identity refreshes, malformed cache fails closed.
4. **Account closure also stops code redemption.** Account endpoints already perform the required revocation read; the OIDC bind route repeats it for the account found in the code, so a code issued before account closure cannot recreate its binding afterward.
5. **Binder-left privacy includes the binder login.** INV-P3 and 2.16 say names and actors disappear after access is lost. The binding list therefore omits both `repository` and `binder`, regardless of stored visibility, while retaining the repository id needed to unbind. Suspended bindings report Codium unavailable. All API errors use neutral wording so a helper's billing-oriented message never escapes this boundary.

## 2026-11-01 V11 (run records, privacy and erasure)

1. **Run ids need internal ownership and token identity.** A GitHub check-run id can be re-minted after a repository is rebound. A global `run:<rid>` without its account, generation and token id could expose the new account's record through the old index, or let a delayed settle overwrite its outcome. Those internal fields now stay in the hash and are never returned to clients. Starts check the live ledger/binding atomically; ends compare all three identities and preserve the first matching outcome. The separately committed contract `f6cbdcbe` names these interfaces before implementation.
2. **Private listing fields are optional.** `repository` and `runUrl` cannot be required in `CloudRun` while binder-left requires omitting them. They are optional in the public type, as is the separate expiring actor. The stored record stays complete and is validated strictly before projection. Run history expires 90 days after its latest start or first matching end; ending a run does not extend its actor's start-time expiry.
3. **Erasure reaches history outside the display index.** The capped index retains only 200 ids, while records expire after 90 days. Erasure scans the record namespace as well as owned ledger/code/cache namespaces and conditionally deletes only matching accounts, preserving a replacement record owned by someone else. Generation counters and shared public default-branch metadata stay. The existing account revocation marker is required before erasure; A57 already owns that ordering.
4. **Every existing delayed writer needs a revocation fence.** Checking account auth before network/storage awaits is insufficient: erasure may finish before a late code issue, bind redemption, cache write or binder-left suspension. Their writes now check the same revocation marker atomically. Run-record starts/ends do too. Ordinary unbind/takeover privacy still records binder-left for an account that remains active. V12 must add the admission fence to ledger reservation, and V13 to ticket creation before those writers are exposed.
5. **Binder caches become attributable before production.** `{githubId, generation}` cannot be erased for its former account once a takeover removed the binding, particularly when no run was ever recorded. The cache now stores `{account, githubId, generation}`, all server-derived and all matched before reuse. Legacy two-field entries always require a fresh permission check and are rewritten only on success. Their prior account cannot be recovered reliably; erasure validates their identity shape and actual remaining TTL of 0–3600 seconds, then leaves that bounded expiry in place. It fails closed on malformed/non-expiring legacy entries. Acceptance must use the new format or wait out the legacy hour; this is not a claim that legacy caches are attributable.
6. **Ticket inventory cannot precede ticket creation.** V11 originally asked its inventory test to call the ticket writer, but that function is V13. The available binding, code, ledger and run modules are exercised now, with exact newly created keys and stored fields; V09/V10's real-store cache format assertions remain part of the lane. V13 must add the ticket-through-its-own-function inventory check, and V30 repeats the full journey.

## 2026-11-01 V12 (mint, heartbeat and settlement)

1. **Settlement needs a durable retry receipt.** V07's live-only verification made an already settled token unusable, while V12 requires settlement retries to be idempotent. A separate internal `requireSettlementToken` accepts the exact ended record's account, generation and JTI, while retaining signature, expiry, revocation, binding and `revokedBefore` checks. There is no client flag that can enable this path. Gateway, heartbeat and artifact verification remain live-only.
2. **Ledger charge and outcome must commit together.** Calling `settleRun` then `recordRunEnd` could delete the live credential before a failed outcome write, preventing a safe retry. `settleRecordedRun` instead uses the ledger's shared charge formula in one EVAL that checks the current binding/revocation and record identity, settles time and saves the first outcome. Parallel retries or a lost response return that stored outcome without applying a changed payload. The ledger-only settlement helper remains for internal callers.
3. **Exposed reservation admission checks the binding atomically.** Every reservation checks account revocation before any write. HTTP mint uses `reserveBoundRun`, the same reservation script with current binding account, generation and suspension checks. The existing unbound ledger helper remains for internal use and focused ledger tests; no HTTP route calls it. This closes the delayed-reservation erasure/takeover window noted in V11.
4. **The ticket exception is implemented in its owning package.** V12 refuses every bot actor and supplied ticket until V13 adds the one-time ticket producer, peek and consumption wiring. The ordinary human paths are complete; the valid configured-App-bot exception is not claimed here. V13 must retain the atomic revocation fence and fulfill V11's deferred ticket inventory assertion.
5. **Shared read-only verification tests identify the actual key layout.** V11's suspension writer and V12's reservation writer also declare revocation keys. The earlier fixture's four-key heuristic could incorrectly run a legitimate writer with `EVAL_RO`. The fixture now checks binding/generation/live/revocation key order; the real verifier continues to run read-only in tests.

## 2026-11-01 V13 (server dispatch and one-time tickets)

1. **A consumed ticket needs durable server provenance for renewal.** Ticket admission stores paired `triggerSource`/`triggerPurpose` in the live reservation in the same EVAL that consumes the ticket. A bot renewal without a fresh ticket must name the exact live check run, current binding generation and previously observed JTI with that provenance. The atomic script refuses stale peeks after renewal, settlement, idle removal, rebind or erasure. A normal human run has no ticket provenance and cannot be inherited by a bot. The source/purpose are projected into run history only when they match the live record.
2. **A lost reservation response must remain recoverable without weakening one-use tickets.** If the reservation commits but its response is lost, its time stays reserved and its ticket stays consumed. The same verified App-bot check run can recover from the live provenance, while a different check run cannot. A fresh mint that receives its reservation and then fails still follows the existing release rule; it does not restore the consumed ticket.
3. **Expiry is checked again at atomic consumption.** GitHub verification and allowance/rate checks can take time after a ticket peek. Admission reads the current clock again and the bound reservation checks the ticket's stored identity, source, purpose and expiry before consuming it. Ticket creation starts its 900-second payload expiry after the allowance call, and checks account revocation plus the current binding in its write transaction.
4. **Dispatch resolves names through the verified installation and pins ownership.** The App helper verifies the stored installation with the documented JWT endpoint, then checks the exact bound repository id and owner through the restricted installation token before sending the prompt. A rename works; a transfer cannot receive the previous binder's server prompt. The helper requests exactly Actions write plus Metadata read, verifies scope, sends only the specified workflow ref/inputs, and revokes its credential in `finally`.
5. **Dispatch failure is not proof of no GitHub side effect.** An unsuccessful response deletes the unconsumed ticket and reports `dispatch-failed` (or `app-required` for no installation). A response can be lost after GitHub accepted the dispatch, and revocation can fail after dispatch; no exactly-once GitHub-action guarantee is claimed. Every resulting job still has to pass ticket mint admission. No real repository dispatch was used for validation.
6. **Ticket inventory is now exercised through its owning writer.** The focused tests call `startCloudRun`, compare the exact newly created hashed ticket key and six JSON fields, and check the TTL and absence of raw ticket/prompt/requester data. Ticket-backed mint also compares exact live/history field inventories, and account erasure removes this writer's ticket while retaining another account's. This closes V11's ticket-writer deferral; V30 still repeats the whole product journey.
7. **The draft's by-id App-JWT metadata lookup was unsupported.** Inspection of GitHub's official OpenAPI at [`github/rest-api-description@f9ed74230d54847b5ca00a1343e8e349e858b8fb`, `descriptions/api.github.com/api.github.com.json`](https://github.com/github/rest-api-description/blob/f9ed74230d54847b5ca00a1343e8e349e858b8fb/descriptions/api.github.com/api.github.com.json) found neither `/repositories/{repository_id}` nor `/repositories/{repository_id}/installation`. The documented JWT endpoint is `/app/installations/{installation_id}`; a scoped installation token uses `/installation/repositories`. Contract correction `c439432a` therefore adds optional `installationId` from server-side `verifyBind` only. It is private binding metadata, cleared by a no-App rebind, omitted from public responses, and erased with the binding. Old bindings without it require App-backed rebind; no broad installation enumeration or guessed identity is used. The corrected fixture models only the supported route sequence. The inspected source file's SHA-256 is `72e362731e73c6adc9b12a4d18eb7dd8e095400ae55d47daab06c54714331b6d`.
8. **Installation credentials can contain JWT separators.** That same official installation-token endpoint documents the 2026 `ghs_APPID_JWT` rollout. The inherited opaque-token character check now allows base64url/JWT dots and hyphens while retaining its length bound and excluding whitespace/control characters. Scope verification and revocation still apply; a focused stateless-credential fixture exercises this path.

## 2026-11-01 V14 (workflow generator v3)

1. **Dollar encoding means a literal JSON escape.** Section 2.11.1's draft repeated `$` on both sides of its encoding rule. It now explicitly says `\u0024`: JSON parsing restores the original data, while generated user content never introduces an Actions expression. The YAML wrapper preserves the JSON escape until the CLI parses that data.
2. **The first agents CLI version is provisional.** Existing plans reserved no @vector CLI version. After an optional owner question and reasonable reply opportunity, root provisionally reserved `1.99.113` for unpublished source/tests. This may change before release. V14 does not change the desktop's current CLI pin, expose v3 setup, publish a CLI, or claim that version exists. The V24 and actual-release gates remain.
3. **Secret names cannot replace workflow controls.** The grammar alone admits names such as `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `VECTOR_MODE`, `NODE_OPTIONS` and `BASH_ENV`. V14 rejects generated controls and runner/shell/runtime environment names, duplicate keys and unknown option fields. Codium explicitly refuses own-key secret lines and automatic review. The header stores names, never secret values.
4. **The issue prefilter includes titles.** The draft's cheap filter inspected only issue bodies while the authoritative parser used title plus body. It now checks both, so a title-only mention reaches the writer/sender checks. Author association still only throttles and controls refusal reactions. Human dispatches receive a fresh writer check; the configured ticketed bot can route task dispatches only.
5. **New workflow options are normalized and bounded.** `publishAuth` is optional at input and defaults to `github` in the emitted header. Header parsing validates known fields but accepts old CLI versions so setup can offer an update. Schedule ids/crons are unique and syntactically bounded; V22 remains responsible for recurrence conversion. Own-key reviews reuse the v2 job structure with version 3 and the configured mentions; v2 output itself remains unchanged.
6. **Own-key reviews also declare custom secret names.** Independent review found the adapted v2 review job lacked `VECTOR_SECRET_ENV_NAMES`. V14 now emits the exact key-name list for own-key review as well as agent steps. A regression feeds the generated env through the real child-environment filter and verifies nonsuffix credentials are removed. V2 output remains unchanged.
7. **Only Codium can admit a ticketed bot.** Independent review found that own-key mode has no mint call to validate/consume a ticket. Its route now refuses all bots; the configured App bot exception is Codium-only. This preserves the no-cloud-requests own-key contract instead of adding an authorization endpoint.
8. **Pinned v3 versions do not use v2's development fallback.** The supplied v3 version must itself be canonical semver and at least `MIN_AGENTS_CLI`; raising the future desktop pin therefore cannot make an invalid header version silently valid. Review control names reserve the full `REVIEW_` prefix, and string grammar rejects final newlines as well as embedded invalid characters.

## 2026-11-01 V15 (engine cloud commands and in-memory credentials)

1. **Loading is separate from authorization.** A syntactically valid run token is held privately but cannot supply a model credential until the starting heartbeat succeeds. Root and the Codium lead confirmed that section 2.13 authorizes this narrow run-domain path to the fixed Codium catalog/chat endpoints, with no account grant, persistence, redirect, free-model/sharing request or fallback. Existing account/PKCE behavior stays unchanged outside this Actions run path.
2. **Actual request boundaries enforce service separation.** The legacy and V2 free-model adapters both use `FreeModels.resolveRoute`; V15 rejects run tokens there before either shared authorization or an OpenRouter own-key catalog lookup, including configured aliases. Sharing already validates strict `vct_` credentials in `share/transport.accountOwner` before authenticated network, so V15 proves that boundary instead of duplicating it. A59 must retain the integrated service verifier and these negative regressions.
3. **Credential files and response schemas fail closed.** The channel is bounded and strict; private files reject links/insecure permissions and are unlinked after capture. Provider keys stay in memory. Mint rate-limit retry waits are capped at 30 seconds each (one second for a malformed/missing header), in addition to the existing three-retry bound and separate ten-minute at-once loop. Server bodies and underlying authenticated exceptions are not printed.

4. **Private reads and authorization are bounded by actual work.** Independent source review identified a file-growth gap after `stat` and a delayed-success race between authorization attempts. The reader now reads at most 1,025 actual bytes and refuses overflow without consuming the invalid file; authorization results are generation-fenced. Causal regressions grow the real file after stat and resolve an older success after a newer refusal.
5. **The at-once deadline includes slow requests.** Twenty sleeps alone could exceed ten minutes once response/OIDC/retry time was included. The loop now uses a monotonic elapsed deadline, caps later work by the remaining time, and ignores late replies. Fake-clock slow-response and late-OIDC cases prove no late token write or mint.
6. **A successful heartbeat may still order a stop.** The service returns 200 with `stop: true` near deadline. Starting authorization now requires strict `ok: true, stop: false`; the refusal has no stored-account or own-key fallback. A loaded own-key channel also requires its own valid CLI token before the gate can consult any saved profile.
7. **Capture cleanup includes actual channel names.** The loader deletes validated or recognizable captured provider-key names even when `VECTOR_SECRET_ENV_NAMES` is empty, including on malformed input. The memory-backed Env service then supplies the exact names to child filtering without exporting any provider/GitHub/CLI/run credential to the OS environment. Real provider cases cover Anthropic, explicit Bedrock access credentials and bearer precedence; hosted channel mode never falls back to the SDK profile chain.
8. **Signals initiate settlement before inner cleanup.** A finalizer alone could miss the five-second cancellation bound, and same-turn success could overwrite cancellation. The heartbeat scope starts one bounded cancelled settlement directly from its signal listener and reuses it during cleanup. CI subprocess regressions cover both causal cases; ordinary completion retains first-settlement semantics.

## 2026-11-01 V16 (hosted agent sandbox)

1. **Own-key sharing consent survives the clean environment.** Root approved adding only `SHARE` and `VECTOR_SHARE_CONSENT` to `SANDBOX_ENV_ALLOW`: section 2.11 already emits these existing own-key controls, but the original allowlist dropped them. Their values retain the existing explicit-consent checks; neither their presence nor a Codium run grants sharing authority. Codium generation remains unchanged.
2. **Cleanup records trusted ownership before entering untrusted work.** A private runner-owned manifest outside the checkout pins the canonical checkout and the newly created agent UID/GID. The sandbox never adopts an existing account. Hosted cleanup kills live processes for that UID before bounded no-link artifact copying and checkout deletion; absent preparation is a no-op. Self-hosted own-key runs explicitly retain the documented lack of UID isolation and never perform hosted sudo setup or process-wide kill.
3. **The CI probe uses the repository's pinned runner.** V16's probe runs on `ubuntu-24.04`, matching every existing Vector workflow and the lane-policy test, rather than the spec's moving `ubuntu-latest` label. The actual privileged probe remains CI-only, uses synthetic markers, and grants no customer credentials or paid inference.

4. **Cleanup failure cannot upload an artifact or leave an executable checkout.** Early independent review found the draft's kill/chown operations preceded its cleanup guard. Hosted finish now enters the cleanup guard before those operations, attempts an exact-path rename into the private state directory before deep deletion, and retains trusted state when cleanup is incomplete. Both generated uploads require the named `sandbox_finish` step to succeed. A failing bundle probe must still prove checkout removal and skipped upload.
5. **Memory-only credentials also need crash-dump protection.** Same-UID tools can signal the engine, so `ptrace_scope` alone does not prevent a dump. Root approved pinning the disposable hosted job's `kernel.core_pattern=/dev/null` and launching through `prlimit --core=0:0`; run verifies the sysctl before delivering credentials. This disables piped crash handlers as well as ordinary core files, with a synthetic induced-crash CI probe. The explicit own-key sudo waiver remains a privilege waiver.

6. **Quarantine assumptions are enforced before launch.** Follow-up review identified cross-filesystem rename and setuid mount helpers as possible obstacles to removing the original checkout path. Preparation verifies equal device IDs for checkout/private state; default no-sudo launches set `no_new_privs`, blocking new privileges from setuid/file-capability helpers. The explicit own-key sudo waiver remains the exception. The hosted probe verifies the child's NoNewPrivs/core limits and the absence of a readable crash dump.

7. **Cancellation uses the same cleanup authority.** Follow-up review noted that default process termination skips async `finally`. Finish now intercepts SIGINT/SIGTERM before the first await, validates private authority, quarantines before slow cleanup operations and returns non-success after cancellation. The CI harness signals the real finish operation and proves checkout/process cleanup plus the closed upload gate. File-command scans use root-installed public path metadata and bounded read attempts; missing/wrong directories cannot count as successful isolation.

8. **Hosted filesystem evidence corrects the checkout location.** Exact probe run `41269536267` showed that a separate UID cannot traverse the runner HOME/work ancestors, that recursively chowning the installed monorepo makes this synthetic fixture exceed its command timeout, and that removing the original workspace breaks Actions post-step cwd/local-action loading. The hosted sandbox now stages the validated checkout by kernel same-device rename beneath a fixed root-owned traversable parent, leaves an empty runner-owned original workspace, and never grants traversal into runner HOME. The private manifest records the chosen staging container before the move; cleanup privatizes that container before quarantine, retains retry authority, and avoids recursive chown of agent-written files. A bounded lstat walk refuses initial hardlinks/special nodes; unsupported devices fail closed instead of invoking a copy fallback. The CI fixture compiles its harness outside the checkout, uses an externally pinned Bun action and stages a small repository fixture, while observing the actual staged location from trusted state. Pre-agent absolute toolchain PATH entries remain available with resolved Node first; relative/empty entries are dropped. Root approved this compatibility correction and a fresh independent reviewer checks the concrete change.

9. **Fresh staging review closes partial-setup edges.** A selected fixed path is not creation proof: the private manifest now records successful exclusive creation before any move or user creation; failed creation never authorizes reading or deleting that path. Sealing/quarantine errors are preserved while trusted UID shutdown and exact owned-path cleanup still run, and uploads remain refused. The runner launches from its empty original workspace and GNU env changes to the staged checkout only after UID switching, preserving valid mode-0700 repositories; the hosted fixture now exercises that case.

10. **Fresh account creation has its own preparation bound.** Run `41270955693` passed both success modes, cancellation and seal-refusal; the linked-bundle scenario failed before launching the agent because `useradd` exceeded the generic 30-second command limit. Only fresh `useradd` now has a 120-second bound; ordinary commands and cleanup retain 30 seconds. There is no retry and no adoption of an existing account. The next exact-head five-case probe must pass before V16 is complete. Run 41271377359 at 0b68e8f6 passed all five scenarios, so V16 is complete.

11. **The fresh account no longer copies `/etc/skel`.** The cause of item 10's 30-second overrun (run `41270955693`, job `124743447125`) and of the later 120-second timeout (connect-finish job `133462227639`, the cancelled scenario, prepare refused after 120.1 s) is the same: `useradd --create-home` copied the runner image's `/etc/skel` into `/home/vector-agent`. GitHub's ubuntu-24.04 image installs rustup's stable toolchain with rustfmt and clippy under `/etc/skel/.rustup` and `/etc/skel/.cargo` (runner-images `install-rust.sh`), plus nvm and other dotfiles, so every prepare copied data the agent never reads, at the runner disk's speed. Prepare took 6 s to 104 s across main, codium-finish, brain-transport and connect-finish. It was not a writeback or passwd-lock wait: a busy lock makes `useradd` exit 10 after about 15 s. `useradd` now runs with `--no-create-home`; after the UID and GID are recorded in private state, `sudo mkdir -m 0750` creates the home (it fails if the path exists, so nothing is adopted) and `chown -h` gives it to the agent. The mode matches the image's `HOME_MODE`, and native isolation's mask check (agent-owned, not group- or other-writable) still holds. The 120-second bound, the fail-closed refusal and the no-retry, no-adoption rule stay. Runner dotfiles (shell profiles that source nvm and cargo, configstore) no longer reach the agent home. The parents that `install -d` creates for the engine's private directories (`~/.local`, `~/.local/share`, `~/.local/state`, `~/.config`, `~/.cache`, `~/tmp`) are now always root-owned 0755; before, any of them that the image's skel held (such as `~/.config` with configstore) arrived agent-owned, so the agent cannot create other entries there now. _As merged into brain-transport:_ `~/.config` alone is created first by the same `install -d`, agent-owned 0700, so it stays the agent's own as before; the other parents stay root-owned (2026-11-16 V16 account skeleton, item 2). A hosted agent also loses the skel-copied `~/.rustup`, which it could reach at most through the inherited `/home/runner/.cargo/bin` proxy and only if `/home/runner` is searchable; if hosted runs need Rust, it is provided explicitly, never through the home copy. A refused sandbox command now says `timed out`, `exited <code>` or `failed`, still without argv, stdout or stderr. Not a CI-only change: real hosted runs did the same copy under the same bound.

## 2026-11-01 V17 (mechanical task helper extraction)

1. The thirteen contract helpers move with two private support functions and their issue/PR data types, so there is no helper-to-handler runtime import cycle. Context callbacks preserve call-time session/auth state, the shared publication object and PR-blocked assignment. The existing source-based marker-escaping assertion follows the new helper boundary; no new tests or behavior are introduced. The initial typecheck's readonly-text and optional-file context mismatches are corrected against the existing schema rather than by runtime coercion.

## 2026-11-08 V18a (Run bundle module, stop-code table and labelled thread text)

1. **V18 is built in parts.** V18a is `github.bundle.ts` (`RunSummary`, `parseRunSummary`, `stopReason`, `describeStop`, `linkLeavesTree`, `writeBundle`) and the agent-phase prompt builders in `github.run-helpers.ts`, none of them called yet. V18b and V18c carry the `--phase agent` path, its prompt sources, channel credentials, heartbeat and settle, the deadline timer and the end-to-end stop codes over a fake model provider. V18's Done holds only after the last part. V18a's pure table carries INV-C7's test name ("stop-code table, including an unknown code"); V18c extends that same test with the fake-provider half rather than adding a second test of that name. INV-S14's named test covers the builders here; "the policy is in the system instructions" stays V03's `github.policy.test.ts` until V18b wires the builders into a session.
2. **The bundle's stop set is closed, and the outcome comes from the patch.** Contract 2.1 allowed `RunSummary.stop` to be "another code from the default row". It is now a recorded `StopCode` or `"deadline"`, and the default row records none (spec 2.1, 2.9.2 and 2.12 now say so; this contract clarification goes first, in its own docs commit). So that publish can still word every case, writeBundle derives the outcome instead of taking it from the caller: changes give `changes`, or `draft` after a stop or the default row; no changes give `no-changes`, or `failed` after a stop or the default row. `failed` or `draft` with no `stop` is the default row ("Vector stopped early"). `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_LIMIT` also records the gateway's `limit` (`ceiling`, `hours` or `at-once`, allowed only with that stop), so publish can choose between the ceiling line and the used-up line. A bundle's `outcome` is one of `changes`, `no-changes`, `draft` and `failed`: `refused` and `cancelled` are settle outcomes with no bundle. `draft` is true exactly for the `draft` outcome. These follow the plan critique; publish (V21) relies on the pair being consistent.
3. **The six "unavailable for this binding" codes are recorded as `MODEL_PLAN_BLOCKED`.** `summary.json` is a run artifact that anyone who can read a public repository can download for `BUNDLE_DAYS`, so `MODEL_PLAN_EXHAUSTED`, `MODEL_PLAN_PAYMENT_REQUIRED`, `MODEL_PLAN_REQUIRED`, `MODEL_PLAN_CAPACITY` and `CODIUM_PERK_REQUIRED` would publish the binder's billing or plan state (INV-P1). This mirrors contract departure 5 at the mint. It is one constant, `UNAVAILABLE_STOP`, and the schema's stop set is derived from the table, so `parseRunSummary` refuses a tampered bundle that carries any of the five. Affects V20 (the review path's reason line) and V21 (publish's refusal table).
4. **`stopReason(error, { codium })` maps only Codium gateway errors.** The plan read `{error: {code}}` from any API error. A third-party provider or proxy body can carry the same words, and an own-key run has nothing to do with Codium (INV-K1, INV-K2), so with `codium: false` every error takes the default row. The caller passes `true` only when the failed request went to the model plans provider in a Codium run. The code comes from the response body's `error.code` (bodies over 64 KiB are not parsed), then from `metadata.code`; only a table code counts, and malformed `data` takes the default row, so it never throws. `MODEL_PLAN_BUSY` and `MODEL_PLAN_RENEWING` answer `draft`, because the session's own client has already retried them when they are the final error; `MODEL_PLAN_CONCURRENCY` answers `wait`, and the caller bounds it by `CONCURRENCY_RETRY_MS`. `DEADLINE` is the heartbeat and local-deadline row. Carried to V18b: `SessionRetry` (`src/session/retry.ts`) skips only errors that carry `metadata.codium`, so it retries the Codium 429s and 5xx that `gatewayFailure` does not classify (the run-limit, daily, concurrency and busy codes among them) up to 10 times within 15 minutes. V18b must make the 2.9.2 draft codes non-retryable, or count that time against the deadline, before wiring.
5. **git runs pinned, with an exact environment and no transport.** Contract 2.12 and V18 name `git add -A && git diff --cached --binary <baseSha>` with hooks and fsmonitor off. writeBundle adds, after an experiment on git 2.43 and the plan critique: `--src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ --no-relative` (they override `diff.noprefix`, `diff.mnemonicPrefix`, `diff.relative`, and `diff.srcPrefix`/`diff.dstPrefix`, which git 2.45 and later honour; the lane's runners have git 2.56.0), `-U3` (`diff.context=0` makes the patch unapplicable), `-O/dev/null` (a missing `diff.orderFile` makes git die), `--no-renames`, `--ignore-submodules=none`, `--submodule=short`, `--no-color`, `--no-ext-diff`, `--no-textconv`, `-c core.safecrlf=false` (with `core.autocrlf`, `safecrlf=true` makes `git add` die on a mixed-line-ending file), `-c core.sparseCheckout=false` (a sparse-checkout definition makes `git add` refuse every new path outside it, exit 1, seen on git 2.43.0), and `-c core.splitIndex=false -c core.untrackedCache=false` (the private index of item 7 stays one file, with no shared index in `.git/`). git starts with `PATH`, `HOME` and `SYSTEMROOT` only, plus `LC_ALL=C`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=none`, `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`, and with `-c protocol.allow=never`. `protocol.allow=never` alone is only a default that a repository's `protocol.<name>.allow` overrides; `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL` overrides those, and `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH` stops a promisor remote's lazy fetch by itself (each one alone blocked the test's partial-clone fetch). Clean and process filters named by the agent's own `.gitattributes`, `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes`, `$HOME/.config/git/attributes` or `.git/config` still run during `git add`, as the agent user with that exact environment (the test records the filter's environment with `printenv`); the agent can already run any command as that user. Because the private index is read from the base with no stat data, the add hashes, and filters, every tracked file, not only the changed ones. `--attr-source` was not used: it needs git 2.40 and leaves `$GIT_DIR/info/attributes` in force. `vector-out/` is excluded from the add and the untracked listing as well as the diff, so a `vector-out/` tracked at the base never reaches the patch and a repository inside it cannot make the add fail. writeBundle runs only from the repository's top level.
6. **A patch over a limit becomes `failed` with an empty patch.** It is never truncated. The checks run in the order `files` (more than `PATCH_LIMITS.files` new paths for the add, or listed changes before item 7's reset; one `files` refusal also covers a listing over 16 MB, which is read no further), `fileBytes` (the size of each new blob) and `bytes` (the patch, read up to `PATCH_LIMITS.bytes`, then git is stopped). writeBundle's result reports `files` as the paths in the patch, or for the `files` refusal the count that tripped it (undefined when a listing was too long to read); the count is taken before anything is dropped, so it can only make the refusal more conservative. The plan put a fixed sentence (`PATCH_REFUSED`) into `summary.comment`; following the critique, the bundle records the limit as data in `refused` and keeps the agent's comment, and publish's fixed refusal table (2.17, V21) words the comment.
7. **The patch comes from a private index, and what publish refuses or git cannot stage is dropped and listed, not left to sink the bundle.** The agent writes `.git/index`, so an entry it plants there survives `git add -A`: a hand-written `.git/hooks/pre-commit` entry marked assume-valid reached `git diff --cached` and the binary patch (seen on git 2.43.0 in review), and assume-valid or skip-worktree entries can hide a worktree change or inject content the tree does not hold. writeBundle therefore sets `GIT_INDEX_FILE` to a fresh file in a `mkdtemp` directory outside the worktree (it throws if the temporary directory is inside the repository), runs `git read-tree <baseSha>` there (read-tree applies git's `verify_path`: a tree holding `.GIT/config` made it exit 128 on git 2.43.0), then the add, the listing, the reset and the diff against that index, and removes it afterwards; the agent's own index is left as it was. INV-S12 makes publish refuse a whole patch that holds a gitlink or a symlink out of the tree, and git refuses the whole add for an untracked repository with no commit (`does not have a commit checked out`) or a name such as `x/.GIT/config` or `git~1` (`invalid path`), so one cloned dependency, one `git init` fixture or one odd name would lose the run's work. Before the add, `git ls-files --others` (without `--directory`, which hides a repository inside an untracked directory) lists the new paths: every repository of its own (git names a directory only for one, with or without a commit) and every path with an unsafe component is kept out of the add with a `:(exclude,literal)` pathspec. The review asked for an `lstat` of each directory's `.git` as well; it is not needed, because the listing names no other directory, and excluding is the safe answer either way. After the add, gitlinks either way, links that leave the tree and, as defence in depth, any listed path with an empty, `.` or `..` component, a leading `/`, or a `.git` component in any case or in an NTFS reading (trailing dots or spaces, a stream suffix, `git~1`) are reset to the base. A literal pathspec also resets everything under a path, so each listed entry the reset took back is listed too (a directory replaced by a link out of the tree comes back whole, its files' deletions with it). All of these are listed in the new `dropped` field, sorted, and the rest of the patch is kept. A link whose blob is over 4,096 bytes (with `core.symlinks=false` the add keeps a link's mode and stores a plain file's content as its target) counts as leaving the tree; link blobs are sized first and only shorter ones read. `linkLeavesTree` is exported for V21's validator: an empty, absolute or NUL-bearing target, one that climbs above the root, or one that reaches a `.git` component. The check is textual, as `git apply` writes the link, and publish keeps the last word. Known limits, carried to V21: files written inside a directory that the base records as a gitlink are never staged (git treats the path as a submodule), so they are in neither the patch nor `dropped`; and a link named `.gitmodules`, which `verify_path` also refuses, still makes the add fail, so writeBundle throws.
8. **Untrusted text is shortened to fit, never refused.** The plan threw when the summary failed the schema or went over `summaryBytes`, which the model controls (a 60,000-character body of 4-byte or control characters passes the character limit and breaks the byte limit). writeBundle now throws only for a wrong input (route, base or stop), a wrong directory, a git failure (a lazy fetch it refuses included) or an unsafe `vector-out/`, all before anything is written. Text is escaped with `escapeVectorMarkers` first. The title becomes one line of at most `TITLE_MAX_CHARS` (256, a new bound), cut with "…". The body and the comment are cut to `BODY_MAX_CHARS` each, and then share what `summaryBytes` leaves: each keeps all of its text when it needs no more than half. Cuts fall on whole characters and end with `SHORTENED` ("(Vector shortened this text.)", after a blank line). That string is new user-visible text outside 2.17's copy-test list, recorded here for V21 and V29. In a path list, a name over 4,096 characters or one that would open a Vector marker is left out (the schema refuses such a name too), a name repeated because two non-UTF-8 paths read alike is kept once, and a list over 32 KiB of JSON is written empty: publish recomputes `heldBack` from the patch (2.12), and `dropped` is informational. The names are the agent's choice, so publish (V21) must write any path it echoes, from a list or the patch, after `escapeVectorMarkers` and inside a code span.
9. **writeBundle takes no signal.** It bounds its own git commands (`timeoutMs`, 120 s by default), so the abort that ends a session at its deadline cannot also stop the bundle that saves its work. `vector-out/` must be a real directory, and each bundle file is removed and created anew with `O_EXCL` and mode 0600, so a planted link at either name is replaced, never followed. A process that swaps `vector-out/` for a link after the check can only make the engine write where the agent user can already write, and the sandbox copies only regular files out (V16).
10. **The agent-phase builders encode every GitHub string, not only bodies.** Titles, bodies, logins, dates, states, branch names, paths, review states and change types all go through `untrustedJson`; numbers are written plainly. Reviews count as comments: a review by someone without write access collapses with its line comments, whose authors are then never looked up, and a line comment inside a writer's review carries its own author's label. Lookups run one at a time, in thread order, as GitHub asks of a single token; `permissionOf` answers a login it has seen from its cache. Bots have no collaborator permission, so their comments, Vector's own replies included, collapse (V27 carries continuity). A missing author is written as JSON `null`, labelled untrusted with no lookup, and a missing text as `""`. The lead line only says the thread is labelled JSON data: the rule stays in `githubSystemInstructions`, never in the user turn (INV-S14). The trigger comment is left out, as in v2; how the agent phase labels the trigger text itself is carried to V18b. No shared or v2 builder changed: `git diff --numstat` shows 155 lines added and 0 removed in `github.run-helpers.ts`, and `githubRun`'s v2 output stays as it was (2.11.5).
11. **"Share" in the ceiling line.** 2.9.2's exact ceiling line is "this run reached its share of the month", while 2.17 says refusal comments never name a share. `STOP_REASON.ceiling` keeps 2.9.2's string as the contract's fixed text. The P6 test is named for what it checks (plan ids, "<Plan> plan" names, the INV-P1 billing words, and `%`, `$`, tokens, credits and price), not "share". The owner's legal read (section 4 step 11) and V21's refusal table should settle the wording.
12. **Review before the first commit.** A review of the uncommitted V18a found two blocking defects, both fixed above before anything was committed: an untracked repository with no commit made `git add` fail and lost the bundle (item 7), and item 7's old claim that `git add` cannot produce a `.git/` path was false while writeBundle staged into the agent's index (item 7 now describes the private index). The non-blocking findings were all taken: the reset's collateral is listed (item 7), a collapsed review's line-comment authors are not looked up and lookups run one at a time (item 10), spec 2.1's `OUTCOME` row names the bundle subset, the `files` count is reported (item 6), the hostile-config test uses `printenv` and the file header says the git fixtures need a POSIX shell and symlinks, the config comment no longer says every command-running setting is pinned (item 5), path lists refuse and leave out marker names (item 8), link blobs are sized before they are read (item 7), and the progress entry names the imported files. The one departure from a suggested remedy is the `lstat` check of item 7.

## 2026-11-08 V18b (Agent phase: own key and shared core)

1. **Contract clarification, which goes first in its own `docs(github)` commit.** Five spec lines change, and nothing else in the contract does:
   - The 2.13 row for `run [--phase agent]` now also exits 0 when the phase refuses the event with one log line and no bundle.
   - V18's Files line names `github.agent.ts` (new: the `--phase agent` path), `github.ts`, the lifted session helpers in `github.run-helpers.ts`, and `github.handler.ts`'s smaller part.
   - V18's prompt bullet says that until V22 a schedule is refused with one log line, no bundle and exit 0.
   - INV-S14's second bullet and V18's thread-text bullet now say that the verified writer's trigger text (the trigger comment's body, an `issues` event's text after the mention, a dispatch's `VECTOR_PROMPT`) is the task and goes first as written, and that everything else reaches the prompt as labelled JSON (item 5). The owner's legal read (section 4 step 11) sees it there.

   The package text asked for a "refused bundle". `RunSummary` has no refused outcome (deviation V18a item 2), so a refusal writes no bundle, which matches V22's "unknown cron logs and exits 0 with outcome refused". "After the mention" is kept as the contract says (item 5), so it needs no change.

   A missing bundle then has two causes, which the bundle alone cannot tell apart: a refusal (the agent step exits 0 and writes none: nothing to publish) and a crash before the route and the base are known (exit 1 and none: one failure comment). Publish must tell them apart by the agent job's result, not by the bundle. Two later packages carry this, and the `docs(github)` commit body names both:
   - **V21:** "a missing bundle → generic failure comment" holds only when the agent job did not succeed; a successful agent job with no bundle publishes nothing.
   - **V24** (the package that moves the builder's callers to v3): publish must learn the agent job's result, which the v3 builder does not pass today (own key's `VECTOR_AGENT_STATUS` is the literal `ok`). One way is `VECTOR_AGENT_RESULT: ${{ needs.agent.result }}` in publish's `env:`, added to the expression allowlist (INV-S15). A missing `vector-run-*` artifact already fails no step: the V14 upload step keeps `actions/upload-artifact@v4`'s default `if-no-files-found: warn`, and the download step has `continue-on-error: true` (`packages/core/src/review/workflow.ts`). The builder must keep both, or set `if-no-files-found: ignore`.

2. **`githubRun`'s session code is lifted, not copied (plan critique).** The plan wrote a second session loop for the phase. Following the critique, `chat`, `summarize`, `subscribeSessionEvents`, `collectSessionEvidence` and `taskPart` move to `github.run-helpers.ts` with explicit contexts, as V17 did.
   - `githubRun` keeps one-line function declarations that call them with the same arguments, so hoisting, logs and messages are unchanged. `taskPart` is re-exported from the handler.
