   - `chat`'s thrown errors keep their messages and now carry the session's own error as `cause`, which V18c's `stopReason` reads. The phase's error test checks that this cause maps to `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_LIMIT` with `codium: true`.
   - `summarize` takes its fallback as a thunk. `githubRun`'s thunk is the same `Fix issue: …` expression, still evaluated in the catch.
   - `getUserPrompt`'s attachment loop is now a private `attachments`, which the phase's comment prompt shares.
   - `fixContext`, `isFixCommand` and `findingForPrompt` move to `github.run-helpers.ts` unchanged, with `GITHUB_ACTIONS_BOT` for the handler's private `ACTIONS_BOT` (the same string), and the handler re-exports them, so v2 callers and `github.run-routing.test.ts` import them as before. The agent phase then never loads `github.handler.ts`: that module pulls in `@actions/github`, whose context reads `GITHUB_EVENT_PATH` at import, `@clack/prompts` and the whole v2 job (review finding).
   - The lift was planned as its own `refactor(github)` commit (`github.handler.ts` and `github.run-helpers.ts`); it went up inside the `feat(github)` commit instead (item 15). Its proof is the CI-only `github.run-routing.test.ts` and `github.review.test.ts`, plus `github.policy.test.ts`'s source checks, which pass locally.

   The phase itself lives in the new `github.agent.ts`, so `githubRun` stays the v2 single job (2.11.5). Routing is the one part not reused. v2's `routeGithubEvent` and `taskRoutePlan` decide review verbs and `PROMPT` fallbacks for the v2 job. The phase instead re-checks the v3 route job's rules (item 6) in `agentRoute`, which uses the same `parseReviewCommand` and `mentionsFrom`.

3. **Codium is refused until V18c, with no instance (deviation V18a item 4).** A Codium run logs `CODIUM_NOT_YET` and settles `refused` with the authorized run token. That releases the at-once slot the gate's starting heartbeat opened. Following the critique, `instance` returns false on Codium, so no configuration, plugin or catalog loads in the engine that holds the run token.
   - **This departs from the package brief, not only from the contract.** The brief defers only the Codium lifecycle (heartbeat, concurrency wait, deadline) to V18c and expects V18b to run Codium sessions on the catalog's default entry, with errors mapped through `stopReason`. A Codium session without that lifecycle would heartbeat never (the journey's "every 60 seconds") and run past its deadline instead of stopping 3 minutes before it, and `SessionRetry` would retry the 2.9.2 draft codes for up to 15 minutes, which V18a item 4 requires fixing before wiring. So the whole Codium session path moves to V18c. `agentModel`'s Codium branch, `stopFor`'s `codium: true` mapping and the `cause` that `chat` now keeps are built and tested here, and no production path reaches them until V18c. A review of the uncommitted package flagged this; it needs the orchestrator's explicit acceptance, and the `feat(github)` commit body repeats it.
   - Heartbeat and settle are gated on `VECTOR_MODE === "codium"`, not on whether a token exists. The INV-K1 test gives the own-key phase a holder with a well-formed `vrt_` token to prove it.
   - V18a item 4 carried the `SessionRetry` change to V18b. It moves to V18c, with the Codium gateway codes, the deadline and the concurrency wait it guards, and V18a's carry list now says so.
   - `agentModel`'s Codium rule (`vector-plan` is the catalog's first entry through `Provider.defaultModelIDs`; `vector-plan/<id>` must be listed) is built and tested now.
4. **Credentials come only from the loader.** index.ts already loads the channel before the account gate, so it is unchanged.
   - Without a loaded holder, `instance` returns false and the handler exits 1 with `AGENT_NEEDS_CHANNEL`.
   - Share consent is read from the holder's CLI token, on own key only. Only `SHARE`, `VECTOR_SHARE_CONSENT` and that token are passed, never a spread of `process.env`.
   - The share service is looked up with `Effect.serviceOption`, so a run without consent never needs it.
   - The phase never writes `process.env`.
   - What each test proves (critique):
     - the in-process case uses a holder of the test's own, and proves the phase does not export it;
     - the `environment` child of `github.agent-channel.test.ts` loads the process's own holder, as index.ts does, with `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false` so the Actions child filter is off. A tool's child then sees none of the fixture values, `process.env` holds none, and `Env` serves the provider key from memory;
     - the CI-only CLI test checks the loader-before-gate order, the gate's use of the channel's CLI token, and the value of a provider key whose name `SECRET_NAME_PATTERN` does not match.
   - No test prints an environment (critique blocker): the shell tool runs a fixed `printf` per fixture value of `env | grep -cF`, which prints counts only. Every check of `process.env` prints names or fixture values only.
   - An early local run printed one of this machine's environment values into a scratch log, through a `toBeUndefined()` failure. That log was deleted at once, and the check now compares name lists.
5. **The task text.** The trigger is the verified writer's task and goes first as written. Every other GitHub string is labelled JSON. This answers V18a item 10's carry, and is how this package reads INV-S14, whose text said every body is labelled JSON; item 1's spec clarification now says so in INV-S14 and V18. It is not added to this file's opening list, which holds only the contract's departures from Codium's SPEC §4.18 decided before V00 and says packages do not record there (a review asked for that line).
   - A comment is used as today: its body, or for a bare mention "Summarize this thread", or on a review comment a review of its lines. Only the comment's own attachments are downloaded.
   - An issue's task is the text after the first mention in its title and body, from the event payload, as the contract says (`afterMention`, which counts a mention as a word, drops the punctuation that ends it, and falls back to the whole text when nothing follows). The plan used the whole issue text; the critique preferred the contract.
   - A dispatch's task is `VECTOR_PROMPT`, never the payload's `inputs.prompt`.
   - The thread comes from the API through V18a's labelled builders.
   - A review comment's file, line and diff hunk follow in `<review_comment_context>` as JSON (`Diff hunk (untrusted)`), where v2 wrote them raw (critique).
   - The `/vector fix` finding goes before the comment as `<review_finding>`, with its location and its whole comment as JSON labelled untrusted. `fixContext` passes only Vector's own bot's comments, and a bot has no write access. `fixContext` gains an optional `format`; v2 still uses `findingForPrompt`.
   - The untrusted-data rule stays in the system instructions only.
6. **The route job's rules are checked again (critique).**
   - Comments and review comments must be `created`, by a person, and must ask for a task by `parseReviewCommand` with `MENTIONS`.
   - An issue must be `opened` by a person, and its author's id must equal the sender's.
   - A dispatch must be in task mode, by a person (own key refuses every bot: V18c admits Codium's ticket dispatches), with a non-empty `VECTOR_PROMPT` and a decimal `GITHUB_RUN_ID`.
   - Fork pull requests are refused after the API read.
   - `VECTOR_THREAD` must be present, equal to the route's thread and match `THREAD_PATTERN`; otherwise the phase exits 1.
   - A `VECTOR_MODE` other than own-key or codium exits 1.
   - The trigger's author is checked again with the cached `permissionOf`. A lookup that fails reads as no permission.
   - Every refusal is one log line, no bundle and exit 0.
7. **Own-key runs never reach Codium, whatever the configuration names (critique).** `GithubSecrets.ownKeyRun()` is true when the holder is loaded with no run token. While it is, the provider layer leaves the Codium provider out of its state and out of `refreshCodium`, and `getModel` refuses it with `OWN_KEY_CODIUM`. So a repository's `small_model`, an agent's model or a subagent's model naming `vector-plan/*` cannot send a Codium request, and `getSmallModel` falls back to the run's own provider.
   - The catalog listing is still read with the CLI token, as INV-K1 allows.
   - `provider.ts` is a shared file (contract 0.2), and the seam is one named predicate.
   - The `codium-config` child tests it with a fake Codium catalog that lists a model and a recording chat transport. With the three guards removed it fails, at the provider listing. In this harness no turn reached the Codium transport even without the guards: the title agent did not run, MODEL overrides the build agent's model, and the general subagent's task ended before the transport. So the test asserts the provider's answers (list, `getModel`, `getSmallModel`) as well as "no Codium request".
8. **GitHub is read-only in the phase, and git runs pinned.**
   - `readOnlyGithub`'s Octokit and GraphQL clients send through `readOnlyFetch`, which wraps `githubFetch`, so Vector Local's egress policy still applies. It allows GET and HEAD, and a GraphQL POST whose body is one query:
     - the body holds `query` and, optionally, `variables`, and nothing else, so no `operationName` can choose another operation of the document;
     - the raw document names no `mutation` or `subscription` anywhere and holds exactly one `query` keyword, or none for a shorthand `{`;
     - once leading whitespace, commas, a byte order mark and comments are skipped, it opens with that `query` or `{`.

     Anything else rejects with `READ_ONLY` before it is sent. The words are looked for in the raw text because GraphQL reads a `#` inside a string as text: the first build stripped `#…` to the end of the line first, so `query Q { a(x: "#") } mutation M { … }` with `operationName: "M"` passed (review finding, blocking). A comment or a string that holds one of the words now refuses the body, which only fails closed; the engine's own queries hold none. Only the engine's fixed queries use this client, and the job's token is read-only too.

   - Following the critique, `ReviewGitHubOptions` gains an optional `fetch`, so `permissionOf` goes through the same function; its `check` rejects every post.
   - The lane's own `fetchIssue` query, which starts with a newline, is tested to pass.
   - `botLogin` is `GITHUB_ACTIONS_BOT`. The plan's MENTIONS heuristic is dropped (critique): `slugFromInstallUrl` never matches a handle, and O14's default posts as github-actions[bot]. The plan's item 10 is gone.
   - The phase runs three kinds of git command itself:
     - `git rev-parse --verify HEAD^{commit}`, once, in `begin`, through the engine's `Git.Service` (which pins only `core.fsmonitor=false`). It runs before the session starts, so before any agent output exists, and it reads only.
     - The evidence's change count, only when the session's own diff lists nothing (an edit made through the shell): `GithubBundle.numstat` reads the base into a private index (`read-tree`) and runs `git diff --numstat --no-ext-diff --no-textconv` with writeBundle's listing flags against the base, outside `vector-out/`. It runs with writeBundle's pinned config (`core.hooksPath=/dev/null`, `core.fsmonitor=false`, `protocol.allow=never` and the rest of deviation V18a item 5) and exact environment (`PATH`, `HOME` and `SYSTEMROOT` only, plus `LC_ALL=C`, `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1`, `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, `GIT_ALLOW_PROTOCOL=none`, `GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH=1` and `GIT_NO_REPLACE_OBJECTS=1`), and takes only a hex commit id as the base. A failure lists no changes, and the bundle still records the patch.
     - `writeBundle`'s commands, pinned the same way (deviation V18a item 5).

     The first build ran the count through `Git.Service`, with the agent's hooks, global and system config, transports and index in force (review finding, blocking). The no-network-git test now makes its edit through the shell, which also points `core.hooksPath` and `core.fsmonitor` at scripts that leave a marker and leaves a tracked file stat-dirty: restoring the old count runs the agent's `post-index-change` hook and fails it. The session's snapshots run git on the engine's own snapshot repository, as in every session, and the shell tool runs whatever git the agent asks for; neither is the phase's own command.

9. **Model rules.** Own key needs MODEL, with no `defaultModel` fallback, since v3 always sets it. It refuses `vector-plan` with `OWN_KEY_CODIUM` (INV-K1), and answers an unlisted model with v2's update message.
10. **Error rows and exits.**
    - Before the route and the base are known (event, route, thread, repository, run id, token, HEAD), the phase exits 1 with no bundle.
    - After that, any failure is a bundle with the default row: a thread read, the model, the session, or a turn's error. An own-key error never maps to a stop code (`stopReason` with `codium: false`).
    - `writeBundle` throwing exits 1.
    - SIGINT and SIGTERM return nothing, and `withGithubSignals` sets 130 or 143.
    - The error test uses a 400 from the fake model. A 5xx is retried by `SessionRetry` for up to 15 minutes, which is V18c's to bound.
11. **Bundle text, checked like everything `githubRun` posts (critique).**
    - The title is the summary turn; after a stop or a failed summary it is the thread's title, or `FALLBACK_TITLE` ("Changes from Vector"). So is a title the posting check empties, because every sentence of it repeats memory (review finding).
    - The body is `buildEvidenceBody` without `Closes` (publish adds it, 2.12), with "Triggered by workflow_dispatch" for dispatches. Its changes come from the session's diff, or from the pinned count of item 8 when the session kept none.
    - The comment is the reply.
    - All three pass `SessionPosting.headless` (`typed: false`, `untrusted: false`, since forks are refused) before `writeBundle`, because publish runs in another job with no session lineage. A refused or failed check keeps the thread title and writes `WITHHELD` as body and comment.
    - The new user-visible strings (`WITHHELD`, `FALLBACK_TITLE` and the fixed log lines in `github.agent.ts`) are outside 2.17's copy-test list, and are recorded here for V29. None uses an INV-H1 word.
12. **Test fixtures.**
    - `commitSetup` commits what `setup()` writes (its `vector.json`) before the base is taken, and fails unless `git status --porcelain` is empty. Without it, every run would have staged the test configuration (critique).
    - The research runtime gains a `replacements` option for the fake Codium catalog.
    - The origin that "requires auth" (`authRemote`) is a loopback HTTP server that answers 401 to every request, not a bare git repository. Any network git to `origin` (fetch, push, a lazy fetch) reaches it, and the test asserts it saw nothing.
    - The phase's injectable `env` covers every read of its own: event, route, `MENTIONS`, `VECTOR_PROMPT`, `MODEL`, `VECTOR_THREAD`, the API and server URLs and the run id. It never reads v2's `PROMPT` or `VARIANT`, which `SANDBOX_ENV_ALLOW` does not pass. `getAgentUserPrompt` takes the mentions explicitly. The attachment download and the provider layer still read process-wide state, as in v2 (critique minor, documented rather than changed).
13. **CI-only tests.**
    - `github.agent-cli.test.ts` runs `vector github run --phase agent` through `cliIt.actionsStep`, with the channel on stdin, `VECTOR_SITE_URL` pointing at a loopback fake site, and a loopback fake GitHub. The CLI fixture sets `VECTOR_DISABLE_MODELS_FETCH`, which turns off the free-model and Codium catalog reads whose URLs are fixed to vectordev.ai (critique blocker). The child also inherits this file's strict network guard through its proxy settings, and the test asserts that no attempt left the machine.
    - The parents of `github.agent-channel.test.ts` spawn `bun test`, as `github.provider-credentials.test.ts` does, because the process's holder outlives a test and the lane runs every engine GitHub file in one process.
    - INV-K1 is asserted through the cloud client's `Services` seam and the guard's attempts, since the engine preload's guard records origins, not paths.
14. **Review before the first commit.** A review of the uncommitted V18b found two blocking defects, both fixed above: the GraphQL check could be bypassed with a `#` inside a string (item 8), and the evidence's fallback count ran unpinned git on the agent's worktree, which also made item 8 contradict item 11 (item 8 now names every git command the phase runs). The non-blocking findings were taken: the agent phase no longer imports `github.handler.ts` (item 2); an emptied title falls back (item 11); the author test asserts `AUTHOR_REFUSED` and gains a bot `issues` case and a missing-id case; the dynamic import in `github.ts` is destructured; the lane's pull-request paths and the policy's required paths gain `packages/engine/src/provider/provider.ts`, `src/session/llm/request.ts`, `src/session/posting.ts` and `test/lib/research.ts`, which the own-key seam, the pinned policy, the posting check and the new tests depend on (the workflow and `packages/cloud/src/workflow-compliance.test.ts` are shared files, listed in the commit body); the missing-bundle carry tells a refusal from a crash and names V24 (item 1); INV-S14's reading is a spec clarification (items 1 and 5). Two departures from a suggested remedy: the Codium session path stays in V18c, pending the orchestrator's acceptance (item 3), and the INV-S14 line goes into the spec rather than this file's opening list (item 5).
15. **Two commits, not three.** The plan (item 2 and the progress entry) put the lift in a `refactor(github)` commit of its own between the spec clarification and the feature. It went up inside `feat(github): V18b run the agent phase and write the bundle`, after the spec clarification `2b597921`. The lift and the new code share `github.run-helpers.ts` hunk by hunk (the lifted `chat` gains the error `cause` and `fixContext` its `format` in the same lines), so a lift-only commit would have been an intermediate tree that nothing typechecked or tested: both commits go up in one push and the lane runs only at the head. The commit gate named one feature commit. The lift's proof is unchanged: the CI-only `github.run-routing.test.ts` and `github.review.test.ts` at the exact head, and the local in-process cases of the progress entry.

## 2026-11-08 V18c (Codium lifecycle, deadline and stop codes in the agent phase)

1. **Contract clarification, which goes first in its own `docs(github)` commit.** Section 0.2 and section 3 V18 change, and nothing else in the contract does:
   - 0.2's shared list gains `packages/engine/src/session/retry.ts` (the pinned Codium gateway rule, used only while a Codium agent phase runs), and V18's Files line names it and `github.lifecycle.ts` (plan critique: retry.ts is used by the desktop for every provider, so it needs the same visibility as `provider.ts`).
   - The prompt bullet: a Codium `workflow_dispatch` from the App bot (a `Bot` sender with the App's login) with a well-formed ticket skips the writer re-check, and its prompt is labelled untrusted JSON under a fixed task line (item 9).
   - The heartbeat bullet: the mode decides, the settle outcomes (item 7), and V15's existing rule that any heartbeat answer other than a strict `{ok: true, stop: false}` stops the run (item 4).
   - A logging bullet: on Codium a gateway answer, or an error with a model provider's answer in its cause chain, is logged only as its 2.9.2 reason line (item 10).
   - The Deadline bullet: epoch milliseconds, the parse, fail closed on Codium, no timer on own key without one, and a stop time already passed (item 5).
   - The Errors bullet: the 2.9.2 retries in a Codium run, with the run-wide concurrency window and its 2 s to 30 s spacing (item 3).

   The package brief named `github.handler.ts` and, if needed, `github.bundle.ts`. The agent phase lives in `github.agent.ts` (V18b), so `github.handler.ts` is not touched, and `stopReason` needed no fix, so `github.bundle.ts` is not edited either.

2. **V18b repair: the secret channel loader reads stdin's events.** V18b's lane run `42455742210` (at `9d78e572`) failed in the CI-only `github.agent-cli.test.ts`: the CLI agent phase exited 1 with "The GitHub run credentials are unavailable or invalid." The planner reproduced it on Bun 1.3.14 under `/tmp/vector-vam/v18c-plan/`, the real CLI included: once anything reads `process.stdin.isTTY` before the loader runs, as the CLI's startup does, `for await (const chunk of process.stdin)` ends at once with none of the bytes, while the stream's `data` and `end` events still deliver the whole line. The implementer's probe (`/tmp/vector-v18c/node-probe/bun-parent.ts`, run through the lock) narrows it: the bytes are lost when Bun.spawn is given stdin as bytes, as the CLI tests' harness gives it (0 of 401 bytes), but not through a node:child_process pipe that is ended, which is how `github.sandbox.ts` writes the channel (401 of 401). So the plan's "every real sandbox run would fail" is not shown; what is shown is that the loader's success depended on how its stdin was made, which the fix removes.
   - `github.secrets.ts`'s default input is now a private `streamChunks(process.stdin)`, an async generator over `data`, `end`, `close` and `error`. A stream that has already ended or been destroyed yields nothing, so the loader refuses it instead of waiting (critique). Its `finally` removes the listeners and destroys the stream, which closes stdin as 2.13 asks; the plan's `pause()` did not (critique). `channelLine` is unchanged, with its `CHANNEL_LIMIT` bound and one-line rule, and tests that pass their own generator are unchanged. It uses Node stream APIs only.
   - It calls `stream.resume()` once its listeners are on (review). A `data` listener does not start a stream that something has paused (`readableFlowing === false`), so without it a stdin paused before the loader ran would deliver nothing and the job would wait for its timeout; the old `for await` resumed the stream itself. Nothing in the CLI's startup pauses stdin today, so this was latent. A probe (`/tmp/vector-v18c/resume-probe/`, through the lock; logs `resume-probe-bun.log` and `resume-probe-node.log`) ran a copy of the fixed loader and a copy without `resume()`, each with a child that reads `isTTY` and, in one variant, calls `process.stdin.pause()` first. Bun 1.3.14 parent, Bun children: with `resume()` all six cases answered (the line through Bun.spawn bytes and through an ended node:child_process pipe loaded the channel and left stdin destroyed, an empty stdin was refused), paused or not; without it the paused child never answered and was killed after 5 s (exit 137, and SIGKILL through the pipe). Node v22.23.4, Node children: the same with `resume()`; without it the paused child left its top-level await unsettled (exit 13).
   - Node was checked, as the critique asked (`/tmp/vector-v18c/node-probe/`, Node v22.23.4, the loader file run under Node's type stripping): with an early `isTTY` access and a 300 ms wait the new reader loads the channel and leaves stdin destroyed, a stdin closed with nothing on it is refused at once, and Node's own `for await` reads all 401 bytes of a pipe either way, so Node never had the bug.
   - The new reader loads the line both ways from a Bun parent (the same probe), and leaves stdin destroyed.
   - The regression proofs are CI-only, because they spawn Bun: the new `github.secrets-stdin.test.ts` (a fresh Bun process with an early `isTTY` access loads the line given as Bun.spawn bytes, which the old reader lost, from a stdin it paused first, and through an ended pipe, the sandbox's way, and closes stdin; an empty stdin is refused at once) and V18b's `github.agent-cli.test.ts`, unchanged. The probes ran copies of the loader from scratch folders with the default environment; the test in its committed form (the real `src/cli/cmd/github.secrets.ts`, run from `packages/engine`, so the bunfig preload applies, with a PATH-only environment) has never run, and its first run is the lane's engine step.
3. **The 2.9.2 retries live in a rule a Codium agent phase pins in `SessionRetry` (V18a item 4, V18b item 3's carry).** `SessionRetry` gains `GatewayRule` and `pinGateway`, in the style of `LLMRequestPrep.pin`, so `retry.ts` imports nothing from GitHub. `policy` asks the pinned rule first: "stop" ends the step with its error, "wait" retries while the rule's window is open, and undefined takes today's path unchanged. Nothing is pinned outside a Codium agent phase, so the desktop, `vector run` and own-key runs retry exactly as before; the phase pins its rule after its deadline checks and unpins it when the phase's promise settles.
   - `gatewayRetry(error, provider)` classifies only the model plans provider's answers: `MODEL_PLAN_CONCURRENCY` waits; `MODEL_PLAN_BUSY`, `MODEL_PLAN_RENEWING` and the default row (any other code, any 5xx) keep the client's retries (10 attempts within 15 minutes), which the deadline bounds; every other table code stops at once. So `MODEL_PLAN_CAPACITY` (402, which `parseAPICallError` marks retryable) and `MODEL_PLAN_MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` (503) are no longer retried in a Codium run, and the run-limit and daily codes (429) no longer wait up to 15 minutes before drafting.
   - **The concurrency window is the run's (critique; the plan's per-step window is rejected).** `processor.ts` builds `Effect.retry(SessionRetry.policy(...))` once per model step, so a window kept in the policy's closure would have restarted for every step, title request and subagent step. `gatewayRule(clock)` keeps the first refusal's time itself, on the phase's injectable clock: the window opens at the run's first concurrency refusal, and a later refusal after a recovered one keeps it, so all of a run's waits together stay within `CONCURRENCY_RETRY_MS`. The spec's Errors bullet says so. A "wait" retry is spaced by the usual delay, capped at `RETRY_MAX_DELAY_NO_HEADERS` (30 s) and at what is left of the window; the attempt and 15-minute caps do not apply to it, since the window replaces them.
   - **The spacing has a floor and a ceiling (review).** Because the window replaces the attempt cap, a gateway that answered concurrency with a zero or tiny `retry-after-ms` would have been polled in a tight loop for the whole 15 minutes. `GatewayRule` gains an optional `minDelayMs` (`RETRY_INITIAL_DELAY`, 2 s, when absent), and a "wait" retry waits `min(max(delay, minDelayMs), 30 s, what is left)`. The agent phase passes its clock's `minDelayMs` (`RETRY_INITIAL_DELAY` by default; the tests' `fakeClock` uses 1 ms). A `retry-after` longer than 30 s is shortened to 30 s by design: the spec's "polling at most every 30 s" asks for it, and a run's 15-minute window and its deadline matter more than the server's hint, so a refusing gateway is polled up to about 30 times in the window. The spec's Errors bullet now names both bounds.
   - A row that answered "wait" has waited out its window when it becomes the final error, so the phase records it as a draft with the busy reason.
4. **A failed heartbeat stops the run, as `stop: true` does: V15's behaviour, recorded, not changed.** `withRunHeartbeat` already stops on `!answer.ok || answer.stop`, and `heartbeat()` answers `{ok: false, stop: true}` on any failure, so one beat that times out after 5 seconds or fails drafts the run with stop `"deadline"`. The spec now says so, so the owner can revisit it (section 4 step 1); this package does not change it (critique). `withRunHeartbeat` gains an optional `intervalMs`, a test seam that defaults to `HEARTBEAT_SECONDS`.
5. **The deadline.** `VECTOR_RUN_DEADLINE` is epoch milliseconds (mint's `deadline`, spec 2.4 `dl`).
   - `runDeadline(value, now)` accepts a decimal integer with no leading zero (`/^[1-9]\d{0,15}$/`) that is a safe integer and lies no more than `TIMEOUT_MIN.codiumAgent` (135) minutes ahead. No real mint can give a later deadline, because the Codium agent job's own timeout ends it first (O8). The plan clamped the timer to 2,147,483,647 ms instead, which would have fired, and drafted, at about 24.8 days for any later deadline (critique); a deadline beyond the bound is now malformed, and no timer needs clamping.
   - On Codium a missing or malformed deadline fails closed: one line (`DEADLINE_MISSING`), exit 1, no catalog, model or GitHub request, no bundle, and settle `failed`. An unauthorized run token (the gate did not authorize it) is checked first: `GithubSecrets.CODIUM_UNAUTHORIZED` (the gate's own constant), exit 1, nothing to settle.
   - On own key a missing deadline sets no timer, and a well-formed one is honoured the same way (own key never mints, so in practice there is none).
   - **A stop time already passed stops the run at once, with no timer (critique).** The plan would have set a 0 ms timer, which fires a macrotask later, so whether the first model request was sent depended on whether `begin` and `gather` had awaited anything. Now the phase halts before `begin` when `deadline − STOP_MARGIN_SECONDS − now ≤ 0`: refusals still answer as refusals, and otherwise the bundle records `"deadline"` with no model request.
6. **How a stop reaches the session.** A private `haltSwitch` holds the run's one stop (the first of the local deadline and the heartbeat's stop wins) and runs what waits on it once, at once when the run had already stopped. After the session is created, the phase hooks `prompt.cancel(session.id)` to it. `chat` gains an optional `halted` check (a new optional `ChatContext` field; `githubRun` passes none, so v2 is unchanged) and sends no turn, the summary turn included, once the run has stopped. A stop that cuts only the title turn still marks the run as stopped (a draft), the more conservative outcome (INV-S17). A stop that lands after `chat`'s check and before the turn's loop registers finds nothing to cancel (review: that `prompt.prompt` loop would then have run on, possibly for many steps, bounded only by the gateway's `MODEL_PLAN_RUN_EXPIRED`). So the hook cancels at once and then again every `CANCEL_REPEAT_MS` (1 s, on the phase's clock seam) until the session's turns are over: the hook is registered right before `chat` and both stop after the title turn, and nothing between those points rejects (`chat`'s failure is handled, and `summarize` answers the fallback title on any failure). A cancel of a session with no running turn settles it idle, which is harmless. Of the two remedies the review offered, the repeat was taken: `chat` re-checking `halted()` once the prompt fiber has started would still race the loop's registration, which happens several steps into `prompt.prompt`. Tests assert the repeat is set at once and cleared when the turns end; the race itself has no deterministic in-process reproduction.
7. **Settle outcomes and signals.** `withRunHeartbeat` wraps the phase on Codium only (`VECTOR_MODE === "codium"`; the mode decides, not the token, INV-K1) and settles once with first-operation semantics: the bundle's outcome; `refused` for a refusal; `failed` for an exit 1 (a crash before the route, the deadline rule, a bundle that could not be written); `cancelled` on SIGTERM or SIGINT. **No bundle is written after a signal (critique).** `Effect.promise` only aborts its signal when the fiber is interrupted, and the async chain went on to `record()` and `writeBundle`, which runs plain fs and git. `record` now returns at once when the phase's signal is aborted, both on entry and right before `writeBundle`. The SIGTERM case runs in-process (item 12).
8. **The Codium instance and the removed refusal.** `instance` for `--phase agent` on Codium is true only when the loaded holder's run token was authorized by the gate, with no sharing; `index.ts` has already set `VECTOR_PURE=1` and `VECTOR_DISABLE_PROJECT_CONFIG=1`. The handler answers `AGENT_NEEDS_CHANNEL`, `GithubSecrets.CODIUM_UNAUTHORIZED` or `UNKNOWN_MODE` with exit 1 where the instance rule started none. The unauthorized line is one constant in `github.secrets.ts`, which the account gate in `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` (a shared file) and the agent phase both print (review: two copies of the literal could drift). `refuseCodiumAgent` and `CODIUM_NOT_YET` are removed. This removes deviation V18b item 3's departure (a Codium run refused and settled until V18c), so the orchestrator's acceptance that item asked for is moot.
9. **Ticket dispatches (V18b's carry list, not the brief; separable).** A Codium `workflow_dispatch` is admitted from a bot only when the sender's type is `Bot` and its login is exactly `<slug>[bot]`, where the slug is the `@<slug>` entry of `MENTIONS` and `GithubAgents.mentions(slug)` accepts it (the generator's own helper and slug pattern, the login compared as the route script compares it), the dispatch is in task mode, and `inputs.ticket` matches `TICKET` (the mint's pattern, now exported from `github.cloud.ts`).
   - **The type check is stricter than the route script (review).** INV-S2 names the App bot by `<slug>[bot]` and its user id; the route script admits the login alone, with or without a type. The phase now also requires `sender.type === "Bot"`, so a sender with the App's login and no type, or another type, is refused with `BOT_REFUSED` (the route row test expects that now). The bot's user id is not compared: nothing trusted gives it to the agent job yet (a generator-written `VECTOR_APP_BOT_ID` would; carried to V24, which writes the v3 workflow). GitHub logins cannot contain brackets, so no person can hold the login either way. Every other bot, and every bot on own key, is refused with `BOT_REFUSED`, as before. This check is defence in depth: mint step 8 verified the App bot and peeked the one-time ticket before any token existed, and the reservation consumed it (INV-S2). Such a route skips the writer re-check, because a bot has no collaborator permission, and reads nothing from GitHub. Its task is the new fixed `SERVICE_TASK` line, then `<service_request>` with `Prompt (untrusted): <untrustedJson(VECTOR_PROMPT)>`, so a `</service_request>` inside the prompt stays escaped (INV-S14's `startCloudRun` bullet); the untrusted-data rule stays in the system instructions only.
   - The critique asked for this trust-boundary change in a `feat(github)` commit of its own after the lifecycle commit, in the same push. The files and tests are separable for that (the ticket rows in `agentRoute`'s test, the ticket case of `github.agent-codium.test.ts`, `serviceDispatch`, `SERVICE_TASK` and the `ticket` branch of `gather`); this package leaves its changes uncommitted, so the committer makes the split.
   - **INV-S18 for ticket runs is V21's.** The bundle records no "server-started" flag: `RunSummary` is strict, and INV-S17 has publish take its routing from trusted sources only, never from the bundle. Publish must re-derive the fact from the event, as the route job does (a `Bot` sender `<slug>[bot]` with a ticket input), and then publish with `GITHUB_TOKEN`.
10. **P6 error lines on Codium.** A Codium run's error lines never carry a gateway message, which can name credits, a plan or a price (`gatewayFailure` appends "Buy credits at …"), into a public Actions log.
    - `stoppedLine(stop)` is the fixed line: "Vector stopped early." for the default row (critique: not "Vector stopped early: Vector stopped early."), otherwise "Vector stopped early: <2.9.2 reason line>.".
    - A run's own stop (deadline or heartbeat) logs its `stoppedLine` in either mode.
    - On Codium a turn's error (`chat` keeps the session's NamedError object as the thrown error's `cause`), or any error that `stopReason` maps, logs only its `stoppedLine`. Every other error keeps its own message, so a GitHub read, a git failure, a bundle failure, `agentModel`'s fixed lines and the context-overflow advice still say what went wrong (critique). Own key keeps its messages.
    - **The whole cause chain is checked (review).** A failure of `prompt.prompt` itself (outside `result.info.error`) could wrap a gateway message, or carry an API error further down, where the one-link turn check does not look. On Codium, `errorLine` now also logs the `stoppedLine` when any link of the cause chain (up to 16 deep) is a session `APIError` (instance or object), an `LLMError` from `@vectordevai/llm`, or the AI SDK's `APICallError`. The review asked for "an APIError, or one the model plans provider produced"; any LLM-layer or SDK call error is taken instead, because a Codium run's only model provider is the gateway (`agentModel`), and recognising the provider by module name or URL would let a wrapper that drops them through. No path found today reaches this (`agentModel` and the catalog use fixed messages); the test fakes one.
    - `ChatContext` gains an optional `reportError`; on Codium it is a no-op, so `chat`'s `console.error("Agent error:", err)` of the whole error object is not printed. `githubRun` passes none, so its output is byte-identical (2.11.5).
    - New copy for V29: `CODIUM_UNAUTHORIZED` (the gate's string), `DEADLINE_MISSING`, `SERVICE_TASK` and the two `stoppedLine` forms; none uses an INV-H1 word. `chat`'s internal "The run stopped before this turn." is never logged (a halted run logs its `stoppedLine`).
    - Not covered here, carried to V19: the Codium bundle body still uses `buildEvidenceBody`'s generic cost line, and Codium status notices are not yet suppressed under Actions.
11. **INV-C7 extends its named test (deviation V18a item 1).** "stop-code table, including an unknown code" in `github.bundle.test.ts` becomes an instance test: the pure rows first, unchanged, then a Codium dispatch for each of the 16 stop codes at its 2.9.2 status (`MODEL_PLAN_RUN_LIMIT` with `limit: "hours"`), `MODEL_PLAN_SOMETHING_NEW` (429) and a 500 with no body, each with a change made first and with none: 36 agent phases.
    - The fake (`codiumFakes()` in the fixture): `ModelPlans.Service` lists one model for the fixture run token and its `request` is the gateway; `FreeModels.CredentialsService` returns the run token for `vector`, as `app-node-builder-v1.ts` does in a Codium run. The gateway sends title requests and queued "forward" actions on to the instance's fake LLM server, and otherwise holds or refuses with the gateway's `{error: {code, message, limit?}}` and `retry-after-ms: 1`. Both services read their state at call time, and the provider layer keeps its list per instance, so each test sets the state before its instance's first provider read (critique), and both fakes return Effects.
    - Each row asserts the outcome (draft with changes, failed without), the recorded stop (the six unavailable codes as `MODEL_PLAN_BLOCKED`, none for the unknown code and the 500), the limit, the patch, the refused request count (1; `RETRY_MAX_ATTEMPTS + 1` for the busy rows, the unknown code and the 500; 3 for concurrency on a clock that moves 8 minutes a refusal), the one settle, the reason line, and that no line holds the gateway's words. Every `expect` carries the row's name (critique). The file ran in about 21 s locally, INV-C7's test about 17 s of it (progress entry).
    - `chat`'s suppressed `console.error` is not asserted: that would need a console mock, which AGENTS.md avoids. The phase's own lines are asserted.
12. **Tests and seams.**
    - `github.agent-codium.test.ts` (new, in-process; it spawns only git and the tools' commands, so it runs locally and in the lane): the heartbeat lifecycle from the gate's starting beat to the settle, the deadline abort (with and without changes, and a stop time already passed, with the cancel repeat set at once and cleared when the turns end), the heartbeat stop, the concurrency window (one step, a recovery, and two steps sharing one window, the critique's case), the deadline rules on Codium and on own key (INV-K1: no `/api/cloud-agents/*` request, no gateway request, no catalog read with the run token), the pure retry rules (now with the 2 s floor for a zero `retry-after-ms`, a rule's own `minDelayMs`, and a wait cut short by the window's end), the P6 cause-chain rows, the ticket dispatch, and SIGTERM.
    - The P6 rows (review) run the phase with the instance's `SessionPrompt.Service` replaced through `Effect.provideService` by one whose `prompt` dies with a gateway-worded error: a session API error two links down, the model plans provider's `LLMError`, and an AI SDK call error two links down. It is a fake service, not a module mock, and it stands in for a failure no real path is known to produce; every other service is the instance's own. Each row asserts exit 0, outcome `failed`, the default reason line, no line with the fixture's gateway words, one settle `failed` and no recorded stop.
    - The SIGTERM case emits SIGTERM to the test process while the gateway holds a request, as `github.lifecycle.test.ts` does in a child: the phase returns nothing, `process.exitCode` is 143, the only cloud request is settle `cancelled`, and once the phase's own work has ended (its deadline timer cleared) there is no `vector-out/`. It restores `process.exitCode` to its previous value or 0 (Bun keeps a code once set) and checks that no signal listener leaked. The critique offered a CI-only subprocess test or a deferral; the in-process case covers it and runs locally too.
    - Seams: `AgentPhaseInput.clock` (`now`, `timer`, `heartbeatMs`, `minDelayMs`), `GatewayRule.minDelayMs`, the exported `CANCEL_REPEAT_MS`, `withRunHeartbeat`'s `intervalMs`, `cloudRecorder`'s `heartbeat` answer, `holder(mode, authorized)`, `stepEnv`'s `deadline`, `fakeClock`, `resetTree`, `TICKET_FIXTURE` and `CODIUM_MODEL`.
    - Deferred to V30: a CLI-level Codium journey (`vector github run --phase agent` with a Codium channel) needs a fake-vectordev.ai seam, because `github.cloud.ts`'s `SITE` and the gate's heartbeat are fixed to `https://vectordev.ai`.
13. **Shared files.** `packages/engine/src/session/retry.ts` (now in 0.2's list, item 1), `packages/engine/src/cli/vector-account.ts` (the gate prints `GithubSecrets.CODIUM_UNAUTHORIZED`, item 8; one line, watched for PR #20's merge), `.github/workflows/vector-at-mention.yml` and `packages/cloud/src/workflow-compliance.test.ts`. The new `github*` test files match the engine step's `test/cli/github*.test.ts`. **The lane gains one step (review):** "Run engine session retry tests" in the `engine and core` job (`working-directory: packages/engine`, `if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS=false bun test --timeout 30000 test/session/retry.test.ts`), because `retry.ts` now decides every provider's retries with a new branch and test.yml has never run on this branch. The workflow's pull-request paths gain `retry.ts` and `test/session/retry.test.ts`; the policy's required paths gain both and its "the lane keeps every check the contract names" list gains `["packages/engine", "test/session/retry.test.ts"]`. No word the policy refuses is added. Each commit body must list the shared files it touches.
14. **Commits.** The plan was three commits: the spec clarification (item 1) alone; `fix(github)` for the loader (item 2), which carries this entry's heading with item 2, so the correction lands with its record (RULES.md and AGENTS.md allow `fix`, which the lane notes' list of titles omits; its body says it repairs V18b's CI-only `github.agent-cli.test.ts`, which failed in lane run `42455742210`); then the `feat(github)` lifecycle commit with the rest. Following the critique, the ticket admission (item 9) is a separate `feat(github)` commit after it, in the same push. The orchestrator asked for the changes to be left uncommitted, so the split is the committer's; the progress entry lists which file belongs to which commit.
15. **Review before the first commit.** A review of the uncommitted V18c found no blocking defect and ten non-blocking findings. All were taken, each recorded in its item: the concurrency wait's 2 s floor and the 30 s ceiling's record (item 3); the cancel repeat that closes the halt race (item 6); one `CODIUM_UNAUTHORIZED` constant shared with the gate (item 8); `stream.resume()` and a paused-stdin case (item 2); the `Bot` type check (item 9); the cause-chain walk and its test rows (items 10 and 12); the lane step for `test/session/retry.test.ts` (item 13); the progress entry's wording about which form of `github.secrets-stdin.test.ts` has run (item 2 and the progress entry); and the `fix(github)` title kept, with its body naming the repaired run (item 14). Two remedies were taken in a different form than suggested, with the reason in their items: the halt race is closed by repeating the cancel, not by `chat` re-checking after the fiber starts (item 6), and the cause-chain walk recognises any LLM-layer or SDK call error rather than only the model plans provider's (item 10). The ceiling finding offered a choice; the 30 s ceiling stays and is recorded (item 3).
16. **Two commits, not four (the commit gate).** The commit gate named one feature commit, `feat(github): V18c add Codium heartbeat, deadline and stop codes to the agent phase`, and the lane notes put a contract clarification first in a `docs(github)` commit of its own. So V18c went up as two commits in one push: the spec clarification alone, `2595ab4a` (item 1), then the feature commit with everything else, including the stdin loader repair (item 2) and the ticket admission (item 9), which items 9 and 14 planned as commits of their own. A split would have needed partial staging of files whose hunks the three parts share (`github.secrets.ts` holds both `streamChunks` and `CODIUM_UNAUTHORIZED`; `github.agent.ts`, `github.agent-phase.test.ts`, `github.agent-codium.test.ts` and the fixture interleave the ticket parts with the lifecycle), and it would have left intermediate trees that nothing typechecked or tested, while the lane runs only at the head (as in deviation V18b item 15). The intent is kept: the feature commit's body names the repaired lane run `42455742210` and the trust-boundary change of item 9, and item 9 still lists the ticket parts for review. The proof is unchanged: the exact-head lane run of the feature commit, recorded in V19's entry once seen.

### Resume integration with main (2026-11-09)

1. The owner's request to continue the newer agents' work is implemented in a fresh worktree, preserving the old unfinished local checkout. The published feature branch remains the source of truth; no old recovery patch is applied over it.
2. Main now owns the CLI command gate and separate per-loop concurrency wait accounting. The merge keeps both, adding the secret-channel setup before account verification and the GitHub gateway policy before the ordinary retry policy. Pinned waits are excluded from later ordinary failure attempts just like main's concurrency waits. This prevents an intermittent provider failure after a busy period from exhausting a budget it never used.
3. The merge is a distinct checkpoint before the V18c credential-output repair and V19. It carries no change to workflow callers, service switches, account settings or publication state. Main's newer Codium evidence and review changes remain intact.

### V18c output-boundary credential repair (2026-11-10)

1. The V18c CLI assertion is retained. The failure came from printing synthetic credential literals in tool input after environment isolation had succeeded. Redaction therefore belongs to the in-memory holder and public output boundaries, not to raw messages or patches. Literal and JSON-escaped values are covered; this is not a claim to detect arbitrary encodings or transformations.
2. The session event and attachment helpers gain optional output callbacks so the agent uses its private-holder filter while existing callers retain their default logging behavior. The runtime regression covers both attachment diagnostics through a URL rejected before download and proves raw history remains intact.
3. V19 and V21 may be drafted in isolated temporary files during the merge/repair checks, as coordinated by the parent agent. They are not accepted packages or included in this checkpoint; review, tests, commits and integration remain ordered. This keeps the shared checkout stable while independent implementation preparation proceeds.

### V19 evidence provenance and Actions output (2026-11-10)

1. The parent approved an evidence-specific correction to label-plan §3.8: portable assistant messages retain Codium attribution but do not retain free-model versus OAuth-plan cost provenance. Mixed evidence therefore says "from other models", omits a zero subtotal, and never infers "on your keys", "free" or a plan label from current credentials. Positive measured non-Codium cost remains visible. Durable review-pass cost kinds continue to support their existing labels; historical messages are not rewritten.
2. `codiumTurns` counts measured non-forked Codium turns, including the existing stamped-step override. Non-Codium totals still require positive provider identity. Unknown and missing usage continue to use main's conservative labels.
3. The Actions status no-op applies before the published CLI's account gate as well as before the status handler's Auth read. Both `codium` and its `credits` alias are covered. Owner/workflow and local policy checks keep their existing order. Real-entrypoint regressions remain CI-only; the local checks exercise the actual yargs command gate and status Effect without nested CLI processes.
4. Untrusted log prose can be interpreted as an Actions command. The runner's legacy parser searches for `##[` anywhere in a line, so a prefix alone is insufficient. The shared output helper breaks every legacy opener, normalizes CR/CRLF/LF, and prefixes every line containing the V2 `::` delimiter. Fixed status lines keep their text. The agent applies this after credential redaction at log/error boundaries; raw history and bundle/patch data are not edited by the log helper. V21 will reuse it for publisher logs. Source reference: <https://github.com/actions/runner/blob/main/src/Runner.Common/ActionCommand.cs>.
5. The accepted main fixture repair `00b50f72b` is cherry-picked as `3d027edae` before this package. Its unchanged focused cases are not rerun here; the parent recorded all 21 regular CI jobs and typecheck passing on PR #23.

### Exact-entrypoint foreign token output follow-up (2026-11-10)

1. The c65 exact CLI failure was the unheld synthetic run-token literal inside its own-key model command. Redaction now covers complete canonical Vector token literals in public text even when they are not this run's credentials. Ordinary `vrt_` prose, incomplete token forms and values outside the canonical length bounds are not treated as tokens. This does not claim to detect arbitrary encodings or unknown providers' credentials.
2. Independent review caught both overlap directions: structured-first replacement can expose the remainder of a full held credential containing a token; held-first replacement can damage a foreign token's grammar. The final implementation unions match ranges from the original text and replaces them once. Tests pin both cases and verify the holder's original credentials remain intact.

### Main integration entitlement and platform fixtures (2026-11-10)

1. Main's snapshot migration invalidated the old broad fake account response. The repair uses existing snapshot and ledger fixture builders with exact keys, retaining the production persistence requirements. A separate current-access guard fixes a real admission gap while this temporary local backing remains in use; A59 still owns replacing it with the Codium allowance seam.
2. The v3 contract requires Ubuntu for all three sandbox jobs, and the implementation explicitly refuses non-Linux lifecycle execution. Successful private-directory tests therefore cannot assert POSIX permissions on Windows. Six such cases skip Windows; explicit refusal cases exercise the unsupported platform instead. No production permission check is weakened, and local Linux results do not claim native Windows coverage.
3. Four conceptual commits retain reviewable separation of fixture repair, current access, foreign-token output and platform fixtures. Focused checks cover the final combined tree; there is no assertion that each partially staged intermediate commit was separately executed.

## 2026-11-12 V20 (Codium review lifecycle)

1. The Codium-only lifecycle is a separate `github.review-codium.ts` module so the existing review job can retain its own-key entry path. The private file reader and starting account heartbeat were already wired before command dispatch by V15; V20 consumes that authorized holder instead of reading or authorizing the token twice.
2. The read-only review runner gains an optional generic abort signal, with admission checks and scoped cancellation of its own sessions. This changes no default reviewer policy or core outcome vocabulary. Public error prose uses the closed stop table and an output-only P6 filter; it does not mutate raw session data.

## 2026-11-12 V13 integration (prepare before Everywhere route commitment)

1. Everywhere must atomically win its laptop/Actions routing claim before dispatch. Returning a ticket hash only after `startCloudRun` has dispatched allows the laptop to claim concurrently. The additive `prepareCloudRun` port creates the existing private ticket first and retains raw ticket/prompt only in server memory; it exposes a hash, a single-operation dispatch closure and a pre-dispatch cancellation closure. The existing function keeps its public result shape by composing both steps. No reserved source is enabled.
2. Dispatch repeats current authorization after preparation rather than treating preparation as continuing authority. Its read-only store script fences revocation, binding generation/suspension and exact ticket contents. Mint continues to repeat the existing checks before granting model authority. Persistent errors fail closed and grant no fallback route.

## 2026-11-12 V27a (authenticated artifact key)

1. Key derivation is isolated in `api/_lib/cloud-agents-artifact.ts`, while the existing API wrapper owns token verification, strict request shape and persistent rate limits. The 16-hex rotation identifier is the SHA-256 prefix of the artifact master, not account or repository detail. No session bytes or new persistent data are stored by the key endpoint.
2. This checkpoint implements only the server key and its fixtures. Privacy copy remains unchanged until the actual authenticated zip/save/restore path is validated. B08's fixed `brain/bundle.json` hook does not itself define an initial desktop upload transport; no implicit upload or production activation is introduced.

## 2026-11-12 V21 (trusted publishing)

1. Publishing has its own narrow Git/API adapters rather than calling the legacy helper's stage-all push functions. Only the validated index can be committed. The existing `createPR` helper gains an optional draft flag without changing old callers; engine App authentication adds the backend's already-defined exact `publish` profile. No GitHub App permission is widened.
2. Binary literal and delta streams are decoded with bounded Node zlib output and delta-result/copy bounds before `git apply`. A declared compressed-patch size alone does not bound its expanded result. A second index audit validates actual blob sizes and link targets after applying. Windows absolute/backslash links are refused on every platform, and Windows aliases of `.github` are held back consistently.
3. A contradictory untrusted summary that claims ready changes while recording a stop is normalized toward a draft before the remaining strict schema checks. It can never be repaired toward a ready result. Existing bot-owned PRs receive the new run marker/evidence; a ready PR is converted to a draft whenever the conservative result requires it. Human/default/fork/moved heads never grant an overwrite target.
4. Public model text uses escaped HTML/entities and safe mention/reference handling before trusted close/marker/CI text is appended. Inline comments, bot-ticket dispatches and non-writer thread content keep `GITHUB_TOKEN` even under App opt-in. A bounded/incomplete thread scan cannot enable App publishing. Actual hosted CLI entrypoint coverage remains a separate gate; local fake-API/real-Git coverage is not labeled as a production run.

## 2026-11-12 A59 (canonical allowance integration)

1. The parent authorized transferring Codium's isolated canonical allowance commit before wiring the existing @vector seam and static registrations. No limits table is copied into the integration. The old local-backing test is deleted exactly as A59 specifies; canonical allowance, real caller/gateway/status boundaries and the existing CI-only mint fixtures cover their respective contracts.
2. Static production `vrt_` registration makes the older global fake-prefix fixture invalid. The integration fixture now mints a genuine signed run credential and answers only its exact read-only binding/live/JTI check; independently scoped malformed-verifier tests remain unchanged. This tests the actual registered service domain without weakening duplicate-registration refusal.
3. The status meter uses a portable fetch wrapper with the caller's abort signal. Bun-only `fetch.preconnect` is not referenced by hosted Node code. The ledger's settled-seconds Lua response is a decimal string; the fake was corrected to that actual contract rather than broadening the production decoder.

## 2026-11-12 V27 framing correction (before codec implementation)

1. The original framing required the prior run's `rid` in AES-GCM additional authenticated data, but stored it only inside the ciphertext. A follow-up could not authenticate or decrypt that file without already knowing the hidden value. The parent approved the intent-preserving correction before codec source: a strict, length-bounded clear routing header carries `keyId`, repository id, thread, prior `rid` and IV; those values remain authenticated by GCM and must match the current token-authorized repository/thread/key. No account id, code, context or credential is cleartext.
2. The full encrypted file, manifest, fixed zip entry names/count, declared expanded sizes and actual streamed expanded bytes are bounded independently. Header/schema/context validation precedes authentication, and zip/manifest parsing occurs only after authentication succeeds. A malformed, foreign, rebound, oversized or unavailable artifact falls back to the live GitHub thread. The framing change itself does not activate runtime restoration or change privacy copy.

## 2026-11-12 V22 (configured schedules)

1. Twenty individually permitted long prompts can exceed Linux's per-environment-variable limit. The workflow builder now caps the combined escaped schedule map at 120,000 UTF-8 bytes, before writing a workflow that could never launch. Calendar validation also rejects impossible numeric fields, reversed ranges and zero steps. The shared schedule id parser rejects a trailing newline rather than relying on JavaScript's end anchor alone.
2. A branch prefix alone is ambiguous when stable ids themselves contain hyphens or digits. Reuse requires the exact generated numeric run/attempt suffix after the schedule id. Older or ambiguous manually named heads receive a fresh proposal instead of granting an uncertain overwrite target.
3. Scheduled runs work from the current default branch. Refreshing an existing proposal therefore writes the independently validated current tree with both its previous proposal and the current default as commit parents. This preserves history and permits a normal fast-forward push without running repository merge tools or carrying stale generated files into every later run. A head that advances during the operation is preserved, and the new result goes to a separate draft.

## 2026-11-12 B08/V27 authority clarification (before owner-key API)

1. The live-run artifact-key grant cannot authorize a desktop read after settlement. The parent approved a separate marked-desktop account-owner key contract, bound to a private persisted run's exact repository/generation/thread and actual GitHub artifact metadata. The server reads metadata only; download, authenticated decryption and proposal-only import are explicit owner actions on the trusted desktop. It accepts no arbitrary thread/repository/URL choice and grants no model authority. The spec records endpoint types, retention and the limit that revocation cannot retract an already downloaded key/plaintext before API implementation.
2. Initial desktop approval does not silently authorize Brain-derived history in a later repository-triggered run. Full-session restoration falls back to the live thread unless current owner-desktop approval covers the exact same plaintext digest, repository, audience and current visibility. Different newly approved bytes cannot recover older derived private context. This restricts restoration of Brain-bearing conversations without guessing which assistant text was derived from memory.
3. Initial approved context and fresh model proposals use separate fixed manifest paths and authority metadata. The server stores only bounded encrypted upload bytes/key wraps, never payload plaintext; the first run performs digest/schema/public-policy checks locally. Runtime source/allowance/listing gates apply at server entry points as well as UI visibility. All new ports remain explicitly off until integrated acceptance.
4. A restore predicate alone cannot restrict a key the generic live-run endpoint already grants. Brain-bearing sessions therefore receive a distinct artifact-master HMAC domain covering exact owner-approved digest, audience and visibility. Only exact live desktop upload authority or the separate verified owner result-read authority can obtain it; ordinary repository runs receive the ordinary conversation key and cannot decrypt Brain history. Private immutable digest/visibility metadata supports post-settlement reads without storing or inspecting source content.

## 2026-11-12 V28 (shared repository webhook handler)

1. The handler exports the exact shared structural event/handler types locally so this lane remains independently buildable before Codium's router is merged. It creates no route and does not duplicate signature/delivery deduplication. Final integration must register this handler alongside the existing Codium handler.
2. Replay-safe no-ops are applied before writes when the current owner/name/suspension already reflects the delivery. The existing binding module remains the authority for generation-fenced suspension and stale-read races; pure handler fixtures do not substitute for its CI-only real-store coverage.

## 2026-11-12 B08/V27 server authority checkpoint

1. The initial upload/start/live reader and completed owner-data reader are separate authorities. Initial sharing requires active canonical source/Brain listing gates; exporting an already completed own result is not revoked merely by exhausted model allowance. Both remain behind the explicit Brain activation switch, positive marked desktop authentication, revocation and current binding/repository authorization.
2. Current GitHub visibility and fresh binder permission come from narrowly scoped, revoked App metadata credentials. Bind-time visibility or a warm permission cache cannot authorize private procedures after a repository/access change. Completed-result metadata uses Actions/Metadata read only and never fetches a payload. The desktop receives the current visibility for its local schema/preview/import policy while the key domain uses the actual stored initial context metadata.
3. New private thread and immutable Brain digest/visibility fields are added to the stored-data contract before runtime activation. Only exact live desktop ticket provenance can write them; public listings omit them. The ordinary artifact key stays unchanged for existing non-Brain conversations. Owner body input contains only run/artifact ids and cannot choose a thread, account, generation, digest or URL.
4. The transferred upload scripts originally accessed index members as undeclared dynamic Redis keys. The integration takes a bounded index snapshot, declares every referenced key, compares the atomic membership before writing and erases only after account revocation. Runtime requests preserve opaque payload storage and never add server plaintext inspection. Actual Redis behavior remains a hosted-CI gate.

## 2026-11-12 V23 (CLI v3 setup)

1. Prompt orchestration is isolated behind typed ports rather than making tests execute interactive browser or project/provider I/O. The real command supplies those ports from existing services. Repeated schedule flags use strict JSON objects so task punctuation and multiline text remain data. Public configuration has a bounded unauthenticated reader and fails closed.
2. The fixed Codium workflow profile deliberately keeps automatic reviews and public sharing off, matching the existing v3 generator's enforced policy. App publishing remains a separate explicit choice from Codium model access, preserving the earlier installer's opt-in ability without granting it by default. The catalog's environment names remain readonly; no type cast weakens that contract.

## V16 process-isolation continuation (source checkpoint)

1. A source audit found that the hosted launcher switched the entire engine to the agent UID, while the actual ShellTool still started ordinary same-UID children in the host PID namespace. Its optional shell sandbox defaulted off. The earlier generic scan did not test a real ShellTool command or Node's SIGUSR1 inspector activation. A separate coordinator control demonstrated that Node 24 opens its inspector on SIGUSR1, and that its signal-disable option is runtime-specific.
2. Published Actions CLIs are compiled Bun executables (`packages/engine/script/build.ts`); the npm Node wrapper forwards to that native binary (`bin/vector-native.cjs`). The new primary boundary is therefore a compulsory tool PID namespace and private proc mount, independent of the engine runtime. The filesystem keeps existing Unix permissions. No Linux kernel setting is weakened to obtain availability; unsupported support refuses the run before it is considered prepared.
3. The launcher derives the new non-secret namespace marker from verified preparation state, never from incoming environment text. Private holders capture the result once; Codium cannot downgrade it through an absent marker. Self-hosted own-key and the explicitly consented own-key sudo mode retain their documented exception. The private channel's JSON format is unchanged.
4. A shared Core process floor is enabled only by the actual private credential singleton, never by portable tests constructing an independent holder. It applies at the common Effect spawner, Engine utility launcher, MCP stdio factory, untrusted Git, bundle Git, OCR and PTY launch factories. ShellTool foreground/background commands therefore use the same lower boundary as formatter and session-shell children. The ordinary optional shell sandbox cannot replace this floor with a permission ask. Inspector/debug launch options and an already active Node inspector are refused before channel capture; no unsupported Node flag is passed to Bun.
5. The credential engine also gets a private mount namespace with an empty, read-only `/proc`. Its fixed root bridge retains only UID/GID switching and DAC-override capabilities until the existing setpriv step drops to the agent UID and sets no-new-privileges. Actual tools get their separate PID namespace and a real private proc mount. This matters because builtin filesystem tools otherwise run inside the credential process and could open its own descriptors. Their non-bypassable path guard refuses proc/sys/FD paths and chained aliases before permission asks; the kernel mount also prevents a concurrent symlink swap from reaching engine proc. Unsupported namespace/runtime behavior refuses the run.
6. The disposable CI probe packages actual ShellTool, Read/Glob/Grep, MCP stdio, LSP and shared spawn paths with unrelated instance bootstrap omitted and an empty embedded model catalog. It uses same-UID disposable Node memory, private IPC and fixed local commands, with a strict network guard and synthetic credentials only. It checks PID visibility, SIGUSR1 refusal across those launch paths, a working inspector positive control, refusal of the active inspector, hidden engine descriptors and actual file-tool denials before an always-allow permission callback. Ordinary repository Read is the positive control. The runner independently observes detached-process identity before asserting UID cleanup. No owner device, live account, model inference or paid provider is part of the probe; local privileged execution remains outside this package's resource rules.
7. Session output provenance uses a fixed private FD3 into a runner-owned mode-0600 file. Since sudo and bubblewrap close extra descriptors, a constant root shell bridge opens that descriptor after both and before the existing prlimit/setpriv/env launch; no shell program is built from input and no model path or hash is accepted. The native npm wrapper forwards it only in this trusted Codium context. The trusted encryption writer can attest one exact byte buffer; after UID shutdown, a missing or mismatched proof refuses any present session before upload. This is provenance of the trusted writer, not proof based on an outer ciphertext header. The disposable matrix adds a model-replaced session refusal and a Bun caller alongside the Node caller; the actual encryption codec remains V27's separate test.
8. Independent review identified a native-loader prerequisite: an unsafe loader environment could execute a constructor in the wrapper before it enters a PID namespace. Existing WorkspaceTrust validation already rejects repository `LD_*` configuration, so this finding is not recorded as a confirmed reachable production exploit. The shared launch API additionally refuses native loader controls against the actual merged child environment before starting bubblewrap, including direct utility/LSP/MCP/Effect callers; ordinary environment values remain unchanged. The disposable C constructor control signals only a disposable Node target, then actual launchers must reject that environment without executing the constructor. Its positive control and ordinary-variable control prevent an inert fixture from passing.
9. Initial portable sandbox tests and Core typecheck passed. The reviewed workflow snapshot changed only the three expected hosted installation steps. Actual-fixture typing errors were corrected without changing its assertions. The broadened shared floor, private engine proc mount, native-loader backstop and expanded actual-tool fixture still need final package typechecks, focused tests and hosted CI acceptance. No new security acceptance or release readiness is claimed until those concrete checks pass.

## Combined desktop integration review (2026-11-12)

- Observed external GitHub credential changes advance Main's monotonic authority epoch. Returning to the previous account does not revive an earlier workflow, run or unbind approval; pending and uncertain operation fences remain in force. The controller's 20 focused tests include GitHub-only A → B → A transitions and a delayed read.
- Returned Brain previews distinguish current repository visibility from conservative artifact screening. A historically public run remains publicly screened when its repository becomes private, while the current visibility still fences preview and import authority. The nine Main artifact tests include authenticated ciphertext, private-only proposal refusal and a separate exact-digest import.
- The combined package typechecks, focused integration tests, browser-safe codec builds and explicit import-only Node smoke passed locally. The dedicated hosted browser lane runs the existing GitHub agents regression file with one worker and zero retries. New binding Lua behavior, actual browser behavior and the native sandbox matrix require their hosted results; local checks do not qualify those gates. Native isolation and trusted launcher provenance remain unresolved, so runtime activation and release remain held.

## 2026-11-14 V16 bubblewrap floor held (branch `v2-vector`)

1. **What failed.** After the V16 process-isolation continuation merged into `build-integrate`, every `sandbox (...)` lane job refused at "Prepare the agent sandbox" (runs 43216635783 and 43565027861; last green at `c0d10fb85`, run 43178097816). There are two separate causes:
   - C1: GitHub's `ubuntu-24.04` image sets `kernel.apparmor_restrict_unprivileged_userns=1`. An unconfined, unprivileged `bwrap --unshare-user` cannot write its uid map. `build-isolation` run 43178633851 shows "bwrap: setting up uid map: Permission denied"; run 43182119477 shows the sysctl and that the same probe passes once root enters a Vector-only AppArmor label.
   - C2: with that label (run 43202763321), preparation passed, but the first real ShellTool call failed. Every tool launch nests `bwrap` under the engine's empty, read-only `/proc`, and a nested `bwrap` needs a real procfs ("bwrap: Can't read /proc/sys/kernel/overflowuid").
     So the floor as built (item 5 of the V16 continuation entry) cannot run on a hosted runner. No kernel setting is weakened to make it run.
2. **What is built instead.** One build-time selection, `ProcessIsolation.FLOOR`, is `false` in this build. There is no runtime, environment or configuration switch, and nothing lowers it. With the floor off:
   - `vector github sandbox prepare` skips both namespace probes and records no `isolatedTools`. `vector github sandbox run` no longer requires that field, starts the engine without its mount namespace, and names the tool boundary `guard` instead of `pid` in `VECTOR_GITHUB_TOOL_PID_NAMESPACE`. A credential holder accepts only the value its own build produces, so a `pid` claim from a floor-off launcher is refused. The npm wrapper forwards FD3 for either value.
   - `ProcessIsolation.launch` starts tools directly on Linux. Off Linux, a hosted launch is still refused.
   - Every hosted Codium and default own-key run still activates the kernel path guard: Read, Glob, Grep and the external-directory check refuse `/proc`, `/sys` and descriptor paths, and symlink aliases to them, before any permission ask. The native loader refusal still applies to every shared launcher, and the optional shell sandbox still offers no unsandboxed fallback.
   - Unchanged: the separate agent UID with no sudo or Docker, `ptrace_scope` 2, the hard zero core limit and `/dev/null` core pattern, no-new-privileges, the file-command checks, the private stdin channel, the FD3 session provenance and the UID-wide cleanup.
   - A floor build keeps item 5 unchanged: both probes, the engine's empty `/proc` and per-tool namespaces. A failed probe now names itself with bounded, control-free stderr from the fixed `/bin/true` probe only (ported from `build-isolation` `af4efb44d`), instead of the generic refusal.
3. **What this costs.** Tools run in the engine's PID namespace under its UID. A tool can see the engine's process entries, signal it and read its `environ`, which holds only the allowlisted environment and no credential. It cannot read the engine's memory (`ptrace_scope` 2) or the runner's processes. A Node engine opens its inspector on SIGUSR1 (continuation item 1). The published CLI is a compiled Bun executable, and an inspector that is already active is still refused before channel capture, but nothing refuses one opened later. The FD3 proof still refuses a session file replaced after the trusted write, but no namespace keeps a same-UID tool away from the engine's descriptors. INV-S5's tool PID namespace sentence is therefore not met by this build. This is acceptable only because hosted activation stays off (no App registration, Codium's `cloudAgents` switch off, `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` off) and native isolation (`build-isolation`, `build-native-shell`) is the ship gate.
4. **The CI probe.** Its report records `floor`. PID invisibility, signal refusal and the hidden engine proc are asserted only for a floor build, and the runner checks the report's floor against its own build. Without the floor, the tool probe tests with signal 0, so the disposable target's inspector stays closed. Every other assertion is unchanged.
5. **Affects:** native isolation, which replaces this selection; spec INV-S5; owner steps 6 and 8 (hosted Codium @vector stays off until native isolation qualifies).
6. **Checked against native-isolation-status.md after the main merge (BT13 item 6; 21 Nov 2026, v2-closeout-ci).** `ProcessIsolation.FLOOR` is still `false` (`packages/core/src/process-isolation.ts:26`) and the native build switch is still `false` (`packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.isolation-build.ts:2`), consistent with this entry. A third possible floor blocker: native run 43697711119 (job 132761821581 at `ce5e5605`, "Shell seam (bun, own-key, baseline)", failure; read through GitHub MCP) recorded `probe-empty-abort-own-zero` (native-isolation-status.md), so the engine's runtime aborts under `engineArguments`' empty read-only `/proc`, which a floor build also uses. That is an inference from the probe, not tested for the floor, so a floor build cannot keep item 5 unchanged on a Bun engine either until the `/proc` decision the status doc assigns is made. The ship gate is unchanged.

## 2026-11-14 BT01 (Declare the brain_upload dispatch input)

1. **Dispatch input.** Section 2.11.2 declared `mode`, `prompt`, `pr`, `code` and `ticket` for both modes. The B08 owner start (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-desktop.ts:181` → `cloud-agents-start.ts:198` → `github-app.ts:725`) also sends `brain_upload`, and GitHub refuses a `workflow_dispatch` that carries an undeclared input with 422. Every desktop Brain start would therefore have failed at GitHub. Built: `buildAgentsWorkflow` declares `brain_upload` (`type: string`, `required: false`) for Codium only. Own key is byte-for-byte unchanged (its golden snapshot did not move). Its route refuses every bot dispatch (the route script answers `none` to a bot unless the file is Codium with a handle and a ticket), so the App never reaches it and INV-K1 holds.
2. **Env only, agent step only.** The Codium `agent` step gets `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD: ${{ inputs.brain_upload }}` through `env:` (INV-S15; the expression joins the allowlist). The value is empty on comment, issue and schedule runs and on any dispatch that does not set it. Mint, route, sandbox prepare and finish, publish, bind and review never see it, and neither do the job-level env, the options header or any `run:` line. `WORKFLOW_VERSION` stays `"3"`; the route script and mint's gate are unchanged.
3. **Sandbox allowlist.** `SANDBOX_ENV_ALLOW` gains `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD`, so the value survives the launcher. It is not a credential:
   - it is 24 random bytes (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-brain.ts:225`), not derived from the Brain content;
   - `brain-download` also needs the live `vrt_` run token from the secret channel, exact live desktop ticket provenance, `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` and the listing gates;
   - it is visible: GitHub prints a step's non-secret env at the top of the step log, and the run's event payload carries the dispatch inputs, so on a public repository anyone can read it (as with `VECTOR_PROMPT`). That is safe only because of the two points above;
