It stays, described as covering that early return, and a new row lands the deadline from the catalog read during `provider.list()` so the read's own pre-check is what stops it (mutation M02).
    - Mutation expectations were restated and their actual results recorded in the progress entry: dropping `artifactKey`'s client `brain-run` gate alone is caught by `github.cloud.test.ts`, not by the agent-phase cases (the save's env gate still holds); dropping the local `UPLOAD` test is caught (a malformed id then logs the neutral line).
    - The interim rule's R2 correction is item 7.
    - test.yml: no run exists on `brain-transport` (checked 2026-11-16), so it is due after this package's push.
14. **Known limit: the model can quote the context into public output (review's minor).** The runner never logs the block, but a real model may repeat memory entries in its reply, which the job prints and the bundle posts as its comment and body; on a public repository that output is public. `record()`'s `SessionPosting.headless` screens against the runner's own Brain, which deliberately does not hold the context, so the desktop's posting check when it packs the bundle (`CloudBrain.preview` in `packages/engine/src/brain/run-bundle.ts`, through `BrainRunBundle.pack`'s `screen`) is the only screen of the approved text. The runner does not re-screen output against the bundle. Seeding the bundle into the runner's Brain to get that screen is not an option (item 5). The code review suggested screening the reply and title against the opened bundle's entry texts before posting (for example `CloudBrain.preview`'s screen over `Result.brain`), at least on public repositories. Not done here: it needs a decision on what a match does (withhold, redact or fail the post), and the screen would load core's Brain graph into `record()`. It stays a recorded known limit, for the lead or BT07b, to be confirmed at owner step 8.
    - **Follow-up on 24 November 2026.** The attached context is a synthetic part of the same task message, so the automatic title helper reads only the task text. When either visibility source says public and a run sees an attachment with screened content, the runner checks the title, result body and comment together against the same memory fields and insight text screened during attachment approval. The matcher checks literal overlap. A matching field gets fixed neutral text or a fallback title. The public Actions log reports the screened final reply and omits incremental model text and tool details. Both checks use the desktop fixture's sealed bundle and a fake model transport.
15. **Code review of the built package (2026-11-16): what changed and what did not.** No finding was blocking.
    - The rule and the label disagreed: fixed in the system layer (item 5); spec 2.17 amended.
    - `Result.brain` is lost when `work()` throws after a read (raised twice): left to BT07b with the test it names (item 1).
    - `const context` used once: inlined into `readBrain`'s block.
    - `ensureTitle` sends the start of the block to the title helper: no change; BT07b's options recorded (item 5).
    - Gate the ordinary-key save on `!start.route.ticket`: declined, with the reason (item 7); the save and restore gates' comments now give their real reasons.
    - Re-screen the reply against the bundle: left as the known limit, for owner step 8 (item 14).
    - The fixture's static `BrainUpload` import: now dynamic (item 12).
16. **Not changed:** `SERVICE_TASK`, the server, the workflow, CLI help, the HttpApi (no `bun run generate`), the egress inventory (the request goes through `github.cloud.ts`, already listed; `github.agent.ts` adds no URL) and every switch: `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` stays unset, so brain-download answers 503 and the run continues as a plain ticket dispatch with one neutral line.
17. **Affects:**
    - BT07b: read `Result.brain` and carry it through item 1's failure path, replace the env save gate with a Brain-key save of the history and `brain/proposals.json` (keeping the ordinary-key save's ask on a plain ticket dispatch, item 7), and restore under the Brain key with the approval.
    - BT08b and BT09: journeys over the real `brain-download` handler and this reader.
    - Owner step 8: a live desktop Brain run should log "Vector added the Brain context approved in the Vector app to this task."; with the switch off it logs the neutral line.
    - Spec INV-S14 (last bullet), 2.13 (a new "Brain context" paragraph), 2.17 (`UNTRUSTED_DATA_RULE`, item 5) and V27 (Files, "B08 source authority"), amended inline; `codium-track-b-checkpoint.md` B08 row; both v2 plans.

## 2026-11-16 BT07b (B08 returned proposals and Brain-history encryption)

1. **The layer check: the agent phase has the Brain services.** `vector github run --phase agent` runs `githubAgentPhase` (`github.ts:138-155`) under `AppRuntime` (`effect-cmd.ts:76-97`), whose layer provides `Brain.node`, `BrainKnowledge.node`, `BrainSkill.node`, `BrainWorkflow.node` and `Instruction.node` (`app-runtime.ts:114-122`), with `Session.node` and `Git.node`. `CloudBrain.node` itself is only in the HttpApi server (`server/routes/instance/httpapi/server.ts:315`), but the `Effect.fn` `CloudBrain.proposals` (`packages/engine/src/brain/run-bundle.ts:68`) needs only Instruction, Brain and BrainSkill, plus `SessionPosting.check`'s Instruction, Session and Git. So the package's first condition holds, and no Brain service is added to the GitHub layer.
2. **No `brain/proposals.json` (the package's second condition).** The agent cannot create "proposed" entries under the GitHub run's policy, so `CloudBrain.proposals` would always pack nothing. This package writes no proposals file, does not call `CloudBrain.proposals`, and records the gap here, as the package text says. The production routes to no proposal, each sufficient on its own:
   - **Taint.** Every task message goes in as `taskPart`, marked `BrainSchema.OUTSIDE: "github"` (`github.run-helpers.ts:590`). `SessionMemory.taint` reads such a text part as outside content and answers `"web"` (`session/memory.ts:920-941`), so `writeHold` always holds the session's memory writes (`memory.ts:245-266`). The memory tool passes the hold to `Brain.propose` (`tool/memory.ts:229-237`, `:341-357`), which turns any hold into status `"quarantined"`, never `"proposed"` (`core/src/brain/brain.ts:1689-1696`, `:1816`).
   - **The agent marker.** On the real CLI path (`vector github sandbox run -- vector github run`), a child that carries `VECTOR_AGENT_SESSION` gets hold `"subagent"` before the taint is read (`memory.ts:250-252`), with the same result.
   - **Pure mode and repository settings.** `index.ts:146-153` sets `VECTOR_PURE` for every Codium agent phase, so trust is false (`config/config.ts:2035`) and a repository with its own settings makes `place.held` true (`session/instruction.ts:266`). Then the memory tool holds as `"untrusted-repository"`, and `CloudBrain.proposals` returns `undefined` before it lists anything (`run-bundle.ts:76`).
   - The skill learner returns under pure mode (`session/skill-learn.ts:106`), and the prompt learner writes personal scope only (`session/prompt.ts:3644-3650`). `CloudBrain.proposals` and `BrainRunProposals.select` (`core/src/brain/run-proposals.ts:27-33`) keep only `"proposed"` repository records, so neither feeds them.
   - Test H7 shows it in the real agent phase: the fake model's `memory` `add` of a repository lesson in a desktop Brain run is stored `"quarantined"` with hold `web`, the repository has no `"proposed"` entry, and the saved file lists only `session.json` and `brain/bundle.json`. The hold is pinned (review round, item 14): the fixture repository's memory is not held (`place.held` is false, asserted) and the test process carries no agent marker, so the task part's `github` mark decides it, and a change in which route holds the write fails by name. On the real CLI path the agent marker holds it first, as `subagent` (and in a repository with its own settings pure mode holds it as `untrusted-repository`), with the same result.
   - **What it would take:** a contract change, for the lead and the Codium lane, in `core/src/brain/run-proposals.ts` and `engine/src/brain/run-bundle.ts` (for example, collect the run's quarantined repository drafts as proposals, since the desktop stages them as unapproved proposals anyway through `BrainRunProposals.stage`), or a GitHub-run hold policy that lets a desktop Brain run's memory writes wait as `"proposed"`. Neither is this package's to decide.
   - **Effect:** the desktop's returned-proposals preview refuses an artifact without `brain/proposals.json` ("This run has no valid returned repository Brain proposals.", `packages/desktop/src/main/github-agents-brain.ts:329-330`), so B08's "drafts return for review" half stays open. The codec half is proven: S2 packs drafts with core's real `BrainRunProposals.pack`, seals them beside the approved bundle under the Brain key and opens them as the desktop does, only as unapproved, unpinned proposals; S3 shows that a proposals entry that claims a desktop approval, or another run's origin, does not open.
3. **The Brain-key save.** It replaces BT07a's interim no-session rule (BT07a item 7).
   - `readBrain` keeps the opened bytes, the `DesktopBrainApproval` and the Brain key in `Deps.kept.brain` right after an open that carried `artifactKey`, before the size gate.
   - The save then runs under that key with no artifact-key request, and attaches the exact approved bytes and the approval as `brain/bundle.json` through `encryptConversation`'s existing `brain` attachment (`github.session.ts:135-170`). Nothing else changes in the codec.
   - A run with a non-empty `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD` and no kept Brain key saves nothing and logs nothing: a writer's dispatch that carries an upload id (BT05's case, renamed), a refused, failed, stopped or unopened read, and a 200 without `artifactKey` (H2). It never asks for the ordinary key (`artifactKey`'s client `brain-run` gate also holds).
   - The size bound counts the bundle with the redacted session, since both travel in the same encrypted zip (H8: at `sessionBytes - SESSION_HEADROOM` it is saved, one byte over it is "too large", and neither asks for a key). `SESSION_HEADROOM` still covers the codec's framing: one more zip entry and a manifest entry with an approval add well under 1 KiB.
   - The bundle bytes are not redacted: they are bound by their digest, were written by the desktop and passed its posting check at pack time. The session JSON is still redacted with the holder.
   - **The over-share choice (the critique's data-minimisation point).** An opened bundle whose block is over `BRAIN_CONTEXT_SHARE` is not attached to the task, but it is still kept, so that conversation is saved under the Brain key with the bundle and approval. Reasons: the server recorded Brain context at the read, so the ordinary key is refused (403) and only a Brain-key save can keep the conversation; and the manifest then always records the approval the run was granted, which only restricts later restores. The cost: an encrypted copy of approved bytes the model never saw is kept in that artifact (item 10). The alternative was to save the over-share conversation under the Brain key without the attachment, which the key's binding to digest, audience and visibility would still restrict. K6's over row pins the choice (the file opens under the Brain key and carries the bundle). The lead can switch it in `writeConversation` without a contract change.
   - **Open for the lead (review round, item 14).** Keep the attachment in the over-share case, or save that conversation without it. The switch is `writeConversation`'s `brain` spread into `encrypt`. If the lead drops it, the same change must add an explicit check that a Brain-key restore requires `conversation.brain`: without the attachment `allowConversationRestore` answers true, and the restore would rely on the key's binding alone. Decide with item 10, before activation or owner step 8.
4. **The kept cell replaces `Result.brain` (closes BT07a item 1).** `Deps.kept` is created in `githubAgentPhase` beside the halt switch, set by `readBrain` and read by the restore (before any session exists) and the save (in `record()`, whatever `work()` returned). `Result.brain` is gone. `failure()` carries no session id, so a run that fails after its read saves nothing and asks for nothing. H6 pins it: `sessions.create` dies after a valid 200, the run records outcome `failed` with a bundle, logs the attached line once, sends brain-download then settle `failed`, and exports and attests nothing.
5. **The Brain-key restore, and that production cannot reach it today.**
   - Built as the package says (it extends BT06): with the runner's session proof and a kept Brain key, on a dispatch's re-run attempt, the restore lists and downloads as before, opens with the kept Brain key (no artifact-key request), and continues only when `allowConversationRestore(conversation, kept.approval)` holds: the stored approval has the same digest, repository, audience and visibility. Every other case falls back with the one line (H3's rows: another digest, a stored approval for a public repository, a different Brain key under the same key id, and a conversation sealed under the ordinary key with the Brain key's id). The dispatch first-attempt rule and the attestation and removal rules (BT05) are unchanged; a Brain run's first attempt never lists (H2's first-attempt row).
   - **Unreachable in production.** A dispatch thread is `dispatch-<runId>`, so only a re-run attempt can find a saved conversation, and a re-run attempt has a new `rid` (the run id carries the attempt). Its ticket was consumed by attempt 1, so `peekRunTicket` answers nothing (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-start.ts:242-244`), and the renewal path needs a live run under the same `rid` (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-api.ts:272-283`), so mint answers 403 `ACTOR_NOT_ALLOWED`. The restore is therefore tested under the fixture only. It fails closed either way.
   - **The block is not sent twice (the critique's minor).** A restored history that already holds the block does not get it again with the new task message (`work()`, `message`); H3's main row pins one block in the re-run attempt's first task request. Without this the restored first message (within `RESTORE_CONTEXT_SHARE`, 0.5) and a new block (within 0.25) could take three quarters of the window.
   - The check reads the history itself (review round, item 14). `readConversation` answers `brain` only when the artifact carries `brain/bundle.json` (whose digest the predicate just matched to this run's approval, so the block would be identical) and a user text part of the decoded history holds the block's opening (`BRAIN_CONTEXT_OPEN`). A desktop Brain run's task text is a ticket dispatch's prompt, which `untrustedJson` escapes (every `<`), so only the runner's own block can hold that opening. Any user message counts, not only the first: after an attempt that was over the share, the next attempt sends the block with its own task message, and a third attempt finds it there.
   - The first draft based the flag on the manifest alone, so an attempt 1 over the share (whose bundle is still saved, item 3) made attempt 2 send no block although its history never had one. H3's new row pins the fix: a history with the bundle and no block restores, and the first task request holds the restored text and then exactly one block. Mutation: the manifest-only flag fails that row (no block).
6. **A comment run cannot open a Brain-key artifact.** A new BT06 case seals a conversation with a Brain attachment under the Brain key with the ordinary key's id, as if it sat under an issue's name: GCM refuses it under the ordinary key, the comment run falls back with its usual two key requests, and the importer is never called. S1 shows the same with the server's own derivations (`cloudRunArtifactKey` and `cloudBrainArtifactKey` from `api/_lib/cloud-agents-artifact.ts`, one master): the two keys share a key id, and neither the ordinary key nor a Brain key for another digest, visibility or thread opens the conversation. So the Brain history restriction is cryptographic as well as the predicate's, as V27 says.
7. **BT07a's interim rule is closed, and the plain-ticket ask stays.** The ordinary key is still never asked by a run with a non-empty `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD`: the save gate, the restore gate and the client's `brain-run` gate all hold. A ticket dispatch without an upload id still asks once for its save (BT07a item 7's decision, unchanged), and still never restores.
8. **Test placement.** The package text names `github.agent-phase.test.ts` and `github.session.test.ts`. The agent-phase cases are in `github.agent-codium.test.ts` (the nested describe "the Brain history (B08, BT07b)" inside BT07a's describe, plus K1, K6 and one BT06 case), as BT05, BT06 and BT07a's are (BT05 item 10, BT06 item 1, BT07a item 11): only that file's runtime has the Codium catalog, and agent-phase starts `bun -e`, which spec 0.3 leaves to CI. `github.session.test.ts` imports `api/_lib/cloud-agents-artifact.ts`, as `vector-login.test.ts` imports `api/_lib`, so S1 uses the server's real key derivations with a fixture master (`VECTOR_RUN_ARTIFACT_SECRET` of 32 characters, passed as `env`, never read from the process). That module's only runtime import is `api/_lib/http.ts`.
9. **Test helper changes and wording.**
   - `artifactZip` and `archiveShare` move from BT06's describe to file scope, unchanged, so the BT07b cases use them.
   - `brainRun` gains `calls` (tool calls before the default ones), `text`, `session`, `share` (the real archive exports and imports, and the counting export is dropped), `artifacts` (the fake GitHub's list), `restore` and `sessions`; the restore always goes through the fake GitHub's transport; it also returns the fake GitHub. Defaults are unchanged.
   - K1 now saves: the session file is the Brain-key save, opens under `BRAIN_KEY` with the exact bundle and approval and no proposals, and does not open under the ordinary key with the Brain key's id; export and attestation are called once; still no artifact-key request. K6's over row no longer uses `withoutBrain`: it saves under the Brain key with the bundle (item 3).
   - Stale wording fixed (the critique's minor): BT05's "a desktop Brain run saves nothing until BT07b" case is now "a writer's dispatch that carries an upload id saves nothing and asks for no key" (its behaviour is unchanged: that run never downloads, so it has no Brain key); BT06 R2's comment no longer calls every ticket dispatch a desktop run that never gets the ordinary key; `withoutBrain`'s comment names a read that opened no Brain key; the header, `SessionSave`, `saveConversation`, `writeConversation`, `brainContext`, `readBrain`, `restoreConversation` and `readConversation` doc comments describe the Brain-key rules.
   - S3 keeps one narrow local sealer (the critique's minor): it mirrors `github.session.ts`'s framing on purpose, because no public function produces an authenticated artifact with a forged manifest, and a control row proves it seals what the codec opens. It forges only the two claims the package names (a proposals entry with a desktop approval, and another run's origin).
   - The code review raised it again (item 14): derive the AAD and the header layout from one exported constant or test-only helper in `github.session.ts`, so the sealer cannot drift from the codec. Left as recorded. It needs a codec export, which the review made conditional on the lead accepting one, and this package does not change the codec (item 12). The control row still fails if the framing drifts in a way that stops the sealer's output from opening. If the lead accepts the export, `github.session.ts` exports the `VSA1` prefix and AAD builder and S3's `forged()` uses them.

## 2026-11-16 BT08a (V30a engine journey: conversation continuity and own-key)

1. **Scope.** V30 listed five engine scenarios (Codium success, a stop code, at-once, a stranger, own key). Plan section 3 V30 item 2 asked for the V27 cases and the V24 flip. Built, in `packages/engine/test/cli/github.agents-journey.test.ts` only: a sixth case, a Codium follow-up on the same numeric issue thread through the generated v3 route script, mint against the loopback fake vectordev.ai and the in-process agent phase, in which run 2 restores run 1's saved conversation; own key re-proved with every save and restore seam present (item 4); the P6 scan widened (item 5); and the generated file's workflow version checked against `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_VERSION`, the CLI's reinstall check and the mint body. No product code changed: the journey found no bug in its owning files. Three observations for the lead, not changed here:
   - The publisher escapes the whole bundle body as model text (V21 item 4), so the evidence's own `<details>`, `<summary>` and `<code>` markup used to render as literal tags on GitHub. The v3 bundle now formats check commands and output as indented code blocks, which survive the untrusted-text sanitizer. The separate publisher still applies the same sanitizer to the full body. The direct CLI's existing evidence formatting is unchanged. The follow-up journey checks the published command, exit status, output indentation and absence of escaped structural tags.
   - `messageOf` in `github.agent.ts` prints a non-`Error` value with `String(...)`, so own key's `reportError` line for an assistant turn's stored error (a `{name, data}` object, not an `Error`) reads "Agent error: [object Object]"; the next line (`errorLine`) still carries the message. Cosmetic, own key only, outside the journey's paths (found by mutation M10a, progress "BT08a").
   - `buildAgentsWorkflow` (`core/src/review/workflow.ts`) builds one `mintEnv` object (`:612`) and hands the same object to the agent job's mint step (`:662`) and the review job's mint step (`:728`); the review job's restamp (`:752`, `step.env.VECTOR_WORKFLOW_VERSION = GithubAgents.WORKFLOW_VERSION`) writes through it, so it also restamps the agent job's mint step. Harmless today, since `base` already carries `WORKFLOW_VERSION` and the YAML is written with `aliasDuplicateObjects: false` (`:891`, so the file holds two plain copies), but any later change to `base`, to one job's stamp or to the restamp would silently change the other job's mint env, and the journey's version check cannot see the agent mint step's own stamp apart from the review job's (mutation M06b: under a `base` of "2" the agent mint step still read "3"). Suggested fix, in its owning file with a regression test: give each mint step its own copy (`env: { ...mintEnv }`).
2. **The served artifact is re-sealed (a departure from the package text).** The package said the fake GitHub artifacts API serves "run 1's session.vsa". It serves run 1's decrypted conversation re-sealed under `ARTIFACT_KEY` for run 1's identity (`rid(4242)`), with only `info.id` replaced by `SessionID.descending()`, an id the test database never saw. Why: run 1 and run 2 share one test database, where the import would find run 1's session (`core/src/share/archive.ts:414-416`) and take the fork-fence path that a hosted runner's fresh database never takes (BT06 item 14). Minimality is asserted: the served zip holds exactly one entry, `session.vsa`, whose header names `rid(4242)`, and its decrypted session deep-equals run 1's with `info.id` put back; `SessionAudience.knows` is false for the new id before and after run 2. Run 2's import is a new session: its id differs from the re-sealed id and from run 1's, and run 1's own session (its info and messages) is unchanged after run 2. Run 1's exact bytes are still checked: the attested buffer equals the written `session.vsa`, which opens under the key for the thread and `rid(4242)` with only `session.json` in its manifest. The exact-bytes path from `vector-out` through sandbox finish, `upload-artifact` and `download-artifact` stays with V16's session-replaced job, `github.session.test.ts` and owner step 8.
3. **Simulated runner steps.** The FD3 attestation is injected (it records the bytes and answers true), and the Codium holder of the follow-up's two runs is loaded with this build's tool boundary and `VECTOR_GITHUB_SESSION_PROOF=fd3`, as `sandboxEnvironment` names them; the proof-checked copy into `vector-session/` and the `vector-session-<thread>` upload are simulated by zipping the file. The real proof needs a runner-owned file under another UID (V16). The single-run Codium scenarios keep a holder without the proof, so they still save and restore nothing and their cloud sequence stays V30's: mint, then settle (heartbeats aside).
4. **"The fake vectordev.ai", and how INV-K1 is proved.** In this journey it is two recorders: the loopback `Bun.serve` behind `Services.request` (recorded at the client, by URL, and at the server, by method, path, authorization and body; every check runs after the run, since a throw in the handler would reach the client only as an outage), and `codiumFakes`' `ModelPlans.Service.request`, the only path to `ModelPlans.CHAT_URL`. The own-key case asserts both are empty. Correction to the plan (the critique's major point, its option one): forcing `sessionProof` alone does not make the mode the gate, because own key looks no archive up, so its export and import would be missing, and it has no `VECTOR_RUN_ID`. Built: the own-key run also gets `GITHUB_REPOSITORY_ID`, `VECTOR_RUN_ID` (`rid(4242)`, agent step only; its publish step keeps the workflow's empty value), export and import seams that record and die, a listed `vector-session-17` artifact and a share whose every method records and dies; one comparison asserts no cloud request, no gateway request, no artifact list or download, no transport request, no seam or share use, no attestation, no `session.vsa` and no session line. Mutations M03 (save without the mode check) and M04 (restore without it) now reach the export seam and the fake GitHub's list and download. Own key holds no run token, so a key request itself would still answer `absent`: the mode is the gate before every reachable seam, not the only conceivable one. Rejected: the critique's extra mutation of the archive lookup (`codium &&` at `github.agent.ts:280`). The lookup is an `Effect.serviceOption` with no effect, and with both seams injected it is skipped altogether; the share is reached only through the save and restore paths, whose mode checks M03 and M04 kill. M09 was run anyway and survives as an equivalent mutant.
5. **P6, widened only.** V30's P6 bullet named four billing words; INV-P1 lists six (`payment`, `dispute`, `fraud`, `past_due`, `payment_failed`, `closing`) and "a percentage of usage". The scan keeps today's unanchored `dispute|fraud|past_due|payment_failed` (so "disputed" or "payment_failed_at" still match), adds `payment` and `closing` as whole words (the fixture memory's "payments" sentence is ordinary text the posting check drops), keeps the plan ids and labels, adds the fake gateway's words and `credits`, and on Codium keeps the amount check and adds the percentage pattern `github.review-codium.ts` withholds. It now covers the route script's `core.info`/`core.warning` lines and both file-command files (`GITHUB_OUTPUT`, `GITHUB_ENV`) beside the agent and publish lines, posts, pull bodies and notices (not the whole process's stdout: the phase's direct `console.log` lines, "Fetching prompt data for issue..." and "Sending message to Vector..." at `github.run-helpers.ts:804` and `:599`, are fixed strings and are not captured or scanned), and every case also asserts that no fixture credential (`VALUES`), the conversation key or the OIDC proof appears in that text. Controls: the at-once mint refusal carries every billing word, a plan, an amount and a percentage in its `message` (no real mint sends one, 2.8 "Errors"), and mutation M07 (mint noticing that message) fails the scan; the stopped scenario's gateway refusal words reach the public lines under M10 and M10b and fail it. The literal `toMatch` checks are regex sanity only. V30's P6 bullet is amended inline to say what is scanned.
6. **Test pointers.** INV-P1's "captured logs and posts pass the P6 scan" and INV-K1's "own-key journey" were never literal test titles. They now name the cases, amended inline.
7. **Affects:** BT08b (the server journey's V27/B08 cases); V30 Done (both journeys green in the lane); owner step 8 (the real FD3 attestation and the exact-bytes upload, item 2); the lead (item 1's three observations). Privacy copy is unchanged (V29/INV-P5 stay "private Actions artifacts").

## 2026-11-16 BT08b (V30b engine journey: desktop Brain run end to end)

1. **The v3 publish step had no dispatch prompt (a product bug the journey found; fixed in its owning file).**
   - What the contract said: 2.11.3's publish-step env list named `VECTOR_SCHEDULE_PROMPTS` but not `VECTOR_PROMPT`, and the builder (`packages/core/src/review/workflow.ts`) followed it. The publisher re-derives the route itself (`publishBundle` and `publishRouting` call `agentRoute`, `github.publish.ts:476` and `:220`), and `agentRoute` refuses a `workflow_dispatch` whose `VECTOR_PROMPT` is empty with `NO_PROMPT` (`github.agent.ts:1286-1287`). So every v3 dispatched task, after the agent had done its work, ended with "The event does not authorize publishing." and the CLI's "Vector could not safely publish this repository task.": the App's desktop dispatch (with or without Brain context), Release Guard and On Call tickets, and a writer's own dispatch, on Codium and own key. Nothing caught it because `github.publish.test.ts` had no dispatch case and the BT08a journey published with the agent step's env, which has the prompt.
   - What was built: the publish step's env carries `VECTOR_PROMPT: "${{ inputs.prompt }}"` beside `VECTOR_SCHEDULE_PROMPTS`, which is there for the same reason (the schedule route is re-derived too). Regression tests: the workflow pin and the three regenerated goldens, a pure `publishRouting` case for a writer's dispatch, and both new journey cases, which failed at publish before the fix (progress "BT08b", `red.log`). Spec 2.11.3 and the environment-name list are amended inline.
   - Why it is safe: env only (INV-S15); `inputs.prompt` is already allowlisted (`github.workflow.test.ts:891`, "every emitted Actions expression is explicitly allowlisted") and the publish job's event file already carries the same text. It grants no authority: the publisher still re-derives and checks the route, and a bot's ticket dispatch still gets no App authority (`!dispatchedByBot`, now exercised by the desktop case with `publishAuth: "app"` and mutation M12).
   - No `GithubAgents.WORKFLOW_VERSION` (and with it `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_VERSION`) bump: v3 is unreleased (no npm CLI at `MIN_AGENTS_CLI`, owner step 7). The desktop compares the installed file with the builder's output (`packages/desktop/src/main/github-agents.ts:345`), so an earlier v3 file reads "old" and the desktop offers the update pull request; the CLI's reinstall check (`github.environment.ts`) compares only the version and will not flag it, and such a file still mints and spends a Codium run before publish refuses it. **For the lead:** a bump is outside spec 2.11.4's rule, which bumps the version only when id-token is added to more Codium jobs; it would need its own deviation covering `GithubAgents.WORKFLOW_VERSION` (and with it `GITHUB_WORKFLOW_VERSION`), `GithubAgents.MIN_CODIUM_WORKFLOW_VERSION`, mint's own version gate (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-api.ts:243`, a literal `< 3`) and the pins that every job carries `"3"` (`github.workflow.test.ts:674`, the fixtures `github.agent-fixture.ts:606` and `github.cloud.test.ts:48`). No v3 file can run before owner step 7 (its install step pins a CLI at or above `MIN_AGENTS_CLI`, and none is on npm yet), so the recommendation is no bump unless v3 files are known to exist on repositories; decide it, with mint's minimum version, before the first v3 CLI release.
   - Alternative not taken (critique): the publisher could fall back to the event file's `inputs.prompt` when `VECTOR_PROMPT` is empty. It would also repair existing v3 files, but only with a new CLI, it makes the publisher read untrusted event text where every other route input comes from its own step's env, and it would leave the agent and publish steps reading the prompt differently. The workflow fix works with the CLI version the file pins and mirrors `VECTOR_SCHEDULE_PROMPTS`.
2. **The fake brain-download, not the real handler.** BT04 item 11 said BT08b would drive the real `createBrainUploadApi(...).download` handler. That handler needs real Redis (BT03), which the engine lane job does not have, so that part moves to BT09, the server journey. Built here: the loopback vectordev.ai answers brain-download with the fixture's `brainUploaded` output (core's own `BrainUpload.upload` through a fake account transport) and the Brain key the server's own `cloudBrainArtifactKey` derives for the run's claims and the approved digest, and artifact-key with the server's `cloudRunArtifactKey` for the same scope (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-artifact.ts`, with a fixture master secret). Until BT09, the strict `BrainAnswer` decoder is checked only against the fixture's shape, so a server field rename would still pass here.
3. **"Approval and proposals".** No `brain/proposals.json` exists (BT07b item 2: every GitHub-run memory write is quarantined, so nothing is proposed). The desktop case pins its absence (`proposals` undefined, the manifest exactly `session.json` and `brain/bundle.json`) and checks the approval in full.
4. **The journey's step envs now come from the generated file (the critique's major points, accepted).** The plan resolved the mint and publish steps but kept today's `stepEnv` under the agent step, whose hand-set `VECTOR_MODE`, `VECTOR_THREAD`, `MODEL`, `MENTIONS`, `VECTOR_WORKFLOW_VERSION` and `VECTOR_BRAIN_UPLOAD` would have hidden a missing name, the same masking that hid item 1. Built: the agent env is the runner's own names, the resolved agent job and step env and the mint's `GITHUB_ENV` file commands (and own key's forced `VECTOR_RUN_ID`), filtered by the launcher's real `sandboxEnvironment` (which also drops `VECTOR_SECRET_ENV_NAMES` names and `SECRET_NAME_PATTERN`); mutation M11 (no `VECTOR_THREAD` on the generated agent step) now fails the journey. The publish step's `needs.agent.outputs.*` come the same way from the generated agent job's `outputs` map, resolved over the mint's `GITHUB_OUTPUT` file commands (review fix; on own key the map is the literal `ok`), and the resolved `status` must equal what mint returned; mutations M13 (`rid` wired to the mint's `status`) and M14 (`status` read from another step) fail the journey. The resolver throws only for an expression outside its known set; a step output the mint never wrote reads empty, as on GitHub, so the at-once case (no `rid`) still runs. The file-command reader throws on a line with no `=` or with the multiline `<<` syntax, so a change to what mint writes fails loudly too. Effects on BT08a's pins: the comment mint body gains `pullRequest: 17` (the route's pr output is the issue number, which the generated mint step sends as `VECTOR_PR`; mint's body accepts it, `api/_lib/cloud-agents-api.ts:230-237`, and the server reads it only for a review comment's App check, `api/_lib/github-app.ts:247-262`, so this is the real request, not a bug), and `generated()` no longer returns `version`, since each step carries its own stamp. All six BT08a cases pass with the new envs, with 232 assertions where BT08a had 215 (the publish bundle-directory check, the refusal check and the agent job's status check run in every run's `pass()`).
5. **Not covered here:** the restore of a Brain run (unreachable in production while a ticket's re-run cannot mint, BT07b item 5; unit-tested in `github.agent-codium.test.ts`); the real FD3 attestation and the exact-bytes upload (owner step 8; injected here as in BT08a item 3); the real download handler (BT09, item 2); the desktop's preview and import of the result (BT10); re-screening the reply against the bundle (BT07a item 14). Covered beyond the plan (the critique's optional case): a writer's own dispatch carrying a live upload id runs its prompt with no brain-download, no artifact-key request, no Brain or session line and no save, even with the session proof, and publishes.
6. **Naming.** This package is BT08b (V30b, the engine journey's desktop Brain run); the server journey is BT09 (V30c) and the desktop preview and import BT10 (V30d). The earlier lines that called the server journey "BT08b" are superseded: progress "BT07a" Deferred, "BT07b" Deferred and "BT08a" Deferred, deviations BT04 item 11, BT07b's affects list and BT08a item 7. `v2-notes/at-vector-v2-plan.md` section 3 V30 item 2 is corrected.
7. **Affects:** BT09 (the real handler and the decoder's shape, item 2); BT10; owner step 8 (a live desktop dispatch can now publish its pull request, item 1); the lead (the version-bump question, item 1); V30 Done (both journeys green in the lane). Every switch stays off (`VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` unset, so the real brain-download still answers 503); privacy copy is unchanged.

## 2026-11-16 BT09 (V30c server journey: artifact keys and the Brain transport)

1. **The owner read refused every record on a RESP3 store (a product defect the journey found; fixed in its owning file).**
   - What the contract said: 2.16 lists `ownerCloudRun`'s inputs and refusals but not how it reads the record. `cloud-agents-binding.ts` READ already reads hashes "HGETALL inside a script, so the reply is a flat array on every protocol (a RESP3 client gets a map otherwise)", and `listCloudRuns` reads inside its READ script too. `ownerCloudRun` alone sent a bare `HGETALL` (`cloud-agents-runs.ts:248` at `6159fc32`), and `fields` accepts only a flat array.
   - What was built: `ownerCloudRun` sends `EVAL "return redis.call('HGETALL', KEYS[1])" 1 <run key>` (`RECORD`), the same record and refusals otherwise. Before the fix, every owner read against the lane's own real store (this journey's KV bridge over Bun's RESP3 `RedisClient`, as in every real-Redis web test, locally and on CI's Valkey) answered 503 `ARTIFACT_READ_UNAVAILABLE`: the desktop case failed at its first owner read (progress "BT09", `red-journey.log`). Regression tests: the journey's owner read, and a new `cloud-agents-runs.test.ts` case that fails before the fix with "Run records are temporarily unavailable." The owner-artifact unit test's fake store answered only a bare `HGETALL`; it now answers the scripted read and checks the exact script (its cases are unchanged). Spec 2.16 is amended inline.
   - Why: the hosted store is a REST adapter whose `HGETALL` reply is a flat array today, so production was probably unaffected, but the read depended on the store's reply encoding while its neighbours do not, and no real-store test could reach it. A script's table reply is an array on every protocol.
   - Alternative not taken: flattening `HGETALL` maps in the journey's KV bridge (emulating the REST adapter). It would keep the read's dependency and give this file a bridge unlike every other real-Redis file's, so a future bare read would pass here and fail elsewhere.
   - Affects: `owner-artifact-key` (the desktop's result read, behind the Brain switch, which stays off). Upstash's reply to the script is not verified against Upstash itself (owner step 8 exercises it).
2. **Scope.** The plan touched the journey only. Item 1 adds the fix in its owning file with the two test edits, as V30 "Changes" requires; no other product file changed. The existing case keeps all 473 assertions; only its route names (named after the `api/cloud-agents/*.ts` files) and its wider scan changed.
3. **"artifact-key for the token's own thread before Brain use".** Under BT03 D3 an owner-desktop run never holds the ordinary key, so the desktop run's own-thread request is 403 `ARTIFACT_KEY_NOT_ALLOWED` before and after its download (through the route and the engine's `artifactKey`, which answers `not-allowed`), and the "BT03 decision after Brain context" is that same 403. The ordinary own-thread key is shown on a comment run of the same repository (the second new case), equal to `cloudRunArtifactKey` for its claims.
4. **The switch lives in two places.** The bind, mint, heartbeat, settle, runs and artifact-key routes read `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` from `process.env` through `cloudAgentsEnabled()` (`model-plan-config.ts`, Codium's, never edited), so the journey sets the process value only between its `beforeAll` and `afterAll`, as before. The desktop Brain routes and the owner read take the switch only from each stage's env object, whose `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` is a getter over `policy.brain`. The switch-off step turns both off and shows the seven routes' fixed 503s with zero store commands (counted at the KV bridge), zero requests to either fake App and no dispatch, then the same download passes with both on. Beyond the plan: a query-string GET to brain-download with the switch off gets the same fixed 503, because the handler's inner switch check refuses everything else first, so only that order separates the `off()` gate from the handler (mutation M02a).
5. **Listed perks.** The task asked for listed perks "supplied by fixture". The plan copied the production predicate (allowance active, `github-agents` and `brain-cloud` listed) into the test's `eligible`; the critique pointed out that this duplicates logic and tests only the copy. Built: `eligible` is a plain fixture flag, as in `cloud-agents-start.test.ts`, standing for Codium's answer; turning it off refuses the download and records nothing. The real default (`cloudAgentEntitlement` with `listedPerks`, `cloud-agents-desktop.ts:52-60`) needs Codium's switches, readiness and App configuration, which these fakes do not have, and no test exercises that composition (`cloud-agents-desktop.test.ts` and `cloud-agents-start.test.ts` use the seam; `codium-public.test.ts` tests `listedPerks` alone). Recorded for the lead as a gap, not built here.
6. **Engine clients in the web tests.** The desktop case runs the engine's own `brainDownload` and `artifactKey` (`github.cloud.ts`) against the real handlers before reading the route's answer itself, and opens the real answer with `GithubBrain.openUpload`: the strict `BrainAnswer` decoder now reads the server's real answer, which closes BT08b item 2 (mutations M08, a decoder field the server never sends, and M08b, a server rename of `ciphertext`, both fail at the engine client's `ok`). This is `packages/web`'s first runtime import of engine modules: dynamic, inside the two cases that need them, through the existing `@vectordevai/engine` devDependency; Bun resolves the engine's `@/` alias from a `packages/web` process. The import chain reaches `@vectordevai/core/global`, which creates its data, config, state, cache, log, bin and repos directories at import time: local runs set fresh XDG roots on the command line (RULES-v2); in CI the web job's one Bun process for `test/cloud-agents-*.test.ts` creates them under the runner's home. The engine client's `request` accepts only `https://vectordev.ai/api/cloud-agents/*` and rewrites it to the stage's loopback server; `oidc` and `notice` throw, so no identity token is asked and nothing is printed.
7. **A same-account rebind isolates the generation.** The rebind steps use the same account, so only the generation changes (the completed run's Brain key is then not derivable; a live run's Brain and ordinary keys and heartbeat end). The first case keeps the takeover by another account.
8. **Another repository's token.** A second stage is a second fake App and repository sharing the first stage's account and clock; equal generations are asserted first, so a key difference comes from the repository alone. Per the critique: the other repository's upload is bound to the desktop run's own live ticket hash before the download, so its 400 can only be the repository check; this repository's own comment run downloading this repository's upload is the "not a desktop ticket run" refusal (only the trigger differs), the other repository's comment run downloading it is labelled a combined refusal, and a body naming a repository is a body-shape refusal (no field selects a repository). Correction to V27's tests (review round one): the contract said "the key endpoint refuses another repository's token". Built (unchanged since V27a): artifact-key derives the key from the live token's own claims, so another repository's token sent to this repository's server gets 200 with its own repository's key, never this repository's; the journey shows the two keys differ with equal generations. Why: the endpoint has no field that selects a repository, so a token can only ever obtain its own scope's key, which is the property "refuses" was meant to state; refusing a token for being "another repository's" would need a repository named by the request, which the body rule forbids. Affects: only the V27 test line's wording (spec amended); no route, key or test changes.
9. **The expired-token clock moves back.** The step sets the shared clock to the latest deadline, checks the 401s (route and engine clients), then restores it and repeats the same requests, which pass. `requireRunToken` refuses at the deadline before it reads the live run, so the refused requests change no run state; nothing is claimed about the IP-limit counters they touch.
10. **Revocation, then erasure.** The revocation marker is set first (A57's order): a revoked prepare is 401 `CLI_TOKEN_INVALID` and adds no upload, and the other repository's run loses its key. `eraseCloudAgentsAccount` then removes the three uploads (one uncommitted at the new generation), the account index, the run records and actors of all three runs, both bindings and every `vector:cloud-agents:*<account>*` key (mutation M04 removes the upload erasure).
11. **The P6 scan.** `expectPublic` covers every answer of both stages, the engine clients' included, and adds `reason`, `share`, `triggerSource`, `jti`, `wrapped`, `envelope`, billing words, amounts, currency and the plan labels to the earlier pattern. Keys, `ciphertext`, `nonce`, `aad` and values starting `vbc_`, `vrt_` or `vbu_` are redacted first: random base64url can spell a short word such as "usd".
12. **What is new here and what is not.** `cloud-agents-start.test.ts` already drives prepare, commit, desktop-run, the bot ticket mint, the download's exact keys and Brain key, `brainSha`/`brainPublic`, the ops, writer, other-ticket, other-repository and `eligible` refusals, the download after rebind, revocation, settlement and the deadline, and artifact-key before and after the download, over HTTP against real Redis. New in the journey: one continuous authority from the HTTP bind to the owner read, the engine's decoder and opener on the real answer, the owner read's Brain domain through the real handler, store and App checks, the switch across all seven routes with zero store commands, another repository's token, and erasure of uploads through `eraseCloudAgentsAccount`. Mutations M01 and M09 are also caught by the start test, M02, M02b, M03, M05, M06 and M07 by other files' cases (progress "BT09").
13. **Corrections to the plan's claims (critique, accepted).** Web `bun typecheck` (astro check) does cover `packages/web/test` (the lane's progress shows it failing on test files); it passes with 0 errors. The skip path claims only "1 skip". The fixture allowance is annotated `CloudAgentAllowance`, the type the entitlement seam returns; since review round one (item 16) it is a constant beside `policy`, because nothing changes it. `created_at` comes from the run record's own `startedAt` (the history entry), within 60 s of real time.
14. **Verification limits.** Local runs used Redis 7.0.15; the lane gate is Valkey 7.2.14 in the hosted API job, which is the acceptance (V30 Done), not these runs. Upstash is not exercised.
15. **Affects:** V30 Done (the lane's hosted API job must show the three cases as four passing tests, the desktop case once per visibility); BT10 (desktop preview and import); owner step 8 (a live owner read); the lead (item 5's gap). Every switch stays off (`VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` unset in production); privacy copy is unchanged.
16. **Review round one (accepted, one part recorded instead of done).**
    - The owner-read step that set the allowance inactive and `eligible` false, then expected 200, is removed, with its progress clause "it needs no allowance or listing": `createOwnerArtifactReadApi` takes no entitlement or listing input at all, so the step could not fail. That the owner read needs no current allowance holds by construction (its dependency list), not by this test.
    - The "not a desktop ticket run" refusal is now this repository's own comment run downloading this repository's upload, so only the trigger differs; the other repository's comment run is kept and labelled a combined refusal (item 8).
    - The desktop case runs once for a public and once for a private repository (`test.each`): the fixture App answers the repository's visibility, the desktop packs a shareable skill that travels only to the private repository, and each run checks `publicRepository` in the download, `brainPublic` (`1` or `0`), the Brain key derived for that visibility (and differing from the other visibility's key), the opened bundle's skills, the approval and the owner read's `publicRepository`. Before this the server journey showed only the public path.
    - The new `cloud-agents-runs.test.ts` case is renamed "a completed desktop run's owner provenance reads its record through a RESP3 store client"; its comment says the flat-array reply a REST store sends is covered by `cloud-agents-owner-artifact.test.ts`'s fake store.
    - The per-record `brainSha` check is one `Promise.all` over the records; the artifact-metadata loop stays a sequential `for...of`, because each change rewrites the shared fixture artifact before its read.
    - Docs: spec V27's artifact-key test line is reworded (item 8); the plan's count says 2 new cases (3 in the journey); the "CI for BT08b" paragraph in BT09's progress entry is retitled as BT08b's CI addendum, which is where the lane records the previous package's runs (BT01's runs are in BT02's entry).
    - Not done as worded: "replace 'Commit: none' with the BT09 commit hash". A commit cannot carry its own hash; the progress entry now says, as every earlier package's does, that the commit adding the entry records BT09 and the next package's entry records its hash. The full turbo typecheck was run on the reviewed tree and is recorded in the progress entry; it is repeated before the push.

## 2026-11-16 BT10 (V30d desktop preview and one-shot import of a real run artifact)

1. **Which artifact (the package text against what exists).**
   - What the package said: build the artifact "with the engine's real `encryptConversation` plus `BrainRunProposals.pack` output exactly as BT07b writes it".
   - What exists: BT07b writes no drafts. `writeConversation` (`github.agent.ts:797`, its `encrypt` call `:822-827`) seals only the session and `{path: "brain/bundle.json", bytes, approval}` under the run's Brain key, because every GitHub-run memory write is quarantined (deviations "2026-11-16 BT07b" item 2). The only artifact with packed drafts is BT07b's codec case S2 (`github.session.test.ts:179-262`), which adds `proposals` to the same save inputs.
   - What was built: `brainArtifact` (`github-agents-brain.test.ts`) seals what the save writes (core's `BrainRunBundle.pack` bundle, and the bytes and approval the engine's `GithubBrain.openUpload`, `github.brain.ts:17`, returns for the desktop's own upload of it, which the run keeps for `writeConversation` at `github.agent.ts:917`; item 2; under `cloudBrainArtifactKey`) and, as S2 does, the drafts core's `BrainRunProposals.pack` returns; `drafts: "none"` is the artifact production writes today, which the third case pins (it previews nothing). The zip around `session.vsa` is GitHub's artifact zip, level 0, as before.
   - The earlier returned case (`:159` at `aa925ff8`, hand-written draft JSON sealed with no bundle) is replaced, not kept beside the new ones (the critique's minor): its wrong-digest, one-shot, authority-during-engine-discovery, account-during-download and artifact-identity steps are in the first new case on the engine-sealed artifact, its procedures file is core-packed (`drafts: "lessons-and-skill"`), and its scenario (the owner read says public while the desktop's own view says private) is the second case's third row.
2. **Which BT07b and engine helpers are reused.** BT07b's `draft`, `drafts`, `packDrafts`, `withBrain` and `brainKey` are local to `github.session.test.ts`, and `artifactZip` to `github.agent-codium.test.ts`; importing a `.test.ts` file registers and runs its tests, and the engine's `encryptSession` and `writeConversation` are not exported. Two exported, non-test sources do exist, and the desktop case uses both (the review's finding; the first draft built the approval as a literal copied from `openUpload` and said no helper could be imported, which missed them): the production opener `GithubBrain.openUpload` (`github.brain.ts:17`) and the engine fixture's `brainUploaded` (`packages/engine/test/cli/github.agent-fixture.ts:326`), which seals the bundle as a real desktop upload with core's `BrainUpload.upload` and answers as brain-download does. `brainArtifact` calls `brainUploaded(bundle.bytes, {repositoryId, publicRepository: publicRun})`, opens the answer with `openUpload` (the run's own visibility view set to the same value), and passes the opened bytes and approval to `encryptConversation`, as `readBrain` keeps them (`github.agent.ts:917`). So the BT10 fix's input (the approval's `publicRepository`, which `openUpload` takes from brain-download's answer) is the engine's own mapping: a one-off mutation of `openUpload` to write `publicRepository: false` fails the second case's first row (13 pass, 1 fail; `/tmp/vector-v2/brain/bt10/fix1/mut/A1.log`), which the copied literal could not catch. The fixture answer's own `artifactKey` is not used; the conversation key stays the server's `cloudBrainArtifactKey`. Otherwise the case calls the same production functions with BT07b's fixture records (`d1` and `s1`, with the active, private and older controls `a1`, `p1` and `o1`): `encryptConversation` (`github.session.ts:135`), `BrainRunBundle.pack` (`core/src/brain/run-bundle.ts:42`), `BrainRunProposals.pack` (`core/src/brain/run-proposals.ts:45`), and the server's `cloudBrainArtifactKey` and `cloudRunArtifactKey` (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-artifact.ts:6,20`) with a fixture master passed as `env`. Only the records are repeated (data, not logic).
   - The desktop already imports `api/_lib` in a test (`packages/desktop/src/main/everywhere-offer.test.ts:4`); `cloud-agents-artifact.ts` has one runtime import, `api/_lib/http.ts`. The engine fixture's top-level imports (bun's `$`, Effect, core's `free-models`, `github-agents` and `model-plans`, and the engine's `github.secrets`) load at run time only in the sealing cases; its `github.cloud` import is type-only. It assigns no `process.env` and registers no tests.
   - Core's two Brain modules, `github.brain` and the fixture are imported dynamically inside `brainArtifact`, so the file's eight local-preview cases do not load them. Core's Brain graph creates core's global directories when imported (`probe.log`: under the scratch XDG roots only, clean exit; after the review fixes, with the engine fixture also loaded, the scratch XDG roots' listing to depth 3 was the same before and after the run, `fix1/xdg-before.txt` and `xdg-after.txt`); CI runners get the same directories under their own home, as every engine test does. A comment at those imports now says that a local run sets the XDG roots and `VECTOR_TEST_HOME` to scratch directories (the review's optional finding). A preload that sets them was not added: the desktop's only preload, `packages/desktop/test-electron.ts`, is shared by every desktop test and is outside spec 0.2's owned files, and a test file that assigns `process.env` is what the lane's fixtures avoid.
3. **Product defect: a public run in a now-private repository was screened as private (fixed in `github-agents-brain.ts`).**
   - What the contract said: the Combined desktop integration review: "A historically public run remains publicly screened when its repository becomes private"; `ReturnedPreview.publicRepository` (`packages/app/src/features/github-agents/agents-model.ts:81`): "public when either the historical run or the current repository is public".
   - What was built before: the desktop took the owner read's `publicRepository` as the run's historical visibility (`previewReturned`, `key.publicRepository || currentPublicRepository`; `importReturned` the same). The owner read answers the repository's current visibility: `createOwnerArtifactReadApi` returns `metadata.publicRepository` (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-owner-artifact.ts:95`), which `app.ownerArtifact` takes from `attachmentRepository` (`api/_lib/github-app.ts:608`, `:649`), `!repository.private` at read time (`:587`); "B08/V27 server authority checkpoint" item 2 says the desktop receives the current visibility while the key domain uses the stored initial one. The old returned case passed only because its fake owner read answered public for a repository its own context called private.
   - Found by: the second case's first row, red before the fix at `preview.publicRepository` (expected true, received false; 13 pass, 1 fail, `red.log`).
   - The fix: `previewReturned` reads the `brain/bundle.json` approval's `publicRepository` from the decrypted manifest (`publicRun`) and screens as public when the owner read, the desktop's own current view or `publicRun` says public; the cached selection keeps `publicRun`, and `importReturned` adds `item.publicRun === true` to the same OR. No exported type, signature, app or renderer changes; `currentPublicRepository` and the visibility fence in `take` are unchanged.
   - What the value is (the critique's major, accepted; the plan called it authenticated by the Brain key's domain, which overstated it): engine-written run metadata. `decryptConversation` checks only the approval's `repositoryId` and `sha256` against the header and file (`github.session.ts:251-258`); nothing ties the approval's `publicRepository` to the visibility in the Brain key's domain string (`cloud-agents-artifact.ts:31`), and the desktop cannot check it. Whoever holds the run's Brain key (the run itself) writes it; the engine writes brain-download's answer (`github.brain.ts:48-55`), so it is as trustworthy as the run's engine, with `ProcessIsolation.FLOOR` false and INV-S5 unmet. It is used only because it can make screening more conservative: a forged or absent `false` falls back to the current-visibility screening the desktop did before, and a `true` only removes procedure drafts.
4. **For the lead: the authoritative source and one remaining gap.**
   - The authoritative historical visibility is the run record's `brain.publicRepository` (`brainPublic`, `api/_lib/cloud-agents-runs.ts:275`), from which the owner read already derives the Brain key. Returning it as an additional field of `OwnerArtifactReadKey`, beside the current `publicRepository`, would be additive and server-authoritative, and the desktop would OR it in place of (or beside) the manifest value. That is a hosted API and contract change (spec V27 "Owner result read", 2.8) outside BT10's scope; it is offered here, not rejected.
   - An artifact with returned drafts and no approved bundle (the ordinary key, which no run writes today: BT07b item 2) has no recorded historical visibility, so its drafts are screened by the current views only. Requiring the approved bundle beside returned drafts, or the server field above, would close it. Decide before returned drafts are produced. The BT10 review agrees and asks for no change in BT10: before any production run writes `brain/proposals.json`, the lead either adds the run record's `brain.publicRepository` as a separate field of the owner-artifact-key answer and ORs it in `previewReturned` and `importReturned`, or refuses returned drafts with no approved bundle beside them.
5. **What the account cases can show.** Zeroing the cached plaintext (`clear`, and `take`'s refusal) cannot be observed through the ports, so the fourth case pins the observable effects: after a switch to another account (observed by a list call, by the import itself, or during a held owner read) or a sign-out, the preview is gone or refused, nothing reaches the import endpoint, and returning to the first account does not revive it. Observed while building it: the first call that sees a different account, or the first one again, is itself refused ("changed"), because `account()` clears and bumps the epoch that call captured; the cases assert that call. An A-to-B-to-A change with no controller call in between is not seen by this controller; production retires it through `github-agents.ts` `invalidateAuthority` and `clearBrain` on account events (`ipc.ts:279`), which this package does not test.
   - **24 Nov 2026, partial V30d carry on main `ab4b352d4ca1ec5951c96c3d867465f21620f8e4`.**
     `github-agents.test.ts` holds a returned Brain owner-key read through the real main controller and signals
     A→B→A through `invalidateAuthority`. The intermediate account receives only the event signal. The pending read
     is rejected before artifact work, and a fresh request reaches the fixture's normal artifact path. With both
     test signals withheld, artifact work runs and the case is red. Restored signals give 23 pass/185 assertions
     (baseline 22/176). The negative control changes only the test signals. This qualifies the main entry point's
     in-flight returned-read fence. Remaining integration includes IPC event delivery and import of an already
     decrypted returned preview after an account cycle.
6. **Token domains through the real account transport (the critique's major, option a).** The plan cited `github-agents.test.ts`'s bind case as covering the owner read's bearer; that case reaches no returned-draft method. A new case in `github-agents.test.ts` drives `previewReturned` through `createGithubAgents`' real `vector()` (`github-agents.ts:158-189`) with the file's Brain fixture: with the owner read answering 503 (as the hosted route does while the Brain switch is off), no GitHub artifact route is called; with a key answer, both owner reads carry `Bearer vector-only` and exactly `{rid, artifactId}`, the artifact metadata and the zip requests carry only `Bearer github-only`, no vectordev.ai request holds the GitHub token and no GitHub request the desktop token. The fixture's account transport serves only vectordev.ai, so the zip download stops there once recorded and the preview fails closed ("The encrypted GitHub run artifact is unavailable."). The fixture gained an owner-read route (503 until a case sets a key) and one GitHub artifact-metadata route; its other 21 cases are unchanged.
7. **The redirect to signed storage (the critique's major, accepted).** The fake GitHub answers the zip route with 302 to `https://fixture.blob.core.windows.net/...`, as GitHub does; the injected `fetch` port answers that host itself, passes only `127.0.0.1` and `localhost` requests to the fakes, and throws for any other host, as `github-agents.test.ts`'s fixture does (the review's finding: the first draft passed every other URL to the real `fetch`, so a dropped `apiUrl` or a redirect to an `actions.githubusercontent.com` host would have reached a real host unnoticed). A one-off mutation redirecting the zip route to `https://fixture.actions.githubusercontent.com/...` fails every returned case closed ("The encrypted GitHub run artifact is unavailable."; 8 pass, 6 fail, `fix1/mut/A2.log`), with no request leaving the port. `expectDomains` asserts that every storage request carries no `authorization` and none of the tokens, the Brain key or the engine credential; mutation M8 (the engine forwarding its API headers to storage) is caught there.

In production the authority hashes `private` (`github-agents.ts:237-246`), so the same flip fails one step earlier at `checkItem`; the case also runs that variant (visibility and authority both change: "authority changed").
9. **Smaller corrections (the critique's minors).** The fake engine returns one fixed answer and the cases assert the request body and that the answer is passed through, not a count the fake computes. The fake owner read checks the account on entry, before a held read, so the fourth case's held-read refusal comes from the controller's `checkItem`. The plan's "moved verbatim" helper is not kept: the zip half of the old `archiveFor` is inlined in `brainArtifact`, its only caller. Local runs use `bun --no-env-file test`, as `packages/desktop/scripts/test.ts` does in CI.

A one-off `tsgo -p` over both test files (a scratch tsconfig that extends the desktop one with `types: [node, bun]`, `/tmp/vector-v2/brain/bt10/tsc/tsconfig.json`; `tc-tests.log`) found one error in the new code, now fixed: the fake engine's record intersected `Seen`'s string `body` with the parsed body, so the two body assertions did not type; the parsed body is now `json`. What remains is the `typeof fetch` ports' missing `preconnect` (the file's existing fixture port at `:66`; the new `returnedDesk` port at `:693`, which replaces the old returned case's identical port; `github-agents.test.ts:140`, unchanged) and `core/src/flag/legacy.ts`'s `.md` import, outside the tests. After the review fixes, the same pass (`fix1/tc-tests.log`) also reaches `github.cloud.ts` through the type-only imports of `github.brain.ts` and the fixture, and reports `github.shared.ts`'s `@/util/repository`, the engine's own path alias, which the desktop tsconfig does not map; with `@/*` mapped to `packages/engine/src/*` in a second scratch tsconfig (`fix1/tsc/tsconfig.json`, `fix1/tc-tests-paths.log`) exactly the four errors above remain.
