## 2026-11-16 BT11 (A59 joint check: the run reader for Remote and Ship Desk)

1. **Where the reader is registered.**
   - What the contract said: the A40 and A49 hand-offs give A59 `registerCloudRunReader((account, input) => listCloudRuns(account, input))`, "from a module the `cloud-runs` route imports" (`codium-a40-remote-api-notes.md:25`, `codium-a49-ship-desk-notes.md:40`). Before BT11 nothing called it in production (`api/_lib/cloud-run-reader.ts:12`), so Remote's run list answered `{runs: [], available: false}` (`remote-ticket.ts:395`), Ship Desk's `readerOf` resolved to undefined (`ship-desk.ts:519`) and `lookupRun` answered `available: false` (`ship-desk.ts:436`). `origin/codium-finish` (`0b9f8337`) and `origin/main` (`2034e1da`) have no call and no A59 commit for it, so nothing was duplicated.
   - What was built: `api/_lib/cloud-agents-run-registration.ts`, a new owned module (spec 0.2, `api/_lib/cloud-agents-*.ts`). It exports nothing and registers at module scope, as `api/_lib/ship-desk-meter.ts` registers its meter. Vercel bundles each function's own import graph, and exactly three functions read runs (every caller of `remoteCloudRuns`, `lookupRun`, `openCard` and `shipDeskIntake`): `api/remote/cloud-runs.ts`, `api/ops/cards.ts` and `api/github/webhook.ts`, whose Ship Desk intake runs inside the ops handler. Each gets one side-effect import: the two routes, and `api/_lib/github-webhook-handlers/ops.ts` for the webhook. All three files are Codium's and are listed in the commit body. `api/github/webhook.ts` is Codium's A47 router and is not edited (spec 0.2).
   - The trade-off (the critique's minor, accepted as stated): `api/github/webhook.ts` already imports this lane's own `github-webhook-handlers/cloud-agents.ts`, which could carry the import without touching a Codium file. The ops handler was chosen because the brief names it and because it keeps the dependency next to the intake that reads it, so any function that bundles the ops handler gets the reader.
   - The guard: `registerCloudRunReader` throws on a second registration, so the module registers only `if (!cloudRunReader())`. A module is evaluated once per process, so the guard matters only when another reader was registered first (a test, or a later second registrant); that reader is kept. The Node probe's "a reader registered first" process pins this.
2. **The reader reads nothing while the GitHub agent service is off.**
   - What the contract said: the reader is `listCloudRuns(account, input)` itself.
   - What was built: `cloudAgentsEnabled() ? listCloudRuns(account, input) : []`, the same gate as the service's own run list (`cloud-agents-api.ts:644`, `safe()`: `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` and `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED`, both off by default). `cloudAgentsEnabled` is only imported from `model-plan-config.ts`, as `cloud-agents-api.ts:28` already does.
   - Why: the service keeps its state untouched while off (`model-plan-config.ts:255-257`: no binding or run state "is read for write or written"), and its own run list, `GET /api/cloud-agents/runs`, refuses every read then (`safe()`); and the root tests that import these routes run on the Codium fake store, whose address no real fetch can reach, so an ungated reader would try the network from Remote's and Ship Desk's tests.
   - Effects while the service is off (the default everywhere): Remote answers `{runs: [], available: true}` (before BT11, `available: false`); a `github-actions[bot]` pull request is ignored as `no-run` instead of `run-unverified` (no card either way); opening an App-bot card with a run id takes its refresh lock (at most one `SET` a minute per card) and gets no verdict.
   - Nothing reads `available` today: no code in `packages/app`, `packages/desktop` or `packages/web` fetches `/api/remote/cloud-runs`, and the phone's runs screen is a static panel (`packages/app/src/phone/routes/cloud-runs.tsx`) shown only when `remote` and `github-agents` are listed and `cloudAgents` is entitled (`packages/app/src/phone/status.ts:99-101`). The A40 contract said `available: false` means "not wired"; it is now wired, so the service being off reads as an empty list here, where the service's own `GET /api/cloud-agents/runs` answers 503 (`CLOUD_AGENTS_UNAVAILABLE`, or Codium's own code while `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` is off).
   - For the lead (the critique's minor, left open): Codium's `cloudAgents` runtime switch is not read by the reader. Records written while the service was on stay listable for their 90 days, as the service's own run list behaves once both env switches are on. Gating on the runtime switch too would add a store read to every call, and the reader receives no fetcher, so it would use global fetch. Either answer keeps this module's one line as the only place to change.
   - For the lead (review round one, left as recorded): with the service off the phone cannot tell "service off" from "no runs", where the A40 contract defined `available: false` as "not readable". If that distinction is wanted, two concrete options, each needing a Codium-side change and neither taken here: (a) register the reader only while the service is on, which ties registration to the env read at a function's cold start, so a later switch-on needs a redeploy or new instances, and Ship Desk's `github-actions[bot]` intake goes back to `run-unverified`; or (b) let the reader throw a typed "off" error (for example `ApiError(503, "CLOUD_AGENTS_UNAVAILABLE")`, the service's own code) that `remoteCloudRuns` (`remote-ticket.ts:397-401`, today every reader error is 503 `REMOTE_RUNS_UNAVAILABLE`) maps to `{runs: [], available: false}`; Ship Desk's `lookupRun` already maps any throw to `available: false` (`ship-desk.ts:437-443`), so its intake would answer `run-unverified` again with no Ship Desk change. Option (b) keeps one registration and changes only this module's line and one branch in `remote-ticket.ts`.
3. **No shape adapter, and how failures map.** `listCloudRuns` returns `CloudRun` (spec 2.16), which both readers already accept unchanged: Remote's `publicCloudRun` (`remote-ticket.ts:344-379`) takes every stored source, purpose and outcome and drops `repositoryId` and `trigger`; Ship Desk's `runFacts` (`ship-desk.ts:408-423`) keeps `rid`, `repositoryId`, `verdict`, `endedAt` and `runUrl`. Both limits (Remote 1 to 50, Ship Desk `SHIP_DESK_LIMITS.runs` = 200) are inside `listCloudRuns`' 1 to 200. Its failures pass through: 503 `PERSISTENT_STORE_UNAVAILABLE` and 400 `INVALID_REQUEST` become Remote's 503 `REMOTE_RUNS_UNAVAILABLE` (`remote-ticket.ts:397-401`) and Ship Desk's `available: false` (`ship-desk.ts:437-443`). The verdict is the one `recordRunEnd` derived (`changes` pass, `draft` and `failed` fail, anything else none), never the pull request's.
4. **A Codium test case replaced.** `test/remote-ticket-api.test.ts`'s "before the integration commit registers Codex's records, the list is empty and says so" asserted `available: false`, which A59 is meant to end. It is replaced by "the route reads the registered run records; while the GitHub agent service is off they are an empty list": the route's own import registered the reader, the list is `{runs: [], available: true}`, and the Remote switch still refuses first. With the service on, the same reader's records are `cloud-agents-runs.test.ts`'s new case, on Redis.
5. **`test/api-module-loading.test.ts` and main's `9be63d68`.** The per-module compile loop and the Node spawn move into two helpers below the tests (`writeNodeTree`, `nodeProbe`), shared by the existing test and the new one; the existing test keeps its entries and assertions. Lines 46-56 (the test's title, its `entries` array, `const temporary` and `try {`) are byte-identical, so main's `9be63d68`, which inserts six entries inside that array, merges cleanly: `git merge-file` of this file with `0f034957` as base and `origin/main` as theirs exits 0. This branch does not need `9be63d68` for these entries: the ledger TDZ it fixes needs a graph entered at the ledger, which none of the four functions is, and the new probe passes here in every import order.
6. **Test design, from the critique.**
   - The Valkey Ship Desk case keeps Ship Desk's own state on the Codium fake (the critique's major, accepted): the route's injected fetcher sends the store address to the fake, as `codiumFakes` does for its own address, while the registered reader and Codex's own writers (`createBindCode`, `redeemAndBind`, `reserveRun`, `recordRunStart`, `settleRun`, `recordRunEnd`) use the default store and global fetch, pointed at a loopback bridge to Valkey that records every key and deletes them afterwards. Cards are seeded on the fake with their expiry and index entry, as the fixtures do, and checked with `decodeCard`. No card open mints a GitHub token (`world.github.minted` is unchanged after the intake step below, whose opt-in read mints one).
   - The Node probe drops `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL` and `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` as well as `KV_REST_API_*` and both service switches from the child environment (the critique's minor, accepted); the contract test's reader case unsets `KV_REST_API_*`, and `codiumTestEnvironment` already clears the `UPSTASH_*` fallbacks.
   - The contract test's per-plan case compares Codex's seam with `cloudAgentView` of the stored snapshot and adds only facts that do not restate the formula (the reserved sources, and `not-in-plan` for scheduled sources where `scheduledPerDay` is 0, Plus and Pro), instead of rebuilding the limits (the critique's minor, accepted).
   - Every refusal row stores a snapshot fresh at the case's `now`, `none` included, so no row reaches Stripe reconciliation or the missing-snapshot wait (the critique's minor, accepted); `cloudAgentView(undefined)` stays in the existing pure case.
   - Each changed file was run alone, as vector-codium runs it, and no import-order failure appeared (the critique's minor).
   - Review round one, the Valkey case's intake step (accepted, with a different entry point): the finding proposed `shipDeskIntake(pull({..., body}), ...)`, but `OpsPullRequest` has no body. The webhook's ops handler is the only code that reads the pull request's body, and only for the run marker (`github-webhook-handlers/ops.ts:310`); it hands the intake `rid` and `title`. So the case drives `routeOpsEvent` with a `github-actions[bot]` payload for the drafted run whose title and body both say `verdict: pass`, through the registered intake with no reader named, and asserts the created card's `{fail, final}` and run link from the record and the bridge's `ZREVRANGE` and `EVAL`. Its random account is allowed through `VECTOR_OPS_ALLOW_ACCOUNTS` (the fixture allowlist names only the fixed accounts), its repository is bound and opted in on the fake, and the intake's opt-in read mints one token, so the "no token minted" assertion now starts after it.
   - Review round one, the local A59 case's card half (accepted, with a different assertion): the card is now opened twice, with `reader: null` and with the route's default. Only a reader makes `openCard` take the card's refresh lock (`ship-desk.ts:844-849`), so the first open sends no command on `cardRefreshKey` and the second exactly one `SET`; mutation M9 (the default reader ignored) is caught by this and was not by the earlier half. The finding's other option, asserting that no `ZREVRANGE` reached `world.fakes.store`, was not taken: the registered reader calls `listCloudRuns` with the default store and global fetch, never the route's fetcher, so a run-record read can never reach that fake and the assertion would pass with or without the gate. The gate's proof stays the Valkey case (the bridge sees no command with `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` unset) and the Node probe (every fetch refused, `[]` answered).
   - Review round one, the bridge: a request with another authorization is answered 401 and recorded, and the case asserts the list is empty at its end, instead of an `expect` inside the `Bun.serve` handler that surfaced as a 500. Cleanup also scans `vector:cloud-agents:*` for keys naming the case's random account or repository id and deletes them with the bridged ones. The scripts read only their declared `KEYS` (the ledger's `ticket` is `KEYS[5]` or `KEYS[7]`, `cloud-agents-ledger.ts`), so this is a guard; after the local run no such key was left.
   - Review round one, the Node probe's label is `path.relative(temporary, fileURLToPath(entry))` (a file URL's pathname is `/C:/...` on Windows); label only.
   - Review round one, hermeticity (recorded, no code change): the root files that import `api/ops/cards.ts`, `api/remote/cloud-runs.ts`, the ops handler or the webhook route (`github-webhook`, `release-guard`, `ops-screens`, `ops-payment-pipeline`, `ship-desk`, `remote-ticket-api`) assume `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` is unset in the shell. `codiumTestEnvironment` forces `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED=true` but does not clear it, so with it exported the registered reader would call global fetch at the Codium fake's store address (`kv.codium-fakes.invalid`, which never resolves): lost hermeticity, never a real host. The new A59 cases pin it themselves (the local Ship Desk case passes it as undefined to `OpsWorld`, the Remote case deletes it and puts it back, the Valkey case sets it and then deletes it, the world's restore putting it back, and the Node probe drops it). Clearing it in `codiumTestEnvironment` is a Codium fixture change, left for the lead.
   - No case drives a signed delivery through the default route in `api/github/webhook.ts` (the critique's minor, the second option): that route reads the store through global fetch, which cannot reach the fakes without replacing `globalThis.fetch` (AGENTS.md), and `no-run` and `run-unverified` give the same delivery answer with no card. The Node probe is the only proof that the webhook function registers the reader; the Ship Desk case "A59: the registered run reader is the default ..." proves the intake's outcome with it.
7. **Comments.** Three Codium source comments said "until A59" and are corrected, comment only, in this commit (listed in its body): the header of `api/_lib/cloud-run-reader.ts`, `remoteCloudRuns` in `api/_lib/remote-ticket.ts` and `lookupRun` in `api/_lib/ship-desk.ts`; also the route comment in `api/remote/cloud-runs.ts` and a test comment in `test/ship-desk.test.ts`. Not edited, for the lead or Codium: the "Not done" and table lines of `codium-a40-remote-api-notes.md` (`:12`, `:62`) and `codium-a49-ship-desk-notes.md` (`:89`), which record those packages as built, and `api/github/webhook.ts:15-17` (A47's router; its "added by the integration commit" sentence is already true).
8. **Found, not built: A59 is still incomplete (for the lead).** BT11's brief names only the run reader; a subagent cannot ask the lead mid-package, so the two other A59 registrations the Codium notes list stay outstanding and are recorded here rather than built:
   - `registerRepoBindingReader((id) => bindingFor(id))` (`codium-a47-github-webhook-ops-notes.md:42`, `ops-settings.ts:636-639`; `bindingFor` is `cloud-agents-binding.ts:280`) is unregistered in production, so `repositoryBinding` finds nothing and the ops handler treats every pull request and deployment as `unbound` (`github-webhook-handlers/ops.ts:177-179`). No ops repository can be set up, and no Ship Desk card, Release Guard watch or On Call fix exists in production. So BT11's reader changes only Remote's list (which no client fetches yet) and the verdict refresh of cards that cannot yet be made. The binding reader reads binding state, so it needs the same service-off decision as item 2, and a check that `bindingFor`'s record fits `OpsRepoBinding` (`account`, `repository`, `installationId`, `suspended`).
   - `registerOpsRunStarter((trigger) => startCloudRun(trigger))` (`codium-a48-release-guard-notes.md:37`, `ops-incidents.ts:817-830`) is unregistered, so On Call's "Fix it" answers 503 `OPS_FIX_UNAVAILABLE`. Codex's `startCloudRun` (`cloud-agents-start.ts:64`) answers `{ok: true, dispatchedAt}` or a refusal, not `{id, url?}`, so it needs an adapter and a contract decision on the run id it reports.
   - Proposed as one follow-up package: register both in `cloud-agents-run-registration.ts` (renamed for its wider job) or a sibling, imported from the ops handler, `api/ops/repos.ts` and the incident routes, each behind the item 2 decision. Not done in BT11.
9. **What is CI-only.** The Valkey Ship Desk case runs only in vector-codium (root `test/` runs there with Valkey; vector-at-mention runs only `api-module-loading` and `cli-token` from `test/`); `cloud-agents-runs.test.ts`'s new case runs in both workflows' Valkey steps. Locally both ran against a disposable loopback `redis-server` 7.0.15; CI's Valkey 7.2.14 is the evidence of record. `cloud-agents-mint-api.test.ts`'s "hours and at-once limits are neutral and OIDC proofs are single-use" (`:458`) and "Plus schedules are refused; Max's sixth schedule is refused and resets at UTC midnight" (`:480`; the brief's `:454` and `:476` moved) are not edited; that they execute is read from the lane's log, not claimed here. P6 is not re-tested: BT08a's `expectPublic` scan (`packages/engine/test/cli/github.agents-journey.test.ts:1469`) asserts it, and `publicCloudRun` and `runFacts` keep their allowlists.
10. **Exposure and switches unchanged.** No switch changes: `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` and `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` stay off, so the reader answers `[]` with no store read, and registration does no I/O (the Node probe refuses every fetch). Remote still needs `VECTOR_REMOTE_ENABLED`, the relay, its key and secret, the `remote` switch and the plan (`remote-ticket.ts:393-394`); Ship Desk still needs the plan's caps, the App configuration, the ops service, `opsPerkOpen("ship-desk")`, the run claim, the repository's `.vector/ops.json` opt-in (`ship-desk.ts:621-687`) and a binding. Listing @vector still waits for owner steps 8 and 9. Nothing needs regenerating: no HttpApi or CLI help change, no engine file (no egress-inventory entry), no install, no env example.
11. **Affects:** spec 2.16 and the section 5 A59 row (amended inline); the A59 paragraph of `codium-track-b-checkpoint.md`; `v2-notes/at-vector-v2-plan.md` section 3 (A59 joint check); the lead (item 2's runtime-switch question and item 8's two registrations). `codium-deviations.md` and the Codium notes files are not edited.

## 2026-11-16 BT12 (V31 Ship Desk capture job)

1. **Which pull request the capture names: the one publish made, from a new `pr` output.**
   - What the contract said: V31 runs `vector ship capture --pr <n>` "after publish" and lists only `packages/core/src/review/workflow.ts`; the A49 hand-off (`codium-a49-ship-desk-notes.md:32-39`) does not say where `<n>` comes from. The brief offered two choices: a `pr` output from `vector github publish`, or capturing only runs whose `needs.route.outputs.pr` is set.
   - What was built: `publishBundle` (`github.publish.ts`) appends `pr=<n>` to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` when the run has a valid run id (`VECTOR_RUN_ID` matching `RUN_ID_PATTERN`, the same test that writes the `<!-- vector:rid -->` marker into the body), the number is a positive safe integer and `GITHUB_OUTPUT` is an absolute path, best effort (`.catch(() => undefined)`: the pull request is published either way, and a missing output only skips the capture). It is the publisher's last action (review fix): the number is noted only after the run's comment was posted, and written in the `finally` only after `authority.dispose()` (the App token's revocation) succeeded, so a publish that throws after creating the pull request names none and no capture job follows a failed publish (item 5's re-run path). The integer check keeps an unexpected API value from adding a second output line. On Codium the publish step gets `id: publish` and the job `outputs: {pr: ${{ steps.publish.outputs.pr }}}`. Own key passes `VECTOR_RUN_ID: ""`, so it writes nothing (INV-K1), and its file has neither the id nor the output.
   - Why not the route's pull request: issue, dispatch and schedule runs have none; and a pull-request thread run that does not own the thread's head publishes a separate draft (`github.publish.ts:643-662`; the route's #17 publishes as #31 in `github.publish.test.ts`), so the card carrying this run's marker is the draft's. Naming #17 would get 403 `SHIP_DESK_TICKET_MISMATCH` (`api/_lib/ship-desk.ts:1047`) or wait for a card that never comes and exit 1.
   - Affects: V31's file list (also `github.publish.ts`, `github-agents.ts` and their tests; amended inline); 2.11.3's publish steps; the engine journey, whose `resolve()` refuses an unknown expression, now resolves the publish job's outputs and the capture step's env as Actions would (the journey's jobs share one file-command file, so the mint's `status` and `rid` sit beside `pr` there; the P6 scan sees `pr=31`, which is harmless).
2. **`!cancelled()`, not `always()`, in the capture job's condition.**
   - What the brief said: `if: always() && vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK == '1' && needs.agent.outputs.capture != '' && <pr known>`.
   - What was built: `!cancelled() && vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK == '1' && needs.agent.outputs.capture != '' && needs.publish.outputs.pr != ''`.
   - Why: the job checks out the pull request and runs the repository's build for up to 15 minutes; `always()` would keep it running after the run is cancelled, and GitHub's docs advise against `always()` for jobs that fetch sources. Both forms run after a failed publish or agent job; only cancellation differs. The own-key review job already uses `!cancelled()` (`workflow.ts:186-191`).
3. **The ticket step: after mint, before checkout, never fatal, two minutes at most.**
   - What was built (Codium agent job): `{id: "capture", if: "steps.mint.outputs.status == 'ok' && vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK == '1'", run: "vector ship ticket >/dev/null 2>&1", shell: <the agent step's OIDC-stripping shell>, "continue-on-error": true, "timeout-minutes": 2}`, right after the mint step, so no repository file is on disk while it reads the run token from `$RUNNER_TEMP/vector-secrets/run-token` (`runTokenFile` equals `GithubSecrets.runTokenPath`, pinned in the test). No `env:`: the runner gives `RUNNER_TEMP` and `GITHUB_OUTPUT`, and the site defaults to vectordev.ai.
   - Why `continue-on-error`: a refusal is already exit 0 (`ship.ts:519-521`), but an unexpected failure (a pinned CLI without `vector ship`, a bad `VECTOR_SITE_URL`) must never skip the checkout and the agent, which carry an implicit `success()`.
   - Why `timeout-minutes: 2` (the critique's minor, accepted): `requestTicket`'s own 30-second abort does not cover the CLI's start, and any hang would eat the 135-minute agent job before checkout. `WorkflowStep` (`workflow.ts:579-591`, unexported) gained the field.
4. **INV-P1 in the ticket step; `vector ship capture`'s refusal lines left for the lead (the plan's D4 not built).**
   - What the contract said: INV-P1 (spec :365-369), "The CLI never prints a server error body in Actions." BT12 puts A49's two commands into Codium workflows. `vector ship ticket` prints the server's refusal verbatim (`ship.ts:520`), and `mintCaptureTicket` can answer `requireUsableSnapshot`'s payment, dispute, closing or plan messages (`api/_lib/codium-snapshot.ts:149-178`) and "Your Vector Codium plan does not include Ship Desk.", all before its public-repository check (`ship-desk.ts:931-973`), so a public repository's log could show them. `vector ship capture` prints `ShipError` messages (`ship.ts:590`), such as `SHIP_DESK_HIDDEN`'s "... while its payment is reviewed." (`ship-desk.ts:822`).
   - The plan proposed a fixed-line `refusalLine` in `ship.ts` (Codium A49's file, not in spec 0.2's owned or shared lists). The brief said `ship.test.ts` stays unchanged, and the critique said not to start that edit before the lead accepts it; a subagent cannot ask mid-package. So the plan's own fallback was built: the ticket step discards the command's output. `UI.println` and `UI.error` write to stderr (`packages/engine/src/cli/ui.ts:24-26`), so the plan's `>/dev/null` alone would discard nothing; the step discards both streams. The ticket itself goes to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`, which is always set in Actions. Lost: the "capture ticket ready" line and a local error's text; the step's outcome still shows.
   - Not covered (for the lead, or Codium): the capture job's log still shows `vector ship capture`'s server refusals. That job exists only with a ticket, and tickets are minted for private repositories only, so the text reaches readers of a private repository's log, not the public. Discarding that job's stderr would also hide the build and Chrome lines needed to debug a capture. The fix proposed: the plan's `refusalLine` (in Actions, the bare fixed line) in both handlers, with a handler-level test, as an A49 follow-up; then spec 0.2's shared list and `codium-deviations.md` gain the entry. `ship.ts` and `ship.test.ts` are unchanged (11 pass), so neither gains one now. Until that follow-up ships, `vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` must not be set on any repository: `docs/vector/owner-actions/vector-mention.md` (the capture paragraph), INV-P1's test list and the A59 paragraph of `codium-track-b-checkpoint.md` now say so, so the owner's activation step names the gate (review fix). Codium's `hosted-ops.md` Ship Desk section is not edited (item 5): the lead carries the same line there.
5. **Codium's minting jobs restore no npm cache; the capture job's residual risk (the critique's major, accepted).**
   - What the contract said: 2.11.3 agent step 2, `actions/cache/restore@v4` for `~/.npm` on both modes; the Codium review job's list and the V14 test list (`:1743`, "cache restore only").
   - What was built: the Codium agent and review jobs drop the restore; the own-key agent keeps it, and own-key files are byte-identical. The step-order test now asserts that no step of any Codium job uses `actions/cache*` (title: "agent credentials, sandbox, and upload order are fixed, and Codium's jobs restore no npm cache").
   - Why: the capture job runs the pull request head's code (install, build and start from the base's `.vector/ship.json`, on files the agent wrote, which thread text can steer) as the runner user, outside the sandbox, with the hosted runner's passwordless sudo. That code can read the job's Actions runtime credentials from the runner's process and write a cache entry in the run's scope, the default branch's for `issue_comment`, dispatch and schedule runs. No Codium job ever saves `vector-cli-<cli>-${{ runner.os }}` (only the own-key review job, built from the legacy builder, saves it with `actions/cache@v4`, `workflow.ts:222-225`), so in Codium the restore could only hit a planted entry, before `npm install -g ... --prefer-offline` in a job with `id-token: write`. Removing it costs a cold install.
   - Residual, not fixable by Vector's own workflow: with the same credentials that code can (a) write default-branch cache entries that the repository's own CI workflows may restore, and (b) upload artifacts into the run. One example: when the agent saved no `vector-session-<thread>`, a forged one becomes the thread's newest; `latestConversation` (`github.session.ts:404`) takes the newest same-repository artifact without falling back, so it fails to open and the thread's real saved conversation is hidden for that follow-up (continuity only: V27's authenticated codec never reads it as genuine). (c) (review finding, plausible only) It can upload an artifact named for a later attempt's result bundle, `vector-run-<run_id>-<attempt + 1>`. On "Re-run all jobs" that attempt's publish job downloads its bundle by that name (`continue-on-error`) with `contents: write`; whether it would receive the planted one depends on how GitHub scopes artifact names across attempts and on whether that attempt's own agent upload replaces it or fails on the name, which is not verified. Publish still runs `validatePatch` and `routingAgrees` on any bundle, but a planted bundle skips the sandbox's bounded copy. The capture job now starts only after a fully successful publish (item 1), so a "Re-run failed jobs" of a failed publish, which would re-run publish without the agent job, never follows a capture. Proposed for the lead, not built: expose the agent's `actions/upload-artifact` `artifact-id` output as an agent job output and have publish download by that id, so publish reads only the bundle its own attempt's agent uploaded (a workflow change with its own goldens, journey resolution and a check of `download-artifact@v4`'s path layout for downloads by id). Not pinned by a new test here. Recorded in INV-S5's residual-risk note and in `docs/vector/owner-actions/vector-mention.md`; turning on `vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` is an owner decision at Ship Desk activation, and Codium's `docs/vector/owner-actions/hosted-ops.md` (Ship Desk section) is not edited here (BT11's precedent): for the lead to carry there.
6. **Constants and the timeout.**
   - What was built: `GithubAgents.JOB.capture = "Vector capture"` and `TIMEOUT_MIN.capture = 15`, since 2.11.3 says "Timeouts: `GithubAgents.TIMEOUT_MIN`". `api/_lib/github-app.ts:369` maps job names to profiles: `capture` would be found but no purpose expects it, so an identity exchange from that job is refused, and the job never asks for one (no `id-token`).
   - The effective window (the critique's minor, accepted as stated): inside the 15-minute job, setup-node, the CLI's install, the `fetch-depth: 0` checkout and up to 2 minutes of waiting for the card (`CAPTURE_LIMITS.cardWaitMs`) come first, so about 10 to 12 minutes remain for two installs, two builds and two starts, whose per-step limits (`CAPTURE_LIMITS`) are 15 minutes each; A49's 15-minute upload window, from first use, is separate. 15 is kept as the brief says; a larger value (setup plus the window) is the lead's call and would be its own recorded change.
7. **The capture job's checkout.** `actions/checkout@v4` of `refs/pull/${{ needs.publish.outputs.pr }}/head` with `fetch-depth: 0` (the capture checks `cat-file -e <baseSha>` and reads the base commit's `.vector/ship.json`) and `persist-credentials: false`, after Install Vector, so no repository file is present while npm runs. The number reaches the run line only through `VECTOR_CAPTURE_PR` (INV-S15: `run: vector ship capture --pr "$VECTOR_CAPTURE_PR"`); `VECTOR_CAPTURE_TICKET` comes from `needs.agent.outputs.capture`, through env only. The job's env is `{VECTOR_WORKFLOW_VERSION}`; no `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `VECTOR_CLI_TOKEN` or secret appears in it, and `actions/checkout` uses the job's read-only token without persisting it.
8. **No version bump; the desktop offers the update.** No new job asks for `id-token`, and the server never compares file content, so `WORKFLOW_VERSION` stays `"3"` (2.11.4). The desktop compares an installed file with the builder's output (`packages/desktop/src/main/github-agents.ts:345`), so earlier v3 Codium files now read as `old` and it offers the update PR, which is acceptable before release. Own-key files are unchanged, byte for byte (both own-key goldens identical).
9. **Not live, and failure modes left for owner-live runs (the critique's minor, accepted).**
   - No ticket or card can exist in production yet: `repositoryBinding` finds nothing until `registerRepoBindingReader` is registered (`ops-settings.ts:653-654`; deviations 2026-11-16 BT11 item 8), so `mintCaptureTicket` answers 409 `OPS_REPO_NOT_BOUND`, the ticket step writes nothing and the capture job is skipped. Ship Desk activation (R2 media storage, the listing or `VECTOR_OPS_ALLOW_ACCOUNTS`, `vars.VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` on a real repository) stays with the owner.
   - With all of that on, these show as red capture jobs, not skips, and are not changed here (A49's code): (a) a reused pull request (an owned one, or a schedule's): the card's `headSha` is the old head until the `synchronize` webhook updates it, and `captureShots` throws "Check out the pull request's head commit" (only a missing card is retried, `SHIP_DESK_CARD_PENDING`), exit 1; (b) re-running only the failed capture job reuses `needs.agent.outputs.capture`, already bound to the first attempt's proof, so `SHIP_DESK_TICKET_BOUND`, exit 1; (c) a draft stacked on a human pull request: the card's opt-in and `.vector/ship.json` are read from that human branch, not the default branch. Also owner-live or CI-only: Chrome's sandbox on `ubuntu-latest` (`--no-sandbox` is A49's switch, not used), how soon `refs/pull/<n>/head` follows a push, and an end-to-end capture with a real card (owner step 8).
10. **Test notes.** The critique's point that `captureEnvironmentProblem({...job.env, ...step.env}, () => false)` would prove nothing (the runner injects `ACTIONS_ID_TOKEN_*` from `permissions`, never from the file's env) is accepted: the assertion is not written. The evidence is the capture job's exact `toEqual` (`permissions: {contents: "read"}`, every step) and the scan of its JSON for `secrets.`, `id-token`, `VECTOR_CLI_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, `vector-secrets`, `github mint` and `actions/cache`. The snapshot was regenerated with `--update-snapshots` (never hand-edited); only the Codium golden changed. The four new expressions are in the allowlist; the `if:` conditions carry no `${{` and are pinned by the exact `toEqual`s. Review fixes in `github.publish.test.ts`'s V31 case: the relative-`GITHUB_OUTPUT` check named `path.relative(process.cwd(), <tmp file>)`, which on test.yml's Windows engine shards (cwd on `D:`, the temporary directory on `C:`) is absolute, so it would have failed there; it now names a bare `vector-publish-output-<uuid>` and asserts that neither `path.resolve(name)` nor `path.resolve(<checkout>, name)` exists, removing both in a `finally`. Three cases were added: a failed comment and a failed revocation write no `pr` (the fixture gained `state.fail`), and a non-integer number (the fixture's `state.created`) writes none. Mutations, each alone, all caught at the intended assertion (`bt12/fix/mut/`): MA the write before the comment (`:535`), MB after the comment but before the revocation (`:543`), MC no integer check (`:551`), MD a relative path written (`:566`).
11. **The capture ticket sits in a pre-sandbox file-command file (review finding; recorded, probe extended).**
    - What the contract said: INV-S5 (spec :260-265) lists what no process the model can start may read, "from ... a file, or a file-command file"; the capture ticket was not listed. The ticket step writes `capture=<ticket>` to its own `$GITHUB_OUTPUT` file (`$RUNNER_TEMP/_runner_file_commands/set_output_<id>`, an ordinary runner file, not the mint's 0700 `vector-secrets` directory) before the sandboxed agent step of the same job runs. Until its first use binds a proof (A49), the ticket is a bearer credential: whoever uses it first can bind it and upload images to this run's card, and the real capture job then fails `SHIP_DESK_TICKET_BOUND`.
    - What the evidence was: `sandbox prepare` checks only that the agent user cannot write the file-command files or their parents (`github.sandbox.ts:580-590`; spec 2.11.3 step 6). The V00 probe reads every file in the runner's command directory as the agent user (`sandbox-probe.ts`, `scan`), but no value was planted in one, and its report (including `enumerationDenied`) is not printed, so no CI run shows whether an earlier step's file-command file is still on disk or readable when the agent runs.
    - What was built: INV-S5's list names the capture ticket. The `sandbox-probe` job (`.github/workflows/vector-at-mention.yml`) gains "Plant a pre-sandbox step output" (`id: plant`) right before "Prepare the agent sandbox", which writes the ticket-shaped `capture=vcap_probe-capture-ticket-private-5e1c-000000000` to `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`. The probe (`packages/engine/test/fixture/sandbox-probe.ts`) adds that value to its markers, so its file-command, environ and cmdline scans fail on it. The assert step gets `PROBE_PLANTED: ${{ steps.plant.outputs.capture }}` and first checks that it equals the marker (the positive control: the runner did record the planted file). The isolation-failure error now names the leaked places (process and file names, never values). The plant sits closer to the agent step than the real ticket step does (checkout, the bubblewrap install and prepare come between those), so it is the stricter case.
    - Evidence: CI only, pending: the seven `sandbox (…)` jobs of vector-at-mention.yml at the commit that carries this (the known-red `sandbox (codium, success, bun)` stops before the assert, so it shows nothing either way). Locally: the probe still bundles (`RUNNER_TEMP=<scratch> bun packages/engine/script/stage-sandbox-probe.ts node`, the marker present once in `probe.mjs`), and `packages/cloud/src/workflow-compliance.test.ts` passes (53 / 0 / 967). No local claim about the hosted runner's file permissions or its file-command cleanup.
    - If the probe finds the value (the assert fails with `The sandbox probe found an isolation failure: ["file-command/set_output_…"]`): the ticket must not be written before the sandbox. Asking for it after `sandbox finish` needs the run token there, but `sandbox run` unlinks the token file (spec :1302), so that needs a design decision (for example a ticket-only copy the mint keeps outside the agent's reach, or the launcher requesting the ticket and handing it out of band): the lead's call, not built. `VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` stays unset meanwhile (item 4). If the job passes, the probe's positive control shows the runner recorded the planted output, and the scan shows the agent user found it in no file-command file.
    - Affects: INV-S5's list and its V16 test bullet, V16's CI-only probe description (spec :1801-1807), amended inline; the V16 lane files above.
    - Addendum (PR finish review, residual risk, not built): the ticket is also in the capture job's own log. It travels as the agent job's `capture` output into the capture step's `env:` (`VECTOR_CAPTURE_TICKET`, `packages/core/src/review/workflow.ts`). The ticket step discards all output, so nothing registers an `::add-mask::` for it, and GitHub prints a step's `env:` block in its log header, which comes before `vector ship capture` binds the ticket on first use. Anyone who can read the private repository's run logs therefore sees the ticket while it is still a bearer credential. Impact is small: tickets exist only for private repositories and `VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` stays unset. A later V31 change could keep it out of the step header, for example by having the capture step read it from a file it writes after registering a mask. Recorded in INV-S5's residual risk and the owner doc's capture paragraph; listed for the lead with the other V31 items.
12. **Affects:** spec 0.1 (the order line), 0.1's table, 1.2's "We provide" row, 2.1 (`JOB`, `TIMEOUT_MIN`), 2.11.3 (the jobs table, agent step 2, the ticket step, the outputs paragraph, publish step 4, the Codium review list, the capture job), INV-S5's list (the capture ticket, item 11), its residual risk and its V16 test bullet, INV-P1's test list, V14's test list, V16's CI-only probe description, V31 and the section 5 A49 row (all amended inline); `.github/workflows/vector-at-mention.yml` and `packages/engine/test/fixture/sandbox-probe.ts` (V16's lane files, item 11); `docs/vector/owner-actions/vector-mention.md` (owned) and `docs/vector/owner-actions/github-app.md` (shared, spec 0.2); `codium-track-b-checkpoint.md` (A59 paragraph); `v2-notes/at-vector-v2-plan.md` (section 3). Not edited: `codium-a49-ship-desk-notes.md` (its V31 hand-off stays accurate; the differences are items 1 to 3), `docs/vector/owner-actions/hosted-ops.md` (item 5), `codium-deviations.md` (A49's CLI is unchanged, item 4), `v2-master-prompt.md`.

## 2026-11-16 V16 account skeleton (CI check before BT13)

_Superseded in part by main's V16 item 11 (`b7c20d1a`, `6895687e`, from #38), the one record of the `/etc/skel` fix since the main merge: item 2 below says what this branch kept._

1. **The agent account no longer copies the runner image's `/etc/skel`.**
   - What the contract said: spec 2.11.3 step 6 has `sandbox prepare` create `SANDBOX_USER` "with its own home"; V16 item 10 above gave only the fresh `useradd` a 120-second bound, with no retry and no adoption of an existing account. As built, `useradd --create-home` filled that home from `/etc/skel`.
   - What happened: vector-at-mention.yml run 43932296517 at `79ad20c3`, job "sandbox (own-key, success, node)" (133527633106), failed "Prepare the agent sandbox" with "Sandbox operation useradd failed or timed out; the run is refused." The step ran 120.17 s (18:52:38.170 to 18:54:38.345), so everything before `useradd` took under 0.2 s and `useradd` alone reached its bound. BT11 changed no engine, core or workflow file: the job ran the same sandbox code that passed at `d49acedf`, and main's newest run (43930209600) passed the same job.
   - Why: GitHub's Ubuntu image installs whole toolchains into `/etc/skel` (actions/runner-images `images/ubuntu/scripts/build/install-rust.sh` sets `RUSTUP_HOME=/etc/skel/.rustup` and `CARGO_HOME=/etc/skel/.cargo` for the stable toolchain with rustfmt and clippy; `install-nvm.sh` uses `NVM_DIR=/etc/skel/.nvm` and adds `/etc/skel/.bash_profile`; `install-dotnetcore-sdk.sh` installs .NET tools into `/etc/skel/.dotnet/tools`). `--create-home` copies all of it into the new home, and on a freshly booted runner that copy is cold-disk I/O whose length varies from run to run. The step times show the spread on unchanged code: "Prepare the agent sandbox" took 25 to 104 s across the seven sandbox jobs of runs 43910604876 and 43932296517 (the CI fixture stages a small repository, item 8 above), and item 10's original 30-second miss was the same copy.
   - What was built: `prepare` (`packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.sandbox.ts`) creates a private runner-owned skeleton, `$RUNNER_TEMP/vector-sandbox/skeleton` (mode 0700, created exclusively; it is removed with the rest of the private state directory), holding only an empty `.config` (mode 0700), and passes it to `useradd` as `--skel`. The account's home then holds that `.config`, owned by the agent as the image's `.config/configstore` made it (runner-images issue 491), and nothing else from the image. The 120-second bound, the no-retry and no-adoption rules, `ENGINE_PRIVATE_DIRECTORIES` and the other ancestors (`.local`, `.cache`, `tmp`, still created root-owned by `install -d`) are unchanged.
   - What the agent loses: the image's `.bashrc`, `.profile`, `.bash_profile` (its nvm loader), and its home copies of Rust, nvm and the .NET tools. None of these was reachable: the agent's `PATH` is the runner's absolute entries plus the system path, and the runner's `$HOME/.cargo/bin` and `$HOME/.dotnet/tools` sit in runner HOME, which the agent cannot traverse (item 8 above). The profile files add nothing a tool uses: the image's `.bash_profile` holds only the nvm loader, which reads `$NVM_DIR`, set by the runner's environment to its own home, and a login shell that has it never reads Ubuntu's `.profile` (which only adds `~/bin` and `~/.local/bin` when they exist); non-interactive tool shells read none of them. Toolchains installed outside the runner's home (`/opt/hostedtoolcache`, `/usr/local`, `/usr/share`) stay reachable as before.
   - Test: the probe (`packages/engine/test/fixture/sandbox-probe.ts`), running as the agent, fails unless its home has none of the image's skeleton entries (`.rustup`, `.cargo`, `.nvm`, `.dotnet`, `.bash_profile`, `.bashrc`, `.profile`) and its `.config` is a directory the agent owns. CI only (the seven sandbox jobs; the known-red "sandbox (codium, success, bun)" job runs this check before its known failure). "Prepare the agent sandbox" should fall to a few seconds in every job; a job that still spends tens of seconds there would mean the copy was not the cost.
   - Affects: spec 2.11.3 step 6 (the account bullet, amended inline); V16 item 10 (the bound now covers account creation without the copy); every hosted @vector run's preparation time.
2. **Merged with main's fix: one implementation, plus the agent-owned `.config`.**
   - What happened: main's #38 commits `b7c20d1a` and `6895687e` fixed the same `useradd` timeout differently: `useradd --no-create-home`, then, after the UID and GID are recorded, `sudo mkdir -m 0750` of the home (failing if it exists) and `chown -h` to the agent, with a source-order test in `github.sandbox.test.ts` that refuses `--create-home` and `--skel`. The main merge conflicted in `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.sandbox.ts`.
   - What was kept: main's structure is the one copy; this branch's private skeleton under the state directory and its `--create-home --skel` arguments are gone. The one case main's version misses is item 1's agent-owned `~/.config`: main's V16 item 11 leaves every parent `install -d` creates root-owned 0755, and this branch's sandbox probe (`packages/engine/test/fixture/sandbox-probe.ts`) fails unless `~/.config` is a directory the agent owns. So the existing `install -d -m 0700 -o <uid> -g <gid>` call now lists the agent home's `~/.config` before `ENGINE_PRIVATE_DIRECTORIES`, creating it agent-owned 0700 before `.config/vector`; `.local`, `.local/share`, `.local/state`, `.cache` and `tmp` stay root-owned. The probe's message now reads "The agent home was not created empty with its own .config".
   - Test: `github.sandbox.test.ts` (main's order and no-skel test still passes; the probe check runs in the seven CI sandbox jobs only). Spec 2.11.3 step 6's account bullet is amended inline; V16 item 11 carries an _As merged_ pointer here.

## 2026-11-16 BT13 (Lane documentation sweep)

1. **V25's copy lives in the components, not in `packages/app/src/i18n/en.ts`.**
   - What the contract said: spec V25's file list ends with `packages/app/src/i18n/en.ts`, so its strings would be dictionary entries.
   - What was built (`3b2476494`, `4c8e7390b`, `e706636ad`; `fe074ed6` from #33 later added one line, item 2): every V25 string is inline English in its component, in `features/github-agents/setup-card.tsx` (`:147-457`), `run-dialog.tsx` (`:108-318`), `bindings-list.tsx` (`:72-345`), `components/settings-v2/github-agents.tsx` (`:14-26`) and `features/scheduled/automations.tsx` (`:736-762`). The shared pieces are in `agents-model.ts`: `ACTIONS_COPY` (`:3`), `agentHandle` (`:156-158`) and `runMinutes` (`:160-164`). `en.ts` (1,721 lines) holds no GitHub-agents string, and neither these components nor the panels they extend (`pull-requests.tsx`, `dialog-settings-v2.tsx`) calls the dictionary.
   - Why it is kept:
     - the neighbouring panels are inline English too, with no dictionary call, so moving only these strings would make them the exception;
     - the strings are fixed English from spec 1.3 B, C and G, 2.15 and INV-H1, and tests assert them literally: `agents-model.test.ts` (`ACTIONS_COPY`), `e2e/regression/github-agents.spec.ts` (32 `toHaveText`/`toContainText` assertions) and `vectorscope-changes-and-automatic-reviews.spec.ts` (`:264`, `:301`, `:302`);
     - moving them is a renderer change (locale keys, the e2e specs and the browser lane), which this docs-only package cannot carry. No string is moved.
   - Affects: spec V25's file list (amended inline); 2.17; owner decision A5 (folding the UI into the redesign is the natural point to move the strings); any later localization of the app.
2. **One shipped string uses an INV-H1 phrase: `setup-card.tsx:318` "Reviews every pull request."**
   - What the contract said: INV-H1, cloud-agent copy "never says ... 'every pull request'".
   - What was built: #33's `fe074ed6` (2026-11-14, on `origin/main` too) added the review-mode line for a current workflow: "Reviews every pull request." when `options.autoReview` is set, otherwise "Reviews when someone comments `<handle> review`.". `e2e/regression/vectorscope-changes-and-automatic-reviews.spec.ts` asserts it (the test at `:259`, assertions `:264` and `:302`). No copy test caught it, because the app's only copy assertion is `ACTIONS_COPY` (item 5).
   - Not changed here: the fix edits `setup-card.tsx` and the e2e spec and needs the browser lane, a renderer change.
   - Proposed, for the lead: "Reviews pull requests automatically.", which matches the setup checkbox "Review pull requests automatically" (`setup-card.tsx:384`), with the two assertions changed to match, in a renderer package that runs github-desktop-validation.yml and test.yml's e2e jobs.
   - Affects: INV-H1, unmet for this one string until then; `origin/main` carries the same line. The fix, with item 5's guard, is due before owner step 9 (listing `github-agents`) or any activation.
3. **The legacy auto-review text in `packages/desktop/src/main/github-pr.ts` also says "every pull request".**
   - What it says: `:756`, the refusal for an existing comment-only workflow ("choose to review every pull request to change it"), and `:767`, the setup pull request's body ("so Vectorscope reviews every pull request when it opens and on every push ... stop at `$<monthlyUsd>` a month (`REVIEW_MAX_COST_USD_PER_MONTH`)"). The dollar figure is the user's own review budget on the user's own provider key, not a Codium plan, amount, share or billing state, so INV-P1 and P6 do not apply to it.
   - Reachability: since `fe074ed6` the renderer calls no `pullRequests.autoReview` method; the preload bridge (`preload/index.ts:164-168`, `types.ts:405`) and the IPC handlers (`ipc.ts:559-563`) remain, so a renderer could still reach both strings.
   - Not changed here (desktop main code). For the lead: retire the bridge and its three handlers, or reword both strings as item 2 proposes.
   - Affects: INV-H1, if this pre-@vector Vectorscope text counts as cloud-agent copy; the desktop's IPC surface.
   - _As fixed_ (21 Nov 2026, `codium-api-fixes`): both strings reworded, the bridge left alone. `:756` now ends "Run `vector github install` and answer yes to "Review pull requests automatically?" to change it." (the CLI's own prompt, `github.install.ts:120`); `:767` opens "... so Vectorscope reviews pull requests automatically when they open and on every push." and keeps the model and the user's own `REVIEW_MAX_COST_USD_PER_MONTH` sentence; the `:645` comment no longer says it either, so `grep "every pull request" github-pr.ts` is empty. `github-pr.test.ts` asserts both texts never contain "every pull request" (two cases fail on the old source).
4. **The run dialog and the bind result differ from spec 1.3 B and C.**

Usually 5 to 30 minutes; at most N minutes", with N "read from the binding's limits or the workflow's timeout", a link to GitHub's Actions billing page and no dollar figure. Up to N minutes, from this workflow's timeout.", N being `GithubAgents.TIMEOUT_MIN.codiumAgent` or `ownKeyAgent` from the desktop status for a current workflow only (`packages/desktop/src/main/github-agents.ts:375-379`). Taking N from the workflow's timeout is one of the two sources 1.3 C allows, so that part is compliant.

(`setup-card.tsx:286`, `bindings-list.tsx:188`).

## 2026-11-16 brain-transport lane (phase-review findings)

The phase review of BT01 to BT13 on `brain-transport` at `748a3d39`. The cheap, plainly correct findings are fixed in the code and the records (progress file, "Phase-review fixes" entry); this entry records the contract corrections, the findings not taken, and why.

1. **The cloud-agents service gate is `cloudAgentsEnabled()`; `VECTOR_CLOUD_AGENTS_ENABLED` is read nowhere.**
   - What the contract said: O10, 1.3 A step 3, the `enabled()` default of the A59 entitlement block and section 4 steps 4 and 8 make `VECTOR_CLOUD_AGENTS_ENABLED` the service gate before A59 and Codium's `cloudAgents` runtime switch the gate after it; step 8 turns on only that runtime switch for acceptance.
   - What is built: no `.ts`, `.tsx`, `.yml` or `.json` file reads `VECTOR_CLOUD_AGENTS_ENABLED`, and A59 removed `createCloudAgentEntitlement` and its `enabled()`. #33's `e417c44d` made `cloudAgentsEnabled()` (`api/_lib/model-plan-config.ts`: `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED === "true" && modelPlansEnabled()`) the gate of `safe`, the wrapper of the bind-code, bind, bindings, unbind, runs, mint, heartbeat, settle and artifact-key routes (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-api.ts`), and of the `cloud-agents` webhook handler; BT11's run reader (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-run-registration.ts`) uses it too. While `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` is off those routes answer 503 `MODEL_PLANS_DISABLED`, while only the GitHub switch is off 503 `CLOUD_AGENTS_UNAVAILABLE`, before any store read. Behind the gate, `cloudAgentEntitlement` (Codium's `cloudAgentAllowance`) reads `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` and then the `cloudAgents` and `codium` runtime switches. The Brain routes check `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` first on their own (503 `BRAIN_UPLOAD_UNAVAILABLE`). `vector-mention.md`'s settings table, BT09 item 4 and BT11 item 2 already describe this; the spec contradicted them.
   - Why the spec is amended and the code is not: the gate is #33's and A59's code, it is stricter than the contract's (two env switches before two runtime switches), and changing a gate or a switch is not this lane's.
   - Done here: 1.3 A step 3, O10, the paragraph after the entitlement block, and section 4 steps 4 and 8 carry an inline _As built_ pointer to this item. Step 4 still says to leave `VECTOR_CLOUD_AGENTS_ENABLED` unset.
   - Open, for the lead and the owner: followed as first written, step 8 would turn on only the runtime switch and every route would answer 503. Step 8 now says the acceptance run also needs `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` and `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED`. Turning `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` on also opens the Brain routes (still behind their own listing and allowance gates), so whether it stays on after the acceptance run, and how that fits step 9 (where the lane notes place it, with the listed perks), is the owner's decision. No switch is changed.
   - Affects: spec 1.3 A, O10, the A59 entitlement block, section 4 steps 4 and 8; owner steps 8 and 9.
2. **The artifact-key thread binding is not fixed in this pass; what the fix must handle.**
   - The finding (twice in the review, at `api/_lib/cloud-agents-api.ts` mint and `api/_lib/github-app.ts` `verifyThread`): mint checks the body's `thread` only against `GithubAgents.THREAD_PATTERN`, and `verifyThread` returns before any thread check for `workflow_dispatch` and `schedule`. A writer who edits the default-branch workflow can therefore mint a run token whose `thr` names another thread and ask artifact-key for that thread's ordinary key (`cloudRunArtifactKey`, `api/_lib/cloud-agents-artifact.ts`, which is derived from `sub`, `repo`, `gen` and `thr` only), which opens its `vector-session-<thread>` artifacts. BT05 and BT06 made the key live: every Codium run with the session proof saves and restores under it. Already recorded as BT03 item 6, BT05 item 14 and BT06 item 20.
   - Why not here: the review's fix (bind a dispatch's thread to `dispatch-<identity.runId>` and a schedule's to its own schedule id, refusing a dispatch that asks for issue 5) is a V27 contract change with cases the finding does not cover, not a review fix:
     - a review dispatch is a `workflow_dispatch` whose thread is the pull request number (`buildAgentsRouteScript` in `packages/core/src/review/workflow.ts` writes `output("review", input.pr, …, input.pr, true)`), so refusing every non-`dispatch-<runId>` dispatch thread breaks dispatched reviews. Review runs never save or restore a conversation, so a review-purpose token needs no ordinary key at all;
     - a schedule's thread is `schedule-` and the first 8 hex digits of the SHA-256 of its cron string (the route script; `github.schedule.ts`). The OIDC identity carries no cron string, so mint cannot recompute it; and a writer who edits the workflow can add the same cron line, so every run of that cron is that thread by design. Binding schedule threads needs a decision: read the configured crons from the workflow file at the identity's `workflow_sha`, or accept that a schedule's conversation is shared by whoever controls its cron.
   - Proposed, for the lead (one V27 package before owner step 8 or any activation): at mint, a `workflow_dispatch` with purpose `task` must name `dispatch-` and the identity's run id; one with purpose `review` must name `String(pullRequest)`, which `verifyRun` already checks is a same-repository pull request when the App is installed; artifact-key refuses tokens whose `pur` is `review`; the schedule rule as decided above; mint-api cases refusing a task dispatch that asks for issue thread 5 and one that asks for another run's `dispatch-<id>`, and an artifact-key case refusing a review token. It is listed as an activation blocker in `docs/vector/owner-actions/vector-mention.md`, next to the bubblewrap floor.
   - Affects: V27, 2.8 (mint and artifact-key), INV-P5; owner step 8.
3. **A Brain run's saved approval keeps the server's visibility; the review's OR is deferred to returned drafts.**
   - The finding: `GithubBrain.openUpload` refuses skills when either the server's `download.publicRepository` or the run's own `expected.publicRepository` (its event payload) says public, but the approval it returns records only the server's value; BT10's desktop then screens returned drafts with `publicRun`, read from that approval.
   - Not changed here: the approval's `publicRepository` names the Brain key's domain (`cloudBrainArtifactKey` derives the key over the server's value; `openUpload`'s comment), and `allowConversationRestore` compares it between runs. Writing the OR there alone would make a saved approval disagree with the key its conversation is encrypted under. The desktop already ORs the owner key's visibility, the repository's current visibility and `publicRun` (`github-agents-brain.ts` `previewReturned`); only the run's own event view is missing from that OR, and no run produces drafts yet (BT07b item 2), so nothing is less conservative today.
   - For the lead, with BT07b item 2 and BT10 item 4: when returned drafts are built, carry the run's own view as a separate manifest field (or use the server's authoritative historical visibility from BT10 item 4) and have the desktop refuse private-only draft content from a run that saw its repository as public.
   - Affects: B08 returned proposals; BT10's `publicRun`.
4. **One repository-id rule for the engine's Brain context, restore and save.**
   - What was built before: `brainContext` accepted `^[1-9]\d{0,15}$` without a safe-integer check, `GithubBrain.bound` the same with `Number.isSafeInteger`, `readConversation` (restore) `^[1-9]\d{0,19}$`, and `conversationIdentity` (save) only `MINTED_RUN_ID`'s 20-digit parts, so a 17-to-20-digit id would restore and save but get no Brain context.
   - What is built now: `isRepositoryId` in `packages/engine/src/cli/cmd/github.cloud.ts`, beside `MINTED_RUN_ID` and `UPLOAD`: `^[1-9]\d{0,15}$` and `Number.isSafeInteger`, the rule of the server's `Decimal` (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-*.ts`), the desktop's (`github-agents-brain.ts`) and core's `brain/upload.ts`. `brainContext`, `readConversation`, `conversationIdentity` and `GithubBrain.bound` use it; `github.brain.ts` imports it and `UPLOAD` from `github.cloud` as values instead of re-declaring the upload pattern (the agent always loads `github.cloud` first; the desktop test's dynamic import of `github.brain` now loads it too, and passes).
   - Behaviour change: restore and save now refuse an id of 17 to 20 digits or above 2^53 − 1, and `brainContext` also refuses an unsafe 16-digit one. The server's `Decimal` refuses such an id at mint, so no run that can hold a run token changes.
   - Not changed: the decoders that keep `\d{0,19}` (`github.session.ts`'s `Header`, `Approval` and `Origin`, `github.cloud.ts`'s `Binding`, and `MINTED_RUN_ID`'s parts). They only decode, and every decoded id is compared with the run's own, which must now pass `isRepositoryId`.
   - Affects: V27 "The file" and "The next run" (the job's repository id), B08 source authority; no contract text changes.
5. **The restore estimate's cast is narrowed, not removed (the review's suggested fix does not compile).**
   - The finding: `local.messages as SessionV1.WithParts[]` in `readConversation` widens the decoded archive messages for `SessionCompaction.estimate` and would hide any future shape difference; make `estimate` accept a readonly array instead.
   - Tried: widening `estimate` and `MessageV2.toModelMessagesEffect` to `readonly WithParts[]` fails the engine typecheck. `SessionV1.WithParts` is a hand-written mutable type whose parts are `Types.DeepMutable` of their schemas (`packages/schema/src/v1/session.ts`), while the decoded archive is deep readonly (for example a user message's `summary.diffs`). Accepting the decoded type would need deep-readonly parameters through `toModelMessagesEffect`, `ComputerScreens.replay` and their callers, shared session files outside spec 0.2's list.
   - Built: `local.messages as Types.DeepMutable<typeof local.messages>`. The archive decodes SessionV1's own `WithParts` schema, so the cast drops only `readonly`; its result is checked against `estimate`'s `SessionV1.WithParts[]` parameter like any argument, so any other difference fails to compile. No shared file changed.
   - Affects: nothing in the contract.
6. **Ship Desk capture's residual risk: the prerequisites are named; the mitigations stay lead packages.**
   - The finding: BT12 item 5's residual risks (the pull request's own code can use the job's Actions runtime credentials to plant default-branch cache entries, a newer forged `vector-session-<thread>`, or a `vector-run-<id>-<attempt+1>` bundle) are mitigated only by `VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` staying unset, which only the documents enforce; the artifact-id download (item 5) and A49's `ship.ts` fixed line (item 4) are unbuilt.
   - Done here: `vector-mention.md`'s capture paragraph now says to leave `VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` unset until both the A49 follow-up and the artifact-id download land.
   - Not built here: the review's "consider" item, `latestConversation` trying the next-newest same-repository artifact when the newest does not open. It changes V27 "The next run" (the newest matching artifact is restored, and acceptance step 7 checks that) and `readConversation`'s single download per key request, so it is a contract change.
   - For the lead: (a) publish downloads the run bundle by the agent step's upload artifact-id output, not by name; (b) A49's `ship.ts` fixed line (Codium's file); (c) optionally the restore fallback above, bounded (one list, one key request, a fixed number of downloads).
   - Affects: V31, V27 "The next run"; the owner's Ship Desk activation.
7. **Records corrected; no contract effect.** test.yml was dispatched with default inputs on `brain-transport` for the first time (run 43970755777 at `748a3d39`), BT10's CI paragraph no longer claims a test.yml dispatch, and BT13's lane-summary row names its commit and its four dispatches (progress file). Also fixed, with no contract effect: `github.publish.ts` keeps the marked pull request in a `const` holder instead of a reassigned `let`, and the three fake servers in the two desktop GitHub-agents test files bind `127.0.0.1` only.
8. **Affects:** spec 1.3 A step 3, O10, the A59 entitlement paragraph and section 4 steps 4 and 8 (amended inline, item 1); `docs/vector/owner-actions/vector-mention.md` (the activation blockers and the `VECTOR_SHIP_DESK` sentence); `v2-notes/at-vector-v2-plan.md` section 3 (V27 built pieces named by symbol, not by line); code: `github.agent.ts`, `github.brain.ts`, `github.cloud.ts`, `github.publish.ts`, `github.cloud.test.ts` (an `isRepositoryId` case) and two desktop test files. No switch, flag, workflow, generated file or legal page is changed.

## 2026-11-16 brain-transport PR finish (review findings)

1. **Brain and owner-read routes did not answer before store work with only `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` off (blocking, fixed).**
   - What the contract said: the lead ruling for this PR: every `api/cloud-agents` route and the cloud-agents webhook answer before any store read or write while either `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` or `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` is off. V27 "B08 source authority" and the owner doc's `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` row named only the Brain switch for the Brain routes, and applied the either-switch rule only to the bind, binding, run, mint and run-report routes.
   - What the evidence was: the B08 gate (`off` in `api/_lib/cloud-agents-desktop.ts`) and the inner `enabled()` checked only `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED`. With the Brain switch on and model plans off, prepare, commit and desktop-run reached `requireCloudAgentsDesktop` (the `vector:cli-revoked:<account>` store read) before eligibility refused, and download reached its run-token and live-run reads. The owner read (`api/_lib/cloud-agents-owner-artifact.ts`) did the revocation read, the rate-limit write, the run and binding reads and the GitHub metadata call, and returned a key (its six cases passed with `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED` unset).
   - What was built: both gates and `enabled()` pass only when both switches are `"true"`, read from the injected env. The owner read checks both switches before its method, then its query check; it still needs no active model allowance. `cloud-agents-desktop.test.ts` and `cloud-agents-owner-artifact.test.ts` run their switch-off cases for Brain off with plans on and for Brain on with plans off, and assert zero store, fetch and limit calls (the owner read adds `PUT`). The start and journey fixtures set `MODEL_PLANS_ENABLED`. Mutation: reverting either gate to the Brain switch alone fails its new case.
   - Affects: spec V27 "B08 source authority" and 2.8 "Brain download" step 1, the owner doc's `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` row, amended inline. The two duplicate blocking findings (one per file from each reviewer) are this one item.
2. **The capture ticket in the capture step's log header (non-blocking, recorded).** Recorded as a residual risk, not built: BT12 item 11's addendum, INV-S5's residual risk and the owner doc's capture paragraph. For the lead with the other V31 items.
3. **BT03 item 4 was missing from BT13's open list (non-blocking, fixed).** Added to the list: the optional owner-read refusal of a completed desktop record without `brainSha`.
4. **Rejected:** none.

## 2026-11-21 A59 registrations (codium-api-fixes; closes BT11 item 8)

1. **What the contract said.** BT11 item 8: `registerRepoBindingReader((id) => bindingFor(id))` (`ops-settings.ts:637`) and `registerOpsRunStarter` (`ops-incidents.ts:824`) had no production caller, so `repositoryBinding()` always answered undefined and "Fix it" always answered 503 `OPS_FIX_UNAVAILABLE`.
2. **What is built.**
   - `api/_lib/ops-registration.ts`: side-effect only, exports nothing, no I/O at import (WP A59 header). `if (!repoBindingsAvailable()) registerRepoBindingReader(...)` with a reader that reads `bindingFor(id)` only while `cloudAgentsEnabled()`; the reader is written `satisfies OpsBindingReader`, so a drift between `RepoBinding` and `OpsRepoBinding` fails typecheck. `if (!opsRunStarter()) registerOpsRunStarter(createOpsRunStarter())`.
   - Imported for its side effect by every function whose handlers call `repositoryBinding`, `repoBindingsAvailable` or `opsRunStarter` (checked with `git grep` of the call sites, then their callers): `api/ops/repos.ts` (`listOpsRepos`, `saveOpsRepo`), `api/ops/incidents.ts` (`startIncidentFix`), `api/ops/uploads.ts` (`mintCaptureTicket`), `api/ops/worker.ts` (`handleOpsReport`'s regressed release) and `api/_lib/github-webhook-handlers/ops.ts` (bundled by `api/github/webhook.ts`, which is not edited). `api/ops/cards.ts`, `api/ops/releases.ts` and `api/remote/cloud-runs.ts` reach `ops-settings.ts` only through imports and call none of the three, so they do not import it.
   - The starter refusals, fixed text, never the run service's own reason: `not-bound` and `not-binder` 409 `OPS_REPO_NOT_BOUND`; `app-required` 503 `OPS_FIX_APP_REQUIRED`; `dispatch-failed`, `source-unavailable` and `purpose-unavailable` 503 `OPS_FIX_UNAVAILABLE`; every allowance reason 403 `OPS_FIX_NOT_ALLOWED`. While the service is off it throws 503 `OPS_FIX_UNAVAILABLE` before any read. `startIncidentFix` already releases its claim and passes an ApiError through (`ops-incidents.ts:955`).
3. **Deviation: no run id.** `OpsRunStarter` now answers `{ id?: string; url?: string }` and the registered starter answers `{}`. `startCloudRun` has no run id at dispatch (the run reports its id when it starts, spec 2.16), and the ticket's SHA-256 is a credential's digest, not a run id; its one caller (`ops-incidents.ts:958-963`) already treats both fields as optional, and `git grep` finds no reader of `incident.fix.run`. For the lead to overrule if a later package links the fix to its run record.
4. **Deviation: the adapter is a sibling module.** The brief puts the adapter inside the registration module, which exports nothing. It is `createOpsRunStarter(deps)` in `api/_lib/ops-run-starter.ts`, so the contract test can pass `cloud-agents-start.test.ts`'s GitHub App fixture and entitlement (the global `githubApp` would call api.github.com). Production registers it with no deps.
5. **Tests.**
   - `test/api-module-loading.test.ts`, a new test (no existing `entries` element changed): each of the six entries, imported first in a fresh Node process with fetch refused and both service switches unset, registers the reader and starter; `repositoryBinding(1)` answers undefined and the starter refuses 503 `OPS_FIX_UNAVAILABLE`, both without I/O; importing every other entry keeps the same starter; a reader and starter registered first are kept. Mutation: drop the import from `api/ops/worker.ts`, and `api/ops/worker.js` fails at `repoBindingsAvailable()`.
   - `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-start.test.ts` (Valkey via `VECTOR_TEST_REDIS_URL`; each case sets `VECTOR_GITHUB_BRAIN_ENABLED` itself and restores it): with the service on, the fixture's binding reads as `{account, repository, installationId: 600}`; importing the module again keeps the starter; with the switch unset the reader answers undefined and the starter refuses with no store command; a suspended binding reads as undefined. The starter maps not-bound, not-binder, app-required, seven allowance reasons and dispatch-failed to the fixed errors, and a started run dispatches exactly once and answers `{}`. Mutation: remove both `cloudAgentsEnabled()` gates and the not-binder branch, and both cases fail. Locally against a disposable loopback `redis-server` 7.0.15 (40 pass); CI's Valkey is the evidence of record.
   - Audit of the files that import an ops route: `release-guard.test.ts` and `ops-payment-pipeline.test.ts` register their own starters, so their `beforeEach` now clears the starter too (the routes register the production one at import, and when one process runs several files the first test after that import would otherwise throw "exactly one starter"). `ops-screens.test.ts`'s 503 assertion is unchanged; its comment now says the registered starter refuses while the service is off. `on-call`, `ship-desk` and `github-webhook` already reset through `OpsWorld`. All green one file at a time.
6. **CI run ids.** Not pushed by this lane; the vector-codium and vector-at-mention run ids are to be cited when the branch is pushed.
7. **Not changed.** No switch, no perk, no listing; with every switch off production behaves as before except that `listOpsRepos` answers `available: true` (a reader exists) and "Fix it" refuses after the binding check (409 `OPS_REPO_NOT_BOUND`, since no repository is bound while the service is off). Not ready to list: owner steps 8 and 9 still gate @vector.
8. **Review note (21 Nov 2026): what `available` means now.** `listOpsRepos`' `available` used to mean "a binding source exists". With this module imported it means only "a reader is registered": it is true on every route that imports the module even while the service is off and the reader answers undefined for every repository. Kept as is, because a test-registered reader must report true with the switches unset and no client reads the field (the `packages/app` ops screens do not). If a client starts reading it, answer `repoBindingsAvailable() && cloudAgentsEnabled()` instead, with a test.

## 2026-11-21 owner read: Brain records only, and the recorded visibility (codium-api-fixes; closes BT03 item 4 and BT10 items 3 and 4)

1. **BT03 item 4 (the owner read for a record without Brain context).** `api/_lib/cloud-agents-owner-artifact.ts` now refuses a run record without `brain` (no `brainSha`/`brainPublic`) with 503 `ARTIFACT_READ_UNAVAILABLE` right after the record read, before `bindingFor` and before `app.ownerArtifact`, so no GitHub metadata request is made. The key is always `cloudBrainArtifactKey`; the `cloudRunArtifactKey` import is gone from the file. Behaviour change: such a record used to get an ordinary key that, after BT03 D3, no artifact can be sealed under.
2. **BT10 item 4 (the authoritative historical field).** `OwnerArtifactReadKey` gains `historicalPublicRepository: boolean`, the record's `brainPublic` (the same value that chose the key's domain). `publicRepository` stays the current visibility. Spec V27 "Owner result read" amended inline.
3. **BT10 item 3's remaining gap (drafts without an approved bundle), and the desktop.** `packages/desktop/src/main/github-agents-brain.ts`: both key validations refuse a present `historicalPublicRepository` that is not a boolean; `previewReturned` ORs `historicalPublicRepository === true` into `publicRepository`, caches it on the returned item, and `importReturned` ORs the cached and the fresh value. When the owner read answers no field (an older server) and the manifest holds no approved `brain/bundle.json`, the preview is refused: "This run has no recorded visibility for its returned proposals." So drafts are screened by the strictest visibility known, never by the current one alone.
4. **Tests.** `packages/web/test/cloud-agents-owner-artifact.test.ts`: the fixture default is a Brain record; the first case expects the Brain-domain key and both visibilities; a record without Brain context answers 503 with `reads` 0 and one store read; a record public at its first download in a repository that is private now answers `publicRepository: false, historicalPublicRepository: true` under the public-domain key. `cloud-agents-journey.test.ts` (Valkey) expects the field. `packages/desktop/src/main/github-agents-brain.test.ts`: the server's `true` with a private run and a private repository screens as public in preview and in import (with the field gone at the Import click, the cached value holds), and refuses a procedure draft; a run's own public approval stays public under a server `false`; no bundle and no field is refused, the field alone is enough, and a string field is refused at both reads. Mutations: dropping the server guard fails the no-Brain case (`reads` 1); dropping the cached value in `importReturned` fails the first desktop case; dropping the refusal fails the no-bundle case. Locally: owner-artifact 8 pass, journey 4 pass against a loopback `redis-server`, desktop brain 17 pass, desktop github-agents 22 pass.
5. **Not changed.** No switch, endpoint, host or flag. The field can only make screening stricter. BT13 item 3 (the legacy `github-pr.ts` text) is a separate item.
